## Executive Summary

The Orchestrator is K1's Layer 2 -- a **pure deterministic actor** that mechanically executes DAG plans via Fabric. It is the blind muscle: it receives instructions (from Concierge or Planner), walks the plan step by step, calls Fabric per step, aggregates results, and returns them to Concierge. It also manages saved workflows (frozen plans as cron jobs) and MCP connector registration.

### What Orchestrator IS

- A blind DAG executor (LangGraph-style runtime)
- A workflow engine (saved plans as scheduled/event-driven tasks)
- A connector host (MCP server tool registration into Fabric)

### What Orchestrator IS NOT (Non-Negotiable)

- NOT an LLM consumer (zero LLM calls, zero token budget)
- NOT a tool caller (zero tools -- calls `Fabric.execute()` directly)
- NOT a state writer (NEVER writes SessionState, ORCH-01)
- NOT a planner (walks a plan, does not create one)

### Tier Involvement

| Tier | Orchestrator? | Path |
|------|---------------|------|
| LOW | No | Concierge calls Fabric directly |
| MEDIUM | Yes | TaskEnvelope -> 1-2 Fabric calls, no planning |
| HIGH | Yes | TaskEnvelope -> PlanRequest -> Planner -> CommittedPlan -> DAG execution |
| CRISIS | No | Safety Agent handles immediately |

### Six Internal Services + ErrorRouter

| # | Service | Purpose | Est. Tests |
|---|---------|---------|------------|
| 1 | OrchestratorService | Central dispatch, message routing, result aggregation, save_workflow | ~65 |
| 2 | DAGExecutor | Adaptive topological DAG walk, parallel waves, agent-aware extensions | ~130 |
| 3 | StepRunner | Single-step execution with retry policy, schema retry | ~45 |
| 4 | ConstraintResolver | Pre-execution validation, capability checks, HIL fallback | ~40 |
| 5 | WorkflowEngine | Registry, compiler, scheduler, gap detection, SQLite persistence | ~55 |
| 6 | ConnectorLifecycleManager | MCP discovery, registration bridge to Fabric, lifecycle monitoring, K0 proxy (provider creation, health, CB, sandbox all owned by Fabric) | ~20 |
| 7 | ErrorRouter | Centralized error classification and routing across all adapters | ~25 |

### Eight Ports (Hexagonal Architecture)

| Port | Direction | Purpose |
|------|-----------|--------|
| IMailboxPort | Inbound | MPSC queue, WFQ with 3 priority classes (REALTIME > INTERACTIVE > BACKGROUND) |
| IFabricGatewayPort | Both | execute(), execute_batch(), spawn(), query_registry() |
| IPlannerPort | Outbound | request_plan(), await_plan(), micro_replan() |
| IStateReadPort | Inbound (read-only) | SessionState snapshot, NEVER writes (ORCH-01) |
| IDeltaEmitPort | Outbound | Fire-and-forget deltas + progress |
| IBridgeWritePort | Outbound | Fire-and-forget audit writes to K0 |
| IEventSubscriptionPort | Inbound | Subscribe to plan.ready, capability.*, workflow.trigger, HIL responses |
| IWorkflowStoragePort | Both | Workflow persistence: save/load WorkflowSpec, TriggerSpec, RunManifest, ProactiveGap |

### 16 Hard Invariants (ORCH-01 to ORCH-16)

| Category | IDs | Rules |
|----------|-----|-------|
| Identity | ORCH-01..03 | Never writes SessionState, No LLM, Zero tools |
| Execution | ORCH-04..07 | Every step through Fabric, topological order, 2 retries, dependent cancel |
| Workflow | ORCH-08..09 | Depth max 3, all events with trace_id |
| Tier | ORCH-10..11 | MEDIUM max 2 Fabric calls, HIGH needs CommittedPlan |
| Cross-Workflow | ORCH-12 | Cycle validation |
| Adaptive DAG | ORCH-13 | Max 1 micro-replan |
| Agent Guard | ORCH-15..16 | Output schema validation, conditional edges at wave boundaries |

### Independence Confirmation

Orchestrator can be developed independently because:

1. **Hexagonal Architecture**: All interactions through 8 ports
2. **ORCH-01/02/03**: Never writes SessionState, never calls LLM, zero tools
3. **Fabric Implemented**: Use `FabricFactory.create_for_testing()` for step execution
4. **SessionState Implemented**: Use `SessionStateFactory.create_for_testing()` for read-only context
5. **Test Adapter Pattern**: Established by SessionState and Fabric

### Dependency Map (External)

| Dependency | Component | How Orchestrator Uses | Test Strategy |
|------------|-----------|----------------------|---------------|
| Fabric (execute) | K1 L2.5 | Execute steps via IFabricGatewayPort | `MockFabricAdapter` (scripted results) |
| Fabric (registry) | K1 L2.5 | Query capability existence | `MockFabricAdapter` (scripted lookups) |
| SessionState (READ-ONLY) | K1 L5 | Context snapshots, safety_band re-read | `MockStateReadAdapter` (scripted snapshots) |
| Planner | K1 L3 | Request plans, await CommittedPlan | `MockPlannerAdapter` (scripted plans) |
| K1 Event Bus | K1 Coordination | Event subscription + emission | `TestEventAdapter` (fire on demand) |
| Delta Bus | K1 Coordination | Progress + orchestration deltas | `TestDeltaAdapter` (capture mode) |
| Bridge (K0) | K1 L6 | Audit writes, plan WAL | `MockBridgeAdapter` (capture mode) |

---

### Cross-System Alignment Notes

> **Constraint Classification** (per cross-system alignment review, see `docs/plans/orchestrator-cross-system-alignment.md`):
>
> - **Fabric (HARD constraint)**: Already implemented (`k1/fabric/`). All Orchestrator ports, adapters, and types touching Fabric MUST align with actual Fabric APIs. Key alignments applied: IStateReadPort matches Fabric's `ISessionStateReader`, IEventSubscriptionPort matches Fabric's `IEventPort`, FabricGatewayAdapter wraps `Fabric` facade class, DeltaEmitAdapter uses `IDeltaBusPort` + `IEventPort`. Types imported: `CapabilityRequest`, `CapabilityResult`, `SessionSnapshot`, `SubscriptionHandle`, `DeltaPayload` from their Fabric authority modules.
> - **SessionState (HARD constraint)**: Already implemented (`k1/sessionstate/`). Orchestrator reads SessionState through Fabric's `ISessionStateReader` Protocol (the established bridge). All read methods require `session_id` parameter. ORCH-01 invariant: NO writes.
> - **Planner (SOFT constraint)**: Not yet implemented. Will be built AFTER Orchestrator. API signatures (`IPlannerPort`, `PlanRequest`, `CommittedPlan`, event topics) are defined in this plan as requirements that Planner will implement. We can improvise exact signatures when building Planner based on Orchestrator's actual needs.
> - **Concierge (SOFT constraint)**: Not yet implemented. Will be built AFTER Orchestrator + Planner. `TaskEnvelope` format, `IMailboxPort` consumption, and result delivery patterns are defined as requirements. Exact API signatures will be finalized when building Concierge.

## Milestone Overview

| Milestone | Name | Epics | Focus |
|-----------|------|-------|-------|
| M1 | Foundation | 5 | ADRs (12), core types (27 issues, ~42+ types), message envelopes, event catalog, port interfaces (8), JSON Schemas (event payloads + protocol), cross-component contract, Schema Whiteboard alignment |
| M2 | Core DAG Engine | 3 | OrchestratorService (event-driven dispatch_high), DAGExecutor (safety band, WAL), StepRunner, ParamResolver, ErrorRouter |
| M3 | Constraint Resolution + Adaptive Extensions | 2 | ConstraintResolver (async HIL), Output Schema Guard, Conditional Edges, Micro-Replan, Execution Monitor (per-step interrupt), Concurrency Guard |
| M4 | Workflow System | 2 | WorkflowRegistry + SQLite persistence, Compiler (gap classification), Scheduler, Supervisor (no-session results), Cross-Workflow, Gap Detection |
| M5 | Connector Lifecycle (Thin) | 1 | MCP Discovery (config-driven), Registration Bridge to Fabric (via register_from_dict + event), Lifecycle Monitoring (subscribe to Fabric health events), K0 Proxy. Provider creation, execution, health, CB, sandbox, output validation ALL owned by Fabric. |
| M6 | Adapters & Factory | 3 | 16 adapters (8 prod + 8 test), OrchestratorFactory (15-step construction), lifecycle (async MCP init), concurrency, Admin API (IAdminPort + HTTP adapter + factory wiring) |
| M7 | Testing | 8 | Unit (7 services), guards (8), integration (5 + 7 advanced), contract (4), cross-subsystem (3), lifecycle (3), performance (2) |
| M8 | SLO/SLI + Observability | 4 | SLI definition, metrics, tracing, alerts |
| M9 | Production Readiness | 4 | Load testing, chaos testing (6 scenarios), documentation (4 docs), final validation |

**Estimated effort**: 10-14 weeks
**Estimated total tests**: ~470+ (was ~515; -45 tests removed: TokenBudgetTracker, QualityGate, CostBudgetGuard guards + CostAccumulator + quality retry stripped for V1 -- no upstream producer exists for cost_usd, tokens_consumed, or quality_score fields)
**Schema Whiteboard dependency**: `docs/whiteboard/schema_whiteboard.md` is the Gate 2 prerequisite -- 10-section pre-implementation schema catalog that defines all types, configs, events, contracts, and protocols referenced across this plan.

## Milestone 1: Foundation

> **Goal**: Establish architectural decisions, define core types, and create message envelopes before any implementation code.
>
> **Pattern**: Same as Fabric M1 (ADR review + types + schemas).

### Epic 1.1: ADR Review & Creation

**Goal**: Review existing ADRs relevant to Orchestrator. Create/update ADRs where gaps exist.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 1.1.1 | Review ADR-0006 (Contract-Net -- Superseded) | DONE | ADR reviewed | ADR-0006 defined Contract-Net Protocol. Now superseded by Blind DAG Executor. Create superseding ADR: ORCH-001-blind-dag-supersedes-contract-net.md. |
| 1.1.2 | Review ADR-0007 (4-Stage Planning) for Orchestrator impact | DONE | ADR reviewed | Orchestrator receives CommittedPlan from Planner Stage 4. Confirm envelope schema, plan structure, dependency graph format. |
| 1.1.3 | Review ADR-0017 (Single Writer) for Orchestrator compliance | DONE | ADR reviewed | Confirm ORCH-01: Orchestrator has NO IStateWritePort. All state changes via IDeltaEmitPort -> DeltaBus -> Concierge. |
| 1.1.4 | Review ADR-0028 (Performance Scheduling) for Orchestrator | DONE | ADR reviewed | Map Orchestrator operations to WFQ priority classes. Mailbox: REALTIME. DAG waves: INTERACTIVE. Progress deltas: BACKGROUND. |
| 1.1.5 | Create ADR: Orchestrator Concurrency Model | DONE | New ADR | Single-threaded mailbox processing loop. Parallelism only within DAG waves via execute_batch(). No concurrent DAG executions (V1). |
| 1.1.6 | Create ADR: Tier Degradation Cascade | DONE | New ADR | HIGH -> CB_PLANNER OPEN -> degrade to MEDIUM. MEDIUM -> CB_ORCHESTRATOR OPEN -> degrade to LOW. **Ownership**: Tier degradation DECISIONS are Concierge FabricOrchestratorAdapter's responsibility per concierge.mmd TIER_DEGRADATION spec. Orchestrator's role: ErrorRouter CLASSIFIES adapter errors (including CIRCUIT_OPEN) and REPORTS severity in AggregatedResult. Concierge ACTS on the severity to decide degradation. This ADR documents the handoff protocol between Orchestrator error classification and Concierge degradation decisions. |
| 1.1.7 | Create ADR: Adaptive Blind DAG Extensions | DONE | New ADR | 4 deterministic extensions: Output Schema Guard (ORCH-15), Conditional Edges (ORCH-16), Micro-Replan (ORCH-13), Execution Monitor. All NO LLM. **V1 scope reduction**: TokenBudgetTracker, QualityGate, CostBudgetGuard REMOVED -- no upstream producer exists for cost_usd, tokens_consumed, or quality_score (Fabric CapabilityResult has none of these fields, no provider populates them). Re-add in V2 when LLM providers report usage metadata. |
| 1.1.8 | Create ADR: Saga Compensation Ordering | DONE | New ADR | Resolves Open Q1 (orchestrator.md S26). Decision: strict reverse order (safest, deterministic). Compensations run sequentially in reverse execution order. Concurrent compensation rejected (V1) -- ordering matters for side-effect rollback correctness. Affects Epic 2.2.5 Saga Recovery implementation. |
| 1.1.9 | Create ADR: Workflow Persistence Strategy | DONE | New ADR | Resolves Open Q2 (orchestrator.md S26). Decision: K1 SQLite LOCAL COLD for WorkflowRegistry + WorkflowScheduler trigger state. Edge-First: always available without K0. Schema: workflows table (WorkflowSpec), triggers table (TriggerSpec + next_fire_time + last_fire_time), runs table (RunManifest). Affects Epic 4.1 + 4.2 persistence layer. |
| 1.1.10 | Create ADR: MCP Connector Security Model | DONE | New ADR | **Updated scope**: Orchestrator owns discovery + registration only. Fabric owns execution isolation (CircuitBreaker timeouts, OutputValidation). Security model covers: (1) K0 proxy auth boundary -- K1 never stores auth tokens, K0 manages credentials. (2) MCP tool naming convention -- server_id prefix prevents cross-server collisions. (3) Audit: all MCP registrations and K0 proxy calls logged with trace_id. (4) Safety band defaults: MCP tools register at GREEN band (conservative) unless config overrides. **Removed from Orchestrator scope**: Per-connector process isolation (Fabric MCPProvider handles transport), resource limits (Fabric CircuitBreaker handles timeouts, OS-level limits are Fabric provider concern), network allowlist (advisory only, deferred to V2 K0 proxy). Affects Epic 5.1 thin discovery layer. |
| 1.1.11 | Create ADR: Open Questions Resolution Batch | DONE | New ADR | Resolves remaining Open Questions from orchestrator.md S26. Q3: MCP health = passive (CB_MCP tracks failures), active for critical tools. Q4: Re-read safety_band at wave boundary, trust rest. Q5: Single active run per workflow (V1), abort previous. Q6: Parent overrides take precedence for sub-workflow params. Q7: DEFERRED to V2 -- cost metering removed from V1 (no provider populates cost_usd). Q8: Conservative discovery heuristic (exact field match V1). Q9: DEFERRED to V2 -- quality thresholds removed from V1 (no provider populates quality_score). Q10: Simple comparisons only V1 (no function calls in conditions). Q11: Sub-step event rate limit max 1 per step per 500ms. |
| 1.1.12 | Create ADR: Event-Driven Concurrency for HIGH Tier | DONE | New ADR | Resolves ARCH-1 (await_plan blocking). Decision: **Option A -- Event-driven model**. dispatch_high() sends PlanRequest to Planner, parks TaskEnvelope context in PendingPlanContext store (keyed by request_id), returns to mailbox loop immediately. CommittedPlan arrives as mailbox message via k1.planner.plan.ready.v1 -> PORT_EVENT -> PORT_MAILBOX. receive_plan() retrieves parked context, dispatches DAG. Same pattern for HIL: PendingHILContext store. Timeouts: PlanRequest 45s (CB_PLANNER), HIL Constraint 60s, HIL Override 30s. Timeout reaper runs every 5s, expires stale contexts with graceful failure. This means the mailbox loop is NEVER blocked by Planner or user -- MEDIUM tasks, WorkflowRunRequests, and InterruptRequests continue processing during HIGH planning. Affects Epic 2.1.4 dispatch_high, Epic 3.1.5 HIL fallback, Epic 6.2.5 mailbox loop. |

> **Wiring**: ADRs are governance artifacts, not code. No runtime wiring. However, every ADR gates specific implementation work:
>
> - ADR-1.1.5 (Concurrency) **decides** mailbox processing model for Epic 6.2. Gates: `_mailbox_loop()` in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py` (6.2.5), `ConcurrencyGuard` in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/concurrency_guard.py` (3.2.9).
> - ADR-1.1.6 (Tier Degradation) **decides** circuit breaker fallback behavior. Gates: `ErrorRouter.route_error()` FALLBACK action in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/error_router.py` (2.1.7), `PlannerAdapter` CB_PLANNER in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/planner_adapter.py` (6.1.3).
> - ADR-1.1.7 (Adaptive DAG) **decides** scope of Epic 3.2 extensions. Gates: 5 guards in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/` -- OutputSchemaGuard (3.2.1), ConditionalEdgeEvaluator (3.2.2), MicroReplanCheckpoint (3.2.5), ExecutionMonitor (3.2.6), ConcurrencyGuard (3.2.9). **V1 removed**: TokenBudgetTracker (3.2.3), QualityGate (3.2.4), CostBudgetGuard (3.2.8) -- phantom field consumers with no data source.
> - ADR-1.1.8 (Saga Ordering) **decides** compensation execution order for Epic 2.2.5. Gates: `DAGExecutor._compensate()` LIFO ordering in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py`.
> - ADR-1.1.9 (Workflow Persistence) **decides** storage backend for Epic 4.1 + 4.2. Gates: `SQLiteWorkflowAdapter` in `k1/orchestrator/workflows/persistence/sqlite_adapter.py` (4.1.5), `IWorkflowStoragePort` in `k1/orchestrator/ports/workflow_storage_port.py` (4.1.6).
> - ADR-1.1.10 (MCP Security) **decides** security model for MCP discovery and K0 proxy. Gates: `MCPRegistrationBridge` safety_band defaults in `k1/orchestrator/connectors/mcp_registrar.py` (5.1.2), `K0ConnectorProxyClient` auth boundary in `k1/orchestrator/connectors/k0_proxy_client.py` (5.1.4). Sandbox isolation REMOVED from Orchestrator scope (Fabric owns execution isolation).
> - ADR-1.1.11 (Open Questions) **decides** 7 design questions that affect M2-M5 implementation. Cross-cutting: Q3 -> MCP health (5.1.5), Q4 -> safety_band re-read (2.2.2), Q5 -> concurrent run abort (4.2.3), Q6 -> sub-workflow param merge (4.2.5), Q8 -> gap heuristic (4.2.7), Q10 -> condition language (3.2.2), Q11 -> sub-step rate limit (3.2.7). **V1 deferred**: Q7 (cost metering), Q9 (quality thresholds).
> - ADR-1.1.12 (Event-Driven Concurrency) **decides** dispatch_high async pattern. Gates: `dispatch_high()` + `receive_plan()` + `PendingPlanContext` store in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py` (2.1.4), `_reap_stale_contexts()` timeout reaper (6.2.5).
> - **File location**: `docs/architecture/decisions-K0/ORCH-001-*.md` through `ORCH-012-*.md`. ADR naming: `ORCH-{number:03d}-{slug}.md`.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: ADRs are NOT code deliverables. Do NOT create Python files for this epic. Do NOT implement any runtime behavior. ADRs are Markdown documents that record decisions.

### Epic 1.2: Core Types & Message Envelopes

**Goal**: Create all Orchestrator domain types as Python dataclasses. These are the message envelopes and data structures used across all services.

> **Reference**: `orchestrator.mmd` ORCH_CORE, `whiteboard` Section 6 -- Message Envelopes.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 1.2.1 | TaskEnvelope dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `TaskEnvelope(envelope_id: str, intent: str, context: Dict[str, Any], tier: str, capabilities: List[str], params: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]], constraints: Dict[str, Any], trace_id: str, caller_id: str, timeout_ms: int)`. **Defaults**: envelope_id=uuid4(), tier="MEDIUM", capabilities=[], params={}, constraints={}, timeout_ms=30000. **Validation** (`__post_init__`): tier in {"MEDIUM", "HIGH"} (LOW/CRISIS never reach Orchestrator); intent non-empty; trace_id non-empty; if tier=MEDIUM then capabilities non-empty and len<=2 (ORCH-10); if tier=HIGH then capabilities may be empty (Planner decides). **Gotcha**: params is keyed by capability_name (e.g. {"tool.calendar.search": {"query": "..."}}) -- Concierge pre-resolves per-capability params from user utterance. **Import**: from k1.fabric.types import Tier (reuse enum for validation). Created by Concierge DISPATCHING state. |
| 1.2.2 | PlanRequest dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `PlanRequest(request_id: str, intent: str, constraints: Dict[str, Any], context: ContextSnapshot, trace_id: str, timeout_ms: int)`. **Defaults**: request_id=uuid4(), constraints={}, timeout_ms=45000 (from CB_PLANNER). **Validation**: intent non-empty; trace_id non-empty. **Derived from**: TaskEnvelope -- dispatch_high() builds PlanRequest from envelope.intent, envelope.constraints, and fresh StateRead snapshot. request_id is NEW (not envelope_id) because one envelope could theoretically be retried with a new plan request. **Key contract**: Planner receives this via IPlannerPort.request_plan() and echoes request_id back in CommittedPlan.request_id for PendingPlanContext correlation. |
| 1.2.3 | CommittedPlan dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `CommittedPlan(plan_id: str, request_id: str, intent: str, steps: List[PlanStep], dependencies: Dict[str, List[str]], created_at: float, trace_id: str)`. **Defaults**: dependencies={}. **V1 scope reduction**: token_budget_max and cost_budget_max_usd REMOVED -- no provider populates cost/token metadata in CapabilityResult; re-add in V2 when LLM providers report usage. **Validation**: plan_id non-empty; request_id non-empty (per SPEC-2/ADR-1.1.12: required for PendingPlanContext correlation); steps non-empty; dependencies keys must be subset of step IDs; no cycles in dependency graph (DAGExecutor trusts Planner but validates defensively). **Gotcha**: dependencies format is {"s2": ["s1"], "s3": ["s1", "s2"]} -- step_id -> list of dependency step_ids. This is REDUNDANT with PlanStep.deps but kept for quick DAG construction (Kahn's uses this dict directly). **Import**: Extends or reuses k1.fabric.types.PlanStep (Fabric has 6 fields, Orchestrator's PlanStep 1.2.18 adds 8 more). If Planner serializes CommittedPlan as JSON over event bus, provide from_dict()/to_dict() class methods. **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §10.3 defines the CommittedPlan reconciliation table -- field mapping between PlanRequest and CommittedPlan including the request_id echo validation rule (CommittedPlan.request_id MUST match PlanRequest.request_id for PendingPlanContext correlation). |
| 1.2.4 | AggregatedResult dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `AggregatedResult(result_id: str, plan_id: Optional[str], total_steps: int, completed: int, failed: int, cancelled: int, skipped: int, step_results: List[StepResult], compensations: List[CompensationRecord], success: bool, duration_ms: int, trace_id: str)`. **Defaults**: result_id=uuid4(), plan_id=None (MEDIUM tier has no plan), compensations=[]. **Computed**: success = (failed == 0 and cancelled == 0). **V1 scope reduction**: total_tokens_consumed, total_cost_usd, budget_utilization REMOVED -- no provider populates cost/token metadata; re-add in V2. **Factory methods**: `AggregatedResult.from_medium(step_results, trace_id)` for MEDIUM tier (no plan, 1-2 steps), `AggregatedResult.from_dag(plan_id, step_results, compensations, trace_id)` for HIGH tier. **Serialization**: to_dict() for event payload on k1.orchestration.dag.completed.v1. |
| 1.2.5 | StepResult dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `StepResult(step_id: str, capability_name: str, status: StepStatus, result: Optional[CapabilityResult], duration_ms: int, retry_attempts: int, schema_retry: bool, error_detail: Optional[str])`. **Defaults**: result=None, retry_attempts=0, schema_retry=False, error_detail=None. **Invariant**: if status=COMPLETED then result is not None and result.success=True; if status=FAILED then error_detail is not None; if status=CANCELLED or SKIPPED then result is None. **Import**: from k1.fabric.types import CapabilityResult. **Created by**: StepRunner.run() wraps CapabilityResult + retry metadata into StepResult. **V1 scope reduction**: cost_usd, tokens_consumed, quality_retry REMOVED -- Fabric CapabilityResult has no cost_usd/tokens_consumed/quality_score fields, and no provider populates them in result.data either. Re-add in V2 when LLM providers report usage metadata. |
| 1.2.6 | WorkflowRunRequest dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `WorkflowRunRequest(request_id: str, workflow_id: str, version: str, trigger_type: TriggerType, trigger_context: Dict[str, Any], param_overrides: Dict[str, Any], trace_id: str, priority: str)`. **Defaults**: request_id=uuid4(), trigger_context={}, param_overrides={}, priority="INTERACTIVE" (per SEM-3: workflows use INTERACTIVE, not REALTIME). **Validation**: workflow_id non-empty; version follows semver; trigger_type in TriggerType enum. **Created by**: WorkflowScheduler (cron/event) or Concierge (manual). **Routing**: OrchestratorService.process() -> dispatch_workflow() -> WorkflowEngine.execute(). |
| 1.2.7 | Wave dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass`. **Mutable** (not frozen -- resolved_params populated during execution). **Signature**: `Wave(wave_index: int, steps: List[PlanStep], resolved_params: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]])`. **Defaults**: resolved_params={}. **Internal only**: used by DAGExecutor, never serialized or sent over event bus. resolved_params is keyed by step_id -> {param_name: resolved_value}. Populated by ParamResolver before wave dispatch. **Invariant**: all steps in a wave have zero unresolved dependencies (all deps completed in prior waves). wave_index is 0-based. |
| 1.2.8 | MicroReplanRequest dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `MicroReplanRequest(request_id: str, original_plan_id: str, completed_results: Dict[str, StepResult], discoveries: List[Discovery], remaining_steps: List[PlanStep], failure_context: Optional[FailureContext], trace_id: str)`. **Defaults**: request_id=uuid4(), discoveries=[], failure_context=None. **Validation**: original_plan_id non-empty; remaining_steps non-empty (nothing to replan if empty). **Contract with Planner**: Planner receives completed_results (what succeeded so far), discoveries (new information found), remaining_steps (what was supposed to run), and failure_context (if step failed, what error/partial). Planner returns a new CommittedPlan with replacement steps. **Gotcha**: max 1 micro-replan per DAG (ORCH-13). MicroReplanCheckpoint (3.2.5) tracks replan_count and rejects if > 0. |
| 1.2.9 | Enums: StepStatus, TriggerType, ProcessResult | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | Three `str, Enum` classes (same pattern as k1.fabric.types.Tier). **StepStatus**(PENDING, RUNNING, COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED, SKIPPED) -- PENDING=initial, RUNNING=dispatched to Fabric, terminal states are COMPLETED/FAILED/CANCELLED/SKIPPED. **TriggerType**(CRON, EVENT, MANUAL) -- drives WorkflowScheduler behavior. **ProcessResult**(COMPLETED, FAILED, DEGRADED, CANCELLED, DEFERRED) -- return value of OrchestratorService.process(). DEFERRED = re-enqueued because DAG active (ConcurrencyGuard 3.2.9). DEGRADED = partial success (some steps failed but independent steps succeeded). **Gotcha**: StepStatus.SKIPPED covers both conditional-edge-false AND dependent-of-failed-optional; log the skip reason separately in StepResult.error_detail. **V1 note**: token-budget-skip reason deferred to V2 (TokenBudgetTracker removed). |
| 1.2.10 | Error severity taxonomy: ErrorSeverity enum + AdapterError | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | **ErrorSeverity**: `str, Enum` with RECOVERABLE, DEGRADED, TERMINAL. **AdapterError**: `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `AdapterError(severity: ErrorSeverity, adapter_name: str, operation: str, error_code: str, error_message: str, original_exception: Optional[Exception], fallback_action: str, trace_id: str)`. **Defaults**: original_exception=None, fallback_action="". **Classification matrix** (per orchestrator.mmd): Mailbox errors -> RECOVERABLE (re-enqueue once), Fabric errors -> DEGRADED (step fails, independent steps continue), Planner errors -> DEGRADED (degrade HIGH to MEDIUM), StateRead errors -> DEGRADED (use stale/empty context), DeltaEmit errors -> RECOVERABLE (retry once, then silent drop -- progress is best-effort), BridgeWrite errors -> RECOVERABLE (retry once, then silent drop -- audit is fire-and-forget), EventSub errors -> RECOVERABLE (resubscribe). **Consumed by**: ErrorRouter.route_error(AdapterError) -> ErrorAction. **Gotcha**: adapters catch raw exceptions and wrap in AdapterError before raising -- services never see raw exceptions from ports. |
| 1.2.11 | WorkflowSaveRequest dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `WorkflowSaveRequest(request_id: str, committed_plan_id: str, workflow_name: str, trigger_spec: TriggerSpec, trace_id: str)`. **Defaults**: request_id=uuid4(). **Validation**: committed_plan_id non-empty (must reference an executed plan); workflow_name non-empty and unique within user context (WorkflowRegistry rejects duplicates); trigger_spec is valid TriggerSpec. **TriggerSpec** (nested): `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `TriggerSpec(type: TriggerType, schedule: Optional[str], timezone: Optional[str], event_topic: Optional[str])`. schedule is cron expression (e.g. "0 9 ** MON" for every Monday 9am); timezone is IANA (e.g. "America/New_York"); event_topic is K1 event bus topic. **Validation**: if type=CRON then schedule non-empty; if type=EVENT then event_topic non-empty; if type=MANUAL then both are None. **Created by**: Concierge when user says "save this as a workflow" or "do this every Monday". |
| 1.2.12 | ProactiveGap + ProactiveGapStatus types | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | Two types. **ProactiveGapStatus**: `str, Enum` with PENDING, ASKED, RESOLVED, AUTO_RESOLVED. **ProactiveGap**: `@dataclass`. **Mutable** (status transitions from PENDING -> ASKED -> RESOLVED). **Signature**: `ProactiveGap(gap_id: str, workflow_id: str, detected_at: float, gap_type: str, affected_step_id: str, capability_name: str, old_contract_version: str, new_contract_version: str, description: str, question: Optional[str], justification: str, status: ProactiveGapStatus, resolved_value: Optional[str])`. **Defaults**: gap_id=uuid4(), detected_at=time.time(), status=PENDING, question=None, resolved_value=None. **gap_type values**: "SCHEMA_DRIFT" (field added/removed/changed), "CAPABILITY_REMOVED" (capability no longer in registry), "PERMISSION_CHANGE" (safety_band_min changed). **Lifecycle**: GapDetector (4.2.7) creates with PENDING -> surfaces to user via IDeltaEmitPort (ASKED) -> user responds (RESOLVED) or auto-filled (AUTO_RESOLVED per gap classification 4.1.3). **Persistence**: stored via IWorkflowStoragePort. |
| 1.2.13 | CompensationRecord dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass`. **Mutable** (status transitions during compensation execution). **Signature**: `CompensationRecord(record_id: str, dag_id: str, step_id: str, compensation_capability: str, compensation_params: Dict[str, Any], status: str, initiated_at: float, completed_at: Optional[float], error_detail: Optional[str])`. **Defaults**: record_id=uuid4(), status="PENDING", initiated_at=time.time(), completed_at=None, error_detail=None. **Status transitions**: PENDING -> EXECUTED (success) or PENDING -> FAILED -> DEAD_LETTERED. DEAD_LETTERED means compensation itself failed and was abandoned (logged + telemetry alert). **Created by**: Saga Recovery (2.2.5) -- one CompensationRecord per side-effect step that needs rollback. **Stored in**: AggregatedResult.compensations list, also IBridgeWritePort for K0 audit. |
| 1.2.14 | MCPServerRegistration dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Replaces old ConnectorHealthStatus** (health monitoring moved to Fabric). **Signature**: `MCPServerRegistration(server_id: str, server_type: str, endpoint: str, critical: bool, registered_capabilities: List[str], registered_at: float)`. **Defaults**: critical=False. **server_type values**: "local" (stdio), "remote" (SSE/HTTP). **Purpose**: Tracks which MCP servers have been discovered and which capability_ids were registered into Fabric for each server. Used by ConnectorLifecycleManager (5.1.3) for unregister/refresh operations. Health monitoring, availability tracking, and circuit breaker state are all owned by Fabric's HealthChecker (3.6.1), AvailabilityTracker (3.6.2), and CircuitBreaker (3.4.1). Orchestrator does NOT duplicate any of that -- it only tracks the server -> capability_id mapping needed to know what to unregister when refreshing. |
| 1.2.15 | InterruptRequest dataclass | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Signature**: `InterruptRequest(request_id: str, target_dag_id: Optional[str], interrupt_type: str, reason: str, trace_id: str)`. **Defaults**: request_id=uuid4(), target_dag_id=None (if None, targets currently-active DAG). **interrupt_type values**: "CANCEL_DAG" (stop execution, compensate side-effects), "PAUSE" (V2 -- not implemented in V1, rejected with error). "MODIFY_PARAMS" removed from V1 (per SEM-2). **Validation**: interrupt_type in allowed set; trace_id non-empty. **Delivery**: arrives via IEventSubscriptionPort on topic k1.orchestration.cancel_request. OrchestratorService.handle_interrupt() sets DAGExecutor.interrupt_flag (atomic bool). DAGExecutor checks flag at per-step completion (per SPEC-5). **Gotcha**: interrupt is cooperative -- currently-executing Fabric call completes before flag is checked. Max interrupt latency = one step execution time. |

| 1.2.17 | Complete Event Catalog constants | DONE | k1/orchestrator/events.py | **Module-level string constants** (not an enum -- plain strings for event bus compatibility). **Pattern**: `ORCH_<category>_<action> = "k1.orchestration.<category>.<action>.v1"`. **Emitted (17)**: TASK_ACCEPTED, PLAN_REQUESTED, DAG_STARTED, STEP_STARTED, STEP_COMPLETED, STEP_FAILED, STEP_CANCELLED, STEP_SKIPPED, STEP_RETRYING, STEP_SCHEMA_RETRY, SAGA_COMPENSATING, DAG_MICRO_REPLAN, DAG_COMPLETED, DELTA_V1, WORKFLOW_TRIGGERED, WORKFLOW_COMPLETED, MCP_TOOL_REGISTERED="k1.orchestration.mcp.tool_registered.v1". **V1 removed events**: STEP_QUALITY_RETRY, DAG_BUDGET_WARNING, DAG_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED (phantom field consumers -- no provider populates cost/token/quality metadata). **Consumed (12)**: PLAN_READY="k1.planner.plan.ready.v1", PLAN_FAILED="k1.planner.plan.failed.v1", PLAN_CANCELLED="k1.planner.plan.cancelled.v1", MICRO_REPLAN_READY, CAPABILITY_COMPLETED, CAPABILITY_FAILED, CONTRACT_UPDATED, AGENT_TOOL_CALL, AGENT_LLM_CALL, WORKFLOW_TRIGGER_DUE, HIL_OVERRIDE_RESPONSE="k1.hil.override_response.v1", HIL_FALLBACK_RESPONSE="k1.hil.fallback_response.v1". **Gotcha**: each constant must match exactly what IEventSubscriptionPort.subscribe() uses -- typos here mean silent event drops. Add a `ALL_EMITTED` and `ALL_CONSUMED` frozenset for bulk validation in tests. |
| 1.2.18 | PlanStep dataclass (full definition) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | **Extends** k1.fabric.types.PlanStep (6 fields: id, capability, prompt_template, params, tools_granted, deps). Orchestrator adds 8 fields for DAG execution. **Strategy**: subclass or re-define with all 14 fields (prefer re-define to avoid tight Fabric coupling; map from Fabric PlanStep via `OrchestratorPlanStep.from_fabric(fabric_step, planner_extensions)`. `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Full 14 fields**: (1) **id**: str, unique within plan, e.g. "s1". (2) **capability**: str, e.g. "tool.calendar.search" or "$s1.result.agent_name" for meta-agent dynamic resolution. (3) **params**: Dict[str, Any], may contain "$s2.result.data.field" references resolved by ParamResolver. (4) **deps**: List[str], step IDs this depends on. (5) **prompt_template**: Optional[str], for agent-type capabilities. (6) **tools_granted**: Optional[List[str]], FAB-07 scoped tools. (7) **~~token_budget~~**: ~~int, per-step from Planner PLAN-07 (default 0 = unlimited)~~ **V1 REMOVED** -- no provider populates token metadata; re-add in V2. (8) **output_schema**: Optional[Dict[str, Any]], JSON Schema for ORCH-15 output validation. (9) **condition**: Optional[ConditionExpr], ORCH-16 conditional edge (None = always execute). (10) **is_optional**: bool, default False (True = skippable by future budget guards, V2). (11) **has_side_effects**: bool, default False (True = needs compensation on failure). (12) **compensation**: Optional[str], compensation capability name. (13) **timeout_ms**: Optional[int], per-step override (None = use adapter default 30s). (14) **required_context**: Optional[List[str]], SessionState sections for Fabric ContextBuilder. **Validation**: id non-empty; capability non-empty; timeout_ms > 0 if set; deps items must exist in same plan (validated at CommittedPlan level). **V1 note**: token_budget validation removed (field removed). **Serialization**: to_dict()/from_dict() for event bus transport + WAL persistence. |
| 1.2.19 | PendingPlanContext + PendingHILContext types | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | Two internal context types for event-driven async patterns. **PendingPlanContext**: `@dataclass`. **Signature**: `PendingPlanContext(request_id: str, task_envelope: TaskEnvelope, state_snapshot: SessionSnapshot, created_at: float, timeout_ms: int)`. **Import**: `from k1.fabric.ports.state_reader import SessionSnapshot`. **Defaults**: created_at=time.time(), timeout_ms=45000. Stored in `OrchestratorService.pending_plans: Dict[str, PendingPlanContext]`. Keyed by request_id (same as PlanRequest.request_id). Created by dispatch_high(), consumed by receive_plan(). Timeout reaper expires entries where `time.time() - created_at > timeout_ms/1000`. On expiry: cancel_plan(request_id) + return FAILED AggregatedResult. **PendingHILContext**: `@dataclass`. **Signature**: `PendingHILContext(request_id: str, dag_execution_id: str, current_wave_index: int, completed_waves: List[WaveResult], remaining_waves: List[Wave], question: str, options: List[str], created_at: float, timeout_ms: int, timeout_fallback: str)`. Stores full parked DAG state. timeout_fallback: "CONTINUE" for user override HIL (silence = proceed), "GRACEFUL_FAIL" for constraint HIL (silence = cannot proceed safely). Stored in `pending_hil: Dict[str, PendingHILContext]`. **Gotcha**: both dicts grow unboundedly if reaper fails -- add max_pending_plans=50 and max_pending_hil=20 hard caps, reject with TERMINAL if exceeded. |
| 1.2.20 | Supporting types batch (15 types) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | All `@dataclass(frozen=True)` unless noted. **(1) ContextSnapshot**: **Fabric alignment**: For V1, use Fabric's `SessionSnapshot` directly (`from k1.fabric.ports.state_reader import SessionSnapshot`). SessionSnapshot is a frozen dataclass with fields: `session_id: str, sections: Dict[str, Any], timestamp_ms: int, section_names: List[str]`. If Orchestrator needs additional fields (e.g., tier, captured_at as float), define `ContextSnapshot` as a thin wrapper: `ContextSnapshot(session_snapshot: SessionSnapshot, tier: str, captured_at: float)`. For V1, prefer using SessionSnapshot directly to minimize type proliferation -- add ContextSnapshot wrapper only if needed during implementation. **(2) SectionData**: **Fabric alignment**: IStateReadPort.read_section() returns `Optional[Dict[str, Any]]` (matching Fabric's ISessionStateReader). No dedicated SectionData type needed for V1 -- sections are plain dicts. If versioning is needed later (optimistic concurrency), introduce SectionData as `data: Dict[str, Any], version: int` at that time. NOT the same as k1.sessionstate.reconstruction.SectionData (that's bytes for restoration). **(3) ValidationResult**: `valid: bool, issues: List[str], alternatives: Dict[str, str], time_pressure: bool`. time_pressure=True when estimated critical-path duration > tier time budget (per BUDGET-1). **(4) CapabilityCheck**: `step_id: str, capability: str, exists: bool, available: bool, safety_band_ok: bool, alternative: Optional[str]`. Used by ConstraintResolver to report per-capability validation. **(5) ResolutionResult**: `resolved: bool, substitutions: Dict[str, str], remaining_issues: List[str]`. ConstraintResolver output after attempting auto-resolution of validation failures. **(6) WaveResult**: `wave_index: int, step_results: List[StepResult], duration_ms: int`. **V1 scope reduction**: tokens_consumed and cost_usd fields REMOVED -- no provider populates cost/token metadata; re-add in V2. Aggregated per-wave result for DAGExecutor internal tracking. **(7) RegistryEntry**: `name: str, provider_type: str, safety_band_min: str, availability: str, compensation_capability: Optional[str], estimated_duration_ms: Optional[int]`. From IFabricGatewayPort.query_registry(). estimated_duration_ms used by BUDGET-1 time estimation. **(8) Discovery**: `field: str, value: Any, source_step_id: str`. Found during execution, triggers micro-replan evaluation. **(9) ConditionExpr**: `type: str, operands: List[Any], path: Optional[str], literal: Optional[Any]`. type in {"AND", "OR", "NOT", "EQ", "NEQ", "GT", "LT"}. path references prior step result: "s1.status", "s1.result.data.count". V1: simple comparisons only (no function calls, per ADR-1.1.11 Q10). **(10) ~~QualityDecision~~**: ~~`action: str, score: float, reason: str`. action in {"PASS", "RETRY", "SOFT_FAIL"}. score from CapabilityResult quality assessment. Thresholds: < 0.3 SOFT_FAIL, 0.3-0.7 RETRY, > 0.7 PASS (hardcoded V1, configurable V1.5).~~ **V1 REMOVED** -- QualityGate guard removed; QualityDecision type deferred to V2. **(11) SchemaResult**: `valid: bool, errors: List[str], suggestion: Optional[str]`. From output schema validation (ORCH-15). suggestion is human-readable hint for schema retry. **(12) HILRequest**: `request_id: str, question: str, options: List[str], context: Dict[str, Any], timeout_ms: int`. Emitted via IDeltaEmitPort for Concierge to surface to user. **(13) PlanAck**: `request_id: str, status: str, estimated_duration_ms: Optional[int]`. Immediate acknowledgment from Planner. status: "ACCEPTED" or "REJECTED". **(14) TaskAck**: `envelope_id: str, status: str, estimated_duration_ms: Optional[int]`. Immediate acknowledgment back to Concierge. **(15) FailureContext**: `step_id: str, error_code: str, error_message: str, partial_result: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]`. For MicroReplanRequest -- Planner needs to know what failed and any partial output. **(16) ProcessingContext**: `@dataclass ProcessingContext: trace_id: str, request_id: str, tier: str, created_at: float, dag_id: Optional[str] = None, workflow_id: Optional[str] = None`. Carried through all service calls for trace correlation (8.3.1). Created by `OrchestratorService.process()` at entry point. Passed as parameter to DAGExecutor, StepRunner, ConstraintResolver, all guards, ErrorRouter, saga compensation, and all port emit/write calls. NOT stored in pending_plans (trace_id is a field on PendingPlanContext directly). **Note**: ProcessingContext promoted to standalone issue 1.2.21 with full WB §1 field set -- remove item (16) from this batch once 1.2.21 is implemented. |
| 1.2.21 | ProcessingContext standalone type | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | **Promoted** from batch item (16) in 1.2.20. **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §1. `@dataclass`. **Full field set per WB §1**: `ProcessingContext(trace_id: str, request_id: str, tier: str, created_at: float, dag_id: Optional[str] = None, workflow_id: Optional[str] = None, parent_trace_id: Optional[str] = None, session_id: Optional[str] = None, user_id: Optional[str] = None)`. **Defaults**: created_at=time.time(), parent_trace_id=None, session_id=None, user_id=None. **Rationale**: WB §1 defines ProcessingContext as the foundational threading type carried through every service call -- standalone issue ensures thorough field coverage, validation logic, and isolated tests. **Validation** (`__post_init__`): trace_id non-empty; request_id non-empty; tier in valid set. **Carried by**: all service calls (DAGExecutor, StepRunner, ConstraintResolver, guards, ErrorRouter, saga compensation, all port emit/write calls). Created by `OrchestratorService.process()` at entry point. |
| 1.2.22 | OrchestratorConfig dataclass (~40 fields) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/config.py | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §2 (full field catalog). `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Organized into logical groups per WB §2**: (1) **Concurrency**: max_concurrent_dags: int = 3, max_wave_parallelism: int = 5, mailbox_capacity: int = 100. (2) **Timeouts**: default_step_timeout_ms: int = 30000, plan_request_timeout_ms: int = 45000, hil_timeout_ms: int = 120000, drain_timeout_ms: int = 30000. (3) **Circuit Breakers (Orchestrator-owned: CB_PLANNER only)**: cb_planner_failure_threshold: int = 3, cb_planner_reset_timeout_ms: int = 60000. (4) **~~Token Budgets~~**: ~~token_budget_multiplier: float = 1.0, cost_budget_max_usd: Optional[float] = None, token_budget_warning_pct: float = 0.80, token_budget_skip_pct: float = 0.95~~ **V1 REMOVED** -- no provider populates cost/token metadata; re-add in V2. (5) **Guard Pipeline**: guard_order: List[str] (default G0-G10 per WB §8). (6) **MCP (thin discovery layer config -- controls Orchestrator's ConnectorLifecycleManager, NOT Fabric provider config)**: mcp_config_path: str = "k1/connectors/mcp_servers.yaml", mcp_discovery_interval_ms: int = 300000, mcp_max_servers: int = 10. (7) **Telemetry**: metrics_interval_ms: int = 10000, trace_sample_rate: float = 1.0. (8) **Admin**: admin_port: int = 8081, admin_enabled: bool = True. **Validation** (`__post_init__`): all timeouts > 0; max_concurrent_dags >= 1; guard_order items unique and in known guard set; trace_sample_rate in [0.0, 1.0]. **V1 scope reduction**: token_budget_multiplier validation removed. **Factory methods**: `OrchestratorConfig.from_dict(d: Dict) -> OrchestratorConfig` and `OrchestratorConfig.default() -> OrchestratorConfig`. **Gotcha**: this is the single config object loaded by OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1) -- all other components receive their config slices from this root object. |
| 1.2.23 | CircuitBreakerConfig + CircuitBreakerState types (CB_PLANNER only) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §7. **Ownership clarification (Fabric dedup audit)**: Orchestrator owns ONLY CB_PLANNER (PlannerAdapter wraps calls to Planner mailbox). CB_FABRIC, CB_MCP, CB_BRIDGE are owned by Concierge (FabricOrchestratorAdapter) and Fabric respectively per concierge.mmd. Orchestrator's ErrorRouter CONSUMES AdapterError(CIRCUIT_OPEN) from adapters to classify errors, but does NOT own or manage CB instances for those external modules. **CircuitBreakerConfig**: `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Fields**: `name: str, failure_threshold: int, reset_timeout_ms: int, half_open_max_probes: int`. **Defaults**: half_open_max_probes=1. **CircuitBreakerState**: `@dataclass`. **Mutable** (state transitions CLOSED -> OPEN -> HALF_OPEN -> CLOSED). **Fields**: `name: str, state: str, failure_count: int, last_failure_at: Optional[float], last_success_at: Optional[float], opened_at: Optional[float]`. **state values**: "CLOSED" (healthy), "OPEN" (tripped -- all calls rejected), "HALF_OPEN" (probing -- limited calls allowed). **Defaults**: state="CLOSED", failure_count=0, all timestamps=None. **Orchestrator CB instance**: CB_PLANNER(threshold=3, reset=60000, probes=1) -- created by PlannerAdapter (6.1.3) constructor. **Removed from Orchestrator**: CB_FABRIC, CB_MCP, CB_BRIDGE config defaults (owned by Concierge/Fabric). **Methods on state**: `record_failure()`, `record_success()`, `should_allow() -> bool`, `check_reset_timeout() -> bool`. **Used by**: PlannerAdapter (6.1.3) for CB_PLANNER gating, ErrorRouter (2.1.7) for classifying AdapterError(CIRCUIT_OPEN) from any adapter (does NOT own the CB instance, just reads the error type). **Consumed CB state for health reporting**: HealthStatus (1.2.25) reports CB_PLANNER state directly + consumed CB states from Concierge/Fabric via event subscription for admin visibility. |
| 1.2.24 | Guard pipeline types (GuardAction, GuardDecision, 3 Protocols) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/types.py | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §8 (guard pipeline G0-G10). **GuardAction**: `str, Enum` with ALLOW, REJECT, DEGRADE, DEFER. ALLOW = proceed; REJECT = stop with error; DEGRADE = downgrade tier (HIGH->MEDIUM); DEFER = re-enqueue for later. **GuardDecision**: `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. **Fields**: `guard_name: str, action: GuardAction, reason: str, degraded_to: Optional[str] = None`. Per WB §8: each guard returns a GuardDecision; pipeline short-circuits on first REJECT. **3 Protocol classes per WB §8**: (1) `PreDispatchGuard(Protocol)`: `def check(self, envelope: TaskEnvelope, ctx: ProcessingContext) -> GuardDecision` -- used by guards G0-G4 that run BEFORE dispatch routing. (2) `PreWaveGuard(Protocol)`: `def check(self, wave: Wave, ctx: ProcessingContext) -> GuardDecision` -- used by guards G7-G8 that run BEFORE each wave. **V1 scope reduction**: CostAccumulator param removed (no provider populates cost metadata). (3) `PostStepGuard(Protocol)`: `def check(self, result: StepResult, ctx: ProcessingContext) -> GuardDecision` -- used by guard G9 that runs AFTER each step. **V1 removed**: G10=QualityGuard. **Guard ordering per WB §8**: G0=SchemaGuard, G1=TierGuard, G2=RateLimitGuard, G3=CircuitBreakerGuard, G4=ConcurrencyGuard, G7=TimeoutGuard, G8=MicroReplanCheckpoint, G9=OutputSchemaGuard. **V1 removed**: G5=TokenBudgetGuard, G6=CostBudgetGuard, G10=QualityGuard -- phantom field consumers. **Gotcha**: guards are ordered; OrchestratorFactory wires them in the sequence defined by `OrchestratorConfig.guard_order` (1.2.22). |

| 1.2.26 | PlanRequest + CapabilityDescriptor JSON Schemas | DONE | k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/ | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §10.2 (PlanRequest wire-format) + §10.5 (CapabilityDescriptor). Create JSON Schema definitions for Planner-Orchestrator protocol validation. **Deliverables**: (1) `plan_request.v1.json` -- required fields (request_id: string, intent: string, trace_id: string, timeout_ms: integer), optional fields (constraints: object, context: object with sections property). Field constraints: request_id format uuid, timeout_ms minimum 1000. (2) `capability_descriptor.v1.json` -- required fields (name: string, provider_type: string, safety_band_min: string), optional fields (estimated_duration_ms: integer, compensation_capability: string). provider_type enum: ["tool", "agent", "meta_agent"]. safety_band_min enum: ["OPEN", "STANDARD", "RESTRICTED"]. **Contract**: these wire-format schemas are what IPlannerPort serializes -- Planner validates on receipt. Must match dataclass signatures in 1.2.2 and 1.2.20 (RegistryEntry). **Gotcha**: keep JSON Schema files separate from Python types -- generated validation uses jsonschema library. |
| 1.2.27 | MicroReplanRequest JSON Schema | DONE | k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/ | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §10.6 (MicroReplanRequest sync round-trip). **Deliverable**: `micro_replan_request.v1.json`. **Required fields**: request_id (string, uuid format), original_plan_id (string), completed_results (object -- keyed by step_id, values are StepResult objects), remaining_steps (array of PlanStep objects, minItems: 1), trace_id (string). **Optional fields**: discoveries (array of Discovery objects, default []), failure_context (object conforming to FailureContext schema -- step_id, error_code, error_message, partial_result). **Validation**: remaining_steps must be non-empty (nothing to replan if empty); completed_results keys must not overlap with remaining_steps ids. **Contract**: Planner validates this schema on receipt and returns new CommittedPlan within CB_PLANNER timeout (60s). Max 1 micro-replan per DAG execution (ORCH-13) -- enforced by MicroReplanCheckpoint (3.2.5), not by schema. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: All types in `k1/orchestrator/types.py` EXCEPT event constants in `k1/orchestrator/events.py` (1.2.17). Do NOT split types into multiple files -- single-file module keeps imports simple.
> - **Creation → Consumption map**:
>   - **TaskEnvelope** (1.2.1): Created by Concierge DISPATCHING state -> enqueued to `IMailboxPort.enqueue()` -> dequeued by `OrchestratorService._mailbox_loop()` (6.2.5) -> routed by `process()` (2.1.1) -> consumed by `route_task()` (2.1.2).
>   - **PlanRequest** (1.2.2): Created by `dispatch_high()` (2.1.4) from TaskEnvelope fields -> sent via `IPlannerPort.request_plan()` (1.4.3) -> consumed by Planner module (external).
>   - **CommittedPlan** (1.2.3): Created by Planner -> arrives via `IEventSubscriptionPort` on topic `k1.planner.plan.ready.v1` -> routed to `IMailboxPort` by event handler (6.2.2 step 8) -> dequeued by mailbox loop -> consumed by `receive_plan()` (2.1.4) -> passed to `DAGExecutor.execute()` (2.2.2) -> `build_waves()` (2.2.1) uses `plan.steps` + `plan.dependencies`.
>   - **AggregatedResult** (1.2.4): Created by `OrchestratorService.aggregate()` (2.1.5) -> emitted via `IDeltaEmitPort.emit()` on topic `ORCH_DAG_COMPLETED` -> consumed by Concierge COMPANIONING state (external).
>   - **StepResult** (1.2.5): Created by `StepRunner.run()` (2.3.1) wrapping CapabilityResult -> stored in `DAGExecutor.merged_results` -> consumed by `collect_results()` (2.2.3) -> embedded in AggregatedResult.
>   - **WorkflowRunRequest** (1.2.6): Created by `WorkflowScheduler._tick()` (4.2.1) for cron/event triggers -> enqueued to `IMailboxPort` at INTERACTIVE priority -> consumed by `dispatch_workflow()` in OrchestratorService.
>   - **Wave** (1.2.7): Created by `DAGExecutor.build_waves()` (2.2.1) from CommittedPlan -> consumed by `execute_wave()` (2.2.2). Internal only, never serialized.
>   - **MicroReplanRequest** (1.2.8): Created by `MicroReplanCheckpoint.after_wave()` (3.2.5) -> sent via `IPlannerPort.micro_replan()` (1.4.3) -> consumed by Planner module.
>   - **WorkflowSaveRequest** (1.2.11): Created by Concierge -> enqueued to mailbox -> consumed by `save_workflow()` (2.1.6) -> `WorkflowRegistry.save()` (4.1.1) -> `IWorkflowStoragePort.save_workflow()` (4.1.6).
>   - **ProactiveGap** (1.2.12): Created by `ProactiveGapDetector._on_contract_updated()` (4.2.7) -> stored via `IWorkflowStoragePort.save_gap()` -> surfaced via `IDeltaEmitPort.emit()` to Concierge.
>   - **CompensationRecord** (1.2.13): Created by saga recovery in `DAGExecutor._compensate()` (2.2.5) -> stored in `AggregatedResult.compensations` list -> audited via `IBridgeWritePort.submit_audit()`.
>   - **InterruptRequest** (1.2.15): Created externally -> arrives via `IEventSubscriptionPort` on topic `k1.orchestration.cancel_request` -> routed to mailbox -> consumed by `handle_interrupt()` in OrchestratorService -> sets `DAGExecutor.interrupt_flag = True`.
>   - **Event constants** (1.2.17): Imported by every service that emits/subscribes to events. `from k1.orchestrator.events import ORCH_DAG_STARTED, ORCH_STEP_COMPLETED, ...`. Single source of truth -- do NOT hardcode topic strings anywhere else.
>   - **PlanStep** (1.2.18): 13-field canonical definition (was 14; token_budget removed V1). Used by DAGExecutor, StepRunner, ConstraintResolver, all 5 guards, WorkflowCompiler, ParamResolver. Import from `k1.orchestrator.types`, NOT from `k1.fabric.types`.
>   - **PendingPlanContext** (1.2.19): Created by `dispatch_high()` (2.1.4) -> stored in `OrchestratorService.pending_plans: Dict[str, PendingPlanContext]` -> consumed by `receive_plan()` (2.1.4) which pops by `request_id`. Expired by `_reap_stale_contexts()` (6.2.5).
>   - **PendingHILContext** (1.2.19): Created by `trigger_hil_fallback()` (3.1.5) or `ExecutionMonitor.after_wave()` (3.2.6) -> stored in `OrchestratorService.pending_hil` -> consumed by HIL response handler via `IEventSubscriptionPort`.
>   - **Supporting types** (1.2.20): Return types for port methods and service functions. ContextSnapshot used by IStateReadPort, ValidationResult by ConstraintResolver, RegistryEntry by IFabricGatewayPort, etc.
>   - **ProcessingContext** (1.2.21): Created by `OrchestratorService.process()` at entry point -> passed as parameter through DAGExecutor, StepRunner, ConstraintResolver, all guards, ErrorRouter, saga compensation, all port emit/write calls -> consumed by every service method for trace correlation. Promoted from batch item (16) in 1.2.20 per Schema Whiteboard §1.
>   - **OrchestratorConfig** (1.2.22): Created by `OrchestratorConfig.from_dict()` during config loading -> consumed by `OrchestratorFactory.create_production()` (6.2.1) to configure all components. Single config root object per Schema Whiteboard §2.
>   - **CircuitBreakerConfig + CircuitBreakerState** (1.2.23): CircuitBreakerConfig created from OrchestratorConfig CB fields -> CircuitBreakerState created per circuit breaker instance (4 total per Schema Whiteboard §7). Consumed by ErrorRouter (2.1.7) and all adapter implementations for call gating.
>   - **Guard types** (1.2.24): GuardAction, GuardDecision, 3 Protocol classes per Schema Whiteboard §8. Consumed by OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1) for guard pipeline wiring, 5 active guard implementations (3.2.1, 3.2.2, 3.2.5, 3.2.6, 3.2.9), DAGExecutor pre-wave checks, StepRunner post-step checks. **V1 removed**: G5/G6/G10 guards.
>   - **Admin types** (1.2.25): HealthStatus, ActiveDAGInfo, DrainResult per Schema Whiteboard §5/§6. Consumed by IAdminPort (Epic 6.3), health check probes (8.1.x), OrchestratorFactory graceful shutdown (6.2.4).
>   - **PlanRequest JSON Schema** (1.2.26): Wire-format schemas in `k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/`. Consumed by IPlannerPort adapter for serialization validation, Planner module for deserialization validation. Per Schema Whiteboard §10.2/§10.5.
>   - **MicroReplanRequest JSON Schema** (1.2.27): Wire-format schema in `k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/`. Consumed by IPlannerPort adapter for micro-replan serialization, Planner module for deserialization. Per Schema Whiteboard §10.6.
> - **Import graph**: `k1.orchestrator.types` imports from `k1.fabric.types` (Tier enum, CapabilityRequest, CapabilityResult) but NEVER from any service or port module (no circular deps). `k1.orchestrator.events` imports nothing except stdlib. New: `k1.orchestrator.config` (1.2.22) imports from `k1.orchestrator.types` only.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: All types are pure dataclasses with NO I/O, NO port references, NO service logic. Do NOT add async methods to dataclasses. Do NOT import from `k1.orchestrator.orchestration/*` in types.py. Do NOT create separate type files per service -- single `types.py` module. Exception: OrchestratorConfig (1.2.22) lives in `config.py` to avoid circular imports with factory.

### Epic 1.3: Contract Schema Registration

**Goal**: Register Orchestrator contract schemas in the K1 contracts system.

Do NOT create runtime config loaders here -- that logic lives in OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1). policies.contract.yaml values are read ONCE at construction and passed to services via constructor args.

### Epic 1.4: Port Interface Definitions (Protocol Stubs)

**Goal**: Define all 8 port interfaces as Python Protocol/ABC classes. These are pure interface stubs with no implementation -- they establish the dependency injection contracts that M2-M5 services code against.

> **Reference**: `orchestrator.mmd` PORTS subgraph (7 original ports + IWorkflowStoragePort).
>
> **Why in M1**: Services in M2-M5 use constructor-injected port dependencies. Defining port interfaces before services ensures services are coded against well-defined contracts from day one. Adapter implementations come later in M6.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 1.4.1 | IMailboxPort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/mailbox_port.py | `class IMailboxPort(Protocol)`. **All methods sync** (mailbox is in-process, no I/O). **Methods**: `def enqueue(self, message: Any, priority: str = "INTERACTIVE") -> int` -- returns queue position (0 = front). Raises `MailboxFullError` if depth >= max_depth (100). `def dequeue(self) -> Optional[Any]` -- returns highest-priority message or None if empty. WFQ across 3 priority classes: REALTIME > INTERACTIVE > BACKGROUND. `def depth(self) -> int` -- current queue size. `def peek_priority(self) -> Optional[str]` -- priority of next message without removing. **Type union for messages**: `MailboxMessage = Union[TaskEnvelope, CommittedPlan, WorkflowRunRequest, WorkflowSaveRequest, InterruptRequest, PlanFailedEvent, PlanCancelledEvent]`. Consider using this union as the enqueue parameter type instead of Any. **Gotcha**: dequeue() must be O(1) or O(log n) -- WFQ with 3 queues + round-robin weights: REALTIME gets 60%, INTERACTIVE 30%, BACKGROUND 10% of dequeue slots. |
| 1.4.2 | IFabricGatewayPort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/fabric_gateway_port.py | `class IFabricGatewayPort(Protocol)`. **All methods async** (Fabric calls are I/O). **Methods**: `async def execute(self, request: CapabilityRequest) -> CapabilityResult` -- single capability invocation. Adapter wraps Fabric errors in AdapterError(DEGRADED). `async def execute_batch(self, requests: List[CapabilityRequest]) -> List[CapabilityResult]` -- parallel invocation via asyncio.gather(). Order of results matches order of requests. If one fails, others still complete (partial failure). `async def query_registry(self, capability_name: str) -> Optional[RegistryEntry]` -- checks Fabric CapabilityRegistry for existence + metadata. Returns None if not found. **NOT provided**: spawn() removed from V1 port (meta-agent spawn flows through regular execute() with agent.build capability). **Import**: from k1.fabric.types import CapabilityRequest, CapabilityResult. **Gotcha**: adapter does NOT own circuit breaker (per PROTOCOL-4: Concierge owns CB_FABRIC). Adapter is pure pass-through that translates errors. |
| 1.4.3 | IPlannerPort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/planner_port.py | `class IPlannerPort(Protocol)`. **SOFT CONSTRAINT**: Planner is not yet implemented -- these method signatures define Orchestrator's requirements that Planner will satisfy. Exact API can be adjusted when building Planner. **All methods async**. **Methods**: `async def request_plan(self, request: PlanRequest) -> PlanAck` -- fire-and-forget to Planner mailbox. Returns PlanAck (ACCEPTED/REJECTED). Does NOT return CommittedPlan (that arrives via event bus, per ADR-1.1.12). `async def cancel_plan(self, request_id: str) -> None` -- cancel in-progress planning. Best-effort (Planner may have already committed). `async def micro_replan(self, request: MicroReplanRequest) -> Optional[CommittedPlan]` -- unlike request_plan, micro_replan is SYNCHRONOUS with 10s timeout (per PROTOCOL-3). Returns new CommittedPlan or None on timeout/failure. Rationale: micro-replan happens mid-DAG, DAGExecutor is already parked, waiting 10s is acceptable. **Removed**: await_plan() -- no longer needed with event-driven model. **Gotcha**: request_plan() translates to Planner mailbox enqueue; the Planner-side adapter must route PlanRequest to Planner's own mailbox. |
| 1.4.4 | IStateReadPort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/state_read_port.py | `class IStateReadPort(Protocol)`. **All methods async** (SessionState may involve I/O). **Fabric alignment**: Aligns with Fabric's `ISessionStateReader` (Protocol in `k1/fabric/ports/state_reader.py`) which is the established read-only interface to SessionState. Uses same method signatures for consistency across K1 modules. **Methods**: `async def read_section(self, session_id: str, section: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]` -- reads single section by name (e.g. "control", "user_preferences", "affective_now"). Returns None if section not found. session_id from TaskEnvelope.session_id or ProcessingContext.session_id. `async def read_sections(self, session_id: str, names: List[str]) -> Dict[str, Any]` -- reads multiple sections, returns dict keyed by section name. `async def get_snapshot(self, session_id: str) -> SessionSnapshot` -- reads all sections atomically into SessionSnapshot (frozen dataclass from `k1.fabric.ports.state_reader`: session_id, sections, timestamp_ms, section_names). If session_id not found, returns empty snapshot. **Import**: `from k1.fabric.ports.state_reader import SessionSnapshot`. **CRITICAL INVARIANT (ORCH-01)**: NO write methods. This port is intentionally read-only. If any implementor adds a write method, it violates the Orchestrator's core identity. **Used by**: dispatch_high() for initial snapshot, receive_plan() for re-snapshot (RACE-1), DAGExecutor.execute_wave() for safety_band re-read. **Gotcha**: get_snapshot() should be cheap (in-memory read from SessionState's latest committed state). Do not trigger reconstruction or hydration. **Note on ContextSnapshot**: Supporting type ContextSnapshot (1.2.20 item 1) is an Orchestrator-internal alias/wrapper if additional fields needed; for V1, prefer using SessionSnapshot directly from Fabric. |
| 1.4.5 | IDeltaEmitPort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/delta_emit_port.py | `class IDeltaEmitPort(Protocol)`. **All methods async, fire-and-forget** (delta delivery is best-effort, never blocks Orchestrator). **Methods**: `async def emit(self, event_topic: str, payload: Dict[str, Any], trace_id: str) -> None` -- emits typed event to DeltaBus. payload is JSON-serializable dict (AggregatedResult.to_dict(), StepResult.to_dict(), etc.). `async def emit_progress(self, step_id: str, summary: str, trace_id: str) -> None` -- shorthand for k1.hil.progress.v1 events (human-readable progress for Concierge PROGRESSING state). `async def emit_hil_request(self, hil_request: HILRequest, trace_id: str) -> None` -- surfaces a question to user (constraint HIL or override HIL). **Error handling**: all methods catch exceptions internally, log warning, and return silently. Orchestrator NEVER fails because delta delivery failed. **Import**: uses event catalog constants (1.2.17) for topic strings. |
| 1.4.6 | IBridgeWritePort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/bridge_write_port.py | `class IBridgeWritePort(Protocol)`. **All methods async, fire-and-forget** (K0 writes are best-effort, Edge-First). **Methods**: `async def submit_audit(self, run_manifest: Dict[str, Any], trace_id: str) -> None` -- writes completed DAG execution record to K0 for audit trail. run_manifest is AggregatedResult.to_dict() + CommittedPlan metadata. `async def write_wal(self, dag_id: str, entry_type: str, payload: Dict[str, Any], trace_id: str) -> None` -- WAL write for crash recovery. entry_type in {"PLAN_START", "WAVE_COMPLETE", "STEP_COMPLETE", "DAG_COMPLETE"}. Replaces submit_plan() with more granular WAL protocol (per 2.2.6). `async def read_wal(self, dag_id: str) -> Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]]` -- reads WAL entries for crash recovery (6.2.4). Returns None if no entries found. `async def list_wal_ids(self) -> List[str]` -- returns all dag_ids with active WAL entries (not yet DAG_COMPLETE or cleaned up). Required by crash_recovery() (6.2.4) to scan for in-flight DAGs after restart. `async def submit_deferred_result(self, result: Dict[str, Any], workflow_id: str, trace_id: str) -> None` -- persists workflow execution result for later retrieval when no user session is active (PROD-4: cron at 3 AM). Concierge checks for pending deferred results on next session start (documented in cross-component contract 1.3.4). Fire-and-forget: if K0 offline, result is lost (V1 limitation, logged). **Error handling**: write methods catch exceptions, log warning, return silently. K0 offline = WAL writes silently fail, DAG cannot be recovered from crash (documented V1 limitation). read_wal() raises AdapterError(DEGRADED) if K0 is unreachable. list_wal_ids() raises AdapterError(DEGRADED) if K0 is unreachable (crash_recovery skips recovery on this error). |
| 1.4.7 | IEventSubscriptionPort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/event_subscription_port.py | `class IEventSubscriptionPort(Protocol)`. **Fabric alignment**: Method signatures aligned with Fabric's `IEventPort` (Protocol in `k1/fabric/ports/event_port.py`) which is the established K1 event bus interface. Reuses `SubscriptionHandle` type from Fabric. **Import**: `from k1.fabric.ports.event_port import SubscriptionHandle`. **Methods**: `def subscribe(self, topic: str, handler: Callable[[str, Dict[str, Any]], None]) -> SubscriptionHandle` -- subscribes handler to topic pattern. Returns SubscriptionHandle (frozen dataclass: subscription_id, topic). Handler is SYNC and receives (topic, payload) -- handler signature is `Callable[[str, Dict[str, Any]], None]` matching Fabric convention. If Orchestrator needs async processing, handler should enqueue to mailbox (sync) and async processing happens in mailbox loop. Supports wildcards: e.g., `k1.capability.*` matches capability lifecycle events. `def unsubscribe(self, handle: SubscriptionHandle) -> bool` -- removes subscription. Returns True if subscription existed, False otherwise. `def emit(self, topic: str, payload: Dict[str, Any]) -> None` -- publishes event to bus (renamed from 'publish' to match Fabric's IEventPort.emit()). Used for internal re-routing (e.g., routing received plan events back to mailbox). **Note on sync methods**: Fabric's IEventPort is synchronous (no async). Orchestrator's IEventSubscriptionPort matches this. If future event bus needs async, the adapter layer bridges it. **Required subscriptions** (registered at startup by OrchestratorFactory): k1.planner.plan.ready.v1 -> route to mailbox as CommittedPlan, k1.planner.plan.failed.v1 -> route to mailbox as PlanFailedEvent, k1.planner.plan.cancelled.v1 -> route to mailbox as PlanCancelledEvent, k1.hil.override_response.v1 -> route to PendingHILContext resolver, k1.hil.fallback_response.v1 -> route to PendingHILContext resolver, k1.fabric.capability.contract_updated.v1 -> route to GapDetector, fabric.agent.*.tool_call.* -> route to ExecutionMonitor (sub-step observability), fabric.agent.*.llm_call.* -> route to ExecutionMonitor. **Reconnection** (per additional finding): exponential backoff 1s..30s, re-subscribe all topics on reconnect, at-most-once delivery (missed events not replayed). |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: Each port in its own file under `k1/orchestrator/ports/`. Package init: `k1/orchestrator/ports/__init__.py` re-exports all 8 Protocols for clean imports: `from k1.orchestrator.ports import IMailboxPort, IFabricGatewayPort, ...`.
> - **Port → Adapter mapping** (prod adapters in M6 Epic 6.1, test adapters in M6 Epic 6.1):
>   - `IMailboxPort` (1.4.1) -> prod: `MailboxAdapter` (6.1.1) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/mailbox_adapter.py` / test: `TestMailboxAdapter` (6.1.8) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/test_mailbox_adapter.py`.
>   - `IFabricGatewayPort` (1.4.2) -> prod: `FabricGatewayAdapter` (6.1.2) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/fabric_gateway_adapter.py` / test: `MockFabricAdapter` (6.1.9) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/mock_fabric_adapter.py`.
>   - `IPlannerPort` (1.4.3) -> prod: `PlannerAdapter` (6.1.3) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/planner_adapter.py` / test: `MockPlannerAdapter` (6.1.10) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/mock_planner_adapter.py`.
>   - `IStateReadPort` (1.4.4) -> prod: `StateReadAdapter` (6.1.4) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/state_read_adapter.py` / test: `MockStateReadAdapter` (6.1.11) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/mock_state_read_adapter.py`.
>   - `IDeltaEmitPort` (1.4.5) -> prod: `DeltaEmitAdapter` (6.1.5) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/delta_emit_adapter.py` / test: `TestDeltaAdapter` (6.1.12) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/test_delta_adapter.py`.
>   - `IBridgeWritePort` (1.4.6) -> prod: `BridgeWriteAdapter` (6.1.6) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/bridge_write_adapter.py` / test: `MockBridgeAdapter` (6.1.13) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/mock_bridge_adapter.py`.
>   - `IEventSubscriptionPort` (1.4.7) -> prod: `EventSubscriptionAdapter` (6.1.7) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/event_subscription_adapter.py` / test: `TestEventAdapter` (6.1.14) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/test_event_adapter.py`.
>   - `IWorkflowStoragePort` (4.1.6) -> prod: `WorkflowStorageAdapter` / `SQLiteWorkflowAdapter` (6.1.15, 4.1.5) / test: `TestWorkflowStorageAdapter` (6.1.16) in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/test_workflow_storage_adapter.py`.
> - **Constructor injection pattern**: Every service in M2-M5 accepts ports as constructor parameters. Example: `OrchestratorService.__init__(self, mailbox: IMailboxPort, fabric_port: IFabricGatewayPort, ...)`. Services NEVER construct their own adapters -- `OrchestratorFactory` (6.2.1) handles all wiring.
> - **Import rules**: Port files import ONLY from `k1.orchestrator.types` and stdlib. NEVER import from services (`k1.orchestrator.orchestration/*`), adapters (`k1.orchestrator.adapters/*`), or external modules (`k1.fabric.*`). Exception: `IFabricGatewayPort` (1.4.2) references `CapabilityRequest` and `CapabilityResult` from `k1.fabric.types` (cross-module types shared at the port boundary).
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (ports reference TaskEnvelope, CommittedPlan, ContextSnapshot, RegistryEntry, etc.).
> - **Consumed by**: All services in M2-M5 (constructor injection), all adapters in M6 (implement the Protocol), all tests in M7 (verify Protocol compliance).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Port files contain ZERO implementation logic. Every method body is either `...` (Protocol) or `raise NotImplementedError` (ABC). Do NOT add default implementations. Do NOT add helper methods. Do NOT add state (no `__init__` beyond Protocol requirements). The `IWorkflowStoragePort` (4.1.6) is defined in Epic 4.1, NOT in this epic -- it ships with the workflow subsystem.

### Epic 1.5: Event Payload JSON Schemas

**Goal**: Pre-define all 32 event payload JSON Schemas referenced by the event catalog (1.2.17). Ensures every event emitted or consumed by the Orchestrator has a validated wire-format schema before any service code is written.

> **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §3 (32 event payload JSON Schemas) + §10 (Planner-Orchestrator protocol schemas).

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 1.5.1 | Create 17 emitted event payload JSON Schemas (3 V1 REMOVED) | DONE | k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/events/emitted/ | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §3 (emitted events). Create one JSON Schema file per emitted event topic from event catalog (1.2.17). **Files**: `task_accepted.v1.json`, `plan_requested.v1.json`, `dag_started.v1.json`, `step_started.v1.json`, `step_completed.v1.json`, `step_failed.v1.json`, `step_cancelled.v1.json`, `step_skipped.v1.json`, `step_retrying.v1.json`, `step_schema_retry.v1.json`, `step_quality_retry.v1.json`, `saga_compensating.v1.json`, `dag_micro_replan.v1.json`, `dag_budget_warning.v1.json`, `dag_budget_exhausted.v1.json`, `dag_completed.v1.json`, `delta_v1.v1.json`, `workflow_triggered.v1.json`, `workflow_completed.v1.json`, `mcp_tool_registered.v1.json`. **Each schema includes**: required fields (event_id, topic, timestamp, trace_id, payload), payload object with event-specific fields per WB §3. **Validation**: All schemas must pass JSON Schema Draft 2020-12 metaschema validation. **Gotcha**: event payloads are Dict[str, Any] on the wire -- schemas define the structure the dict must conform to. IDeltaEmitPort.emit() should validate payload against schema before publishing. |
| 1.5.2 | Create 12 consumed event payload JSON Schemas | DONE | k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/events/consumed/ | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §3 (consumed events). Create one JSON Schema file per consumed event topic from event catalog (1.2.17). **Files**: `plan_ready.v1.json` (CommittedPlan payload from Planner), `plan_failed.v1.json`, `plan_cancelled.v1.json`, `micro_replan_ready.v1.json`, `capability_completed.v1.json`, `capability_failed.v1.json`, `contract_updated.v1.json`, `agent_tool_call.v1.json`, `agent_llm_call.v1.json`, `workflow_trigger_due.v1.json`, `hil_override_response.v1.json`, `hil_fallback_response.v1.json`. **Each schema includes**: required fields (event_id, topic, timestamp, trace_id, payload), payload object with event-specific fields. **Key schemas**: `plan_ready.v1.json` mirrors CommittedPlan (1.2.3) fields; `capability_completed.v1.json` mirrors CapabilityResult from Fabric. **Validation**: IEventSubscriptionPort handlers should validate incoming payloads against these schemas -- malformed events logged + dropped (RECOVERABLE per 1.2.10). |
| 1.5.3 | Create Planner contract YAML (module + wiring) | DONE | k1/contracts/modules/planner/ | **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §10 (Planner-Orchestrator protocol). Define the Planner-side contract that the Orchestrator depends on. **Deliverables**: (1) `module.contract.yaml` -- Planner module metadata, exports (PlannerService), ports consumed (capability registry read, event pub/sub). (2) `wiring.contract.yaml` -- emitted events (plan_ready, plan_failed, plan_cancelled, micro_replan_ready), consumed events (plan_requested from Orchestrator), required request/response schemas (PlanRequest per WB §10.2, CommittedPlan per WB §10.3, MicroReplanRequest per WB §10.6). **Key protocol fields per WB §10**: request_id echo contract (PlanRequest.request_id MUST appear in CommittedPlan.request_id), PlanAck within 500ms (WB §10.4), CapabilityDescriptor format for registry queries (WB §10.5). **Gotcha**: this is a contract FROM the Orchestrator's perspective -- Planner team may extend it but must honor these minimums. Cross-referenced by 1.3.1 dependencies section. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: Event payload schemas in `k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/events/emitted/` (1.5.1) and `k1/contracts/schemas/orchestrator/events/consumed/` (1.5.2). Planner contract in `k1/contracts/modules/planner/` (1.5.3).
> - **Schema validation integration**: IDeltaEmitPort adapter (6.1.5) validates emitted event payloads against schemas in 1.5.1 before publishing. IEventSubscriptionPort adapter (6.1.7) validates consumed event payloads against schemas in 1.5.2 on receipt. Validation failures are RECOVERABLE (logged + dropped, per ErrorSeverity taxonomy 1.2.10).
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (schemas mirror dataclass field sets), Epic 1.3 contracts (event lists in wiring.contract.yaml reference these schemas).
> - **Consumed by**: Adapter implementations in M6 (schema validation), contract tests in M7 Epic 7.4 (schema round-trip tests), Planner module (1.5.3 defines the protocol boundary).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: JSON Schema files are DECLARATIVE -- no Python code generation from schemas in V1. Validation uses `jsonschema` library at runtime. Do NOT create a schema registry service -- simple file-based lookup by topic name.
