## Milestone 2: Core DAG Engine

> **Goal**: Implement the heart of the Orchestrator -- message routing, DAG execution, and single-step execution. This enables MEDIUM and HIGH tier flows (without adaptive extensions).
>
> **Prerequisite**: M1 complete (types and envelopes exist).
>
> **Pattern**: Same as Fabric M2-M3 -- build core data path first.

### Epic 2.1: OrchestratorService (Central Dispatch)

**Goal**: Implement the central routing logic that dequeues messages and dispatches by type/tier.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 2.1.1 | Implement OrchestratorService | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, mailbox: IMailboxPort, dag_executor: DAGExecutor, constraint_resolver: ConstraintResolver, workflow_engine: WorkflowEngine, connector_lifecycle: ConnectorLifecycleManager, error_router: ErrorRouter, concurrency_guard: ConcurrencyGuard, fabric_port: IFabricGatewayPort, planner_port: IPlannerPort, state_port: IStateReadPort, delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort, bridge_port: IBridgeWritePort)`. **Internal state**: `self.pending_plans: Dict[str, PendingPlanContext] = {}`, `self.pending_hil: Dict[str, PendingHILContext] = {}`, `self.executed_plans: OrderedDict[str, float] = OrderedDict()` (bounded LRU set, max 100 keys, evicts oldest when full). **Primary method**: `async def process(self, message: MailboxMessage) -> ProcessResult`. Routes by isinstance: TaskEnvelope -> route_task(), CommittedPlan -> receive_plan(), PlanFailedEvent -> receive_plan_failed(), PlanCancelledEvent -> receive_plan_cancelled(), WorkflowRunRequest -> dispatch_workflow(), WorkflowSaveRequest -> save_workflow(), InterruptRequest -> handle_interrupt(). Unknown type -> log warning + return FAILED. **Preconditions**: concurrency_guard.acquire() must succeed before dispatching to DAGExecutor (checked in route_task/dispatch_workflow, not in process()). **Error handling**: all adapter errors caught, routed via error_router.route_error(). Uncaught exceptions -> TERMINAL + log. |
| 2.1.2 | Implement route_task() (MEDIUM vs HIGH) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def route_task(self, envelope: TaskEnvelope) -> ProcessResult`. **Logic**: (1) Validate envelope: trace_id non-empty, intent non-empty, tier in {"MEDIUM", "HIGH"}. Invalid -> log + return FAILED via error_router. (2) Emit TASK_ACCEPTED event via delta_port. (3) Branch on tier: MEDIUM -> dispatch_medium(envelope), HIGH -> dispatch_high(envelope). (4) Return ProcessResult from dispatch. **Precondition**: envelope has already been dequeued from mailbox by the mailbox loop (6.2.5). **Error handling**: if envelope validation fails, return FAILED immediately (don't enter dispatch). If dispatch raises AdapterError, route through error_router -- RECOVERABLE -> re-enqueue envelope to mailbox (once), DEGRADED -> return DEGRADED, TERMINAL -> return FAILED. |
| 2.1.3 | Implement dispatch_medium() | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def dispatch_medium(self, envelope: TaskEnvelope) -> ProcessResult`. **Preconditions**: envelope.tier == "MEDIUM"; envelope.capabilities non-empty; len(envelope.capabilities) <= 2 (ORCH-10). **Logic**: (1) Read current safety_band via state_port.read("control.safety_band"). (2) For each capability in envelope.capabilities: call fabric_port.query_registry(cap_name). If None -> return FAILED ("capability not found: {cap_name}"). If entry.safety_band_min > current_safety_band -> return FAILED ("safety band insufficient"). (3) Build CapabilityRequest per capability: `CapabilityRequest(capability_name=cap_name, params=envelope.params.get(cap_name, {}), tier=Tier.MEDIUM.value, caller="orchestrator", caller_id=envelope.envelope_id, trace_id=envelope.trace_id, timeout_ms=envelope.timeout_ms)`. (4) If 1 capability: result = await fabric_port.execute(req). If 2: results = await fabric_port.execute_batch([req1, req2]). (5) Wrap in StepResult(s), aggregate via aggregate(). (6) Return COMPLETED or DEGRADED. **ORCH-10 clarification** (per INV-3): max 2 distinct capabilities; retries within StepRunner are permitted. **MEDIUM is tool-only V1**: agent-type capabilities require HIGH tier (Concierge routes these to HIGH). **Error handling**: FabricGateway AdapterError(DEGRADED) -> step fails, other steps continue -> return DEGRADED. |
| 2.1.4 | Implement dispatch_high() | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def dispatch_high(self, envelope: TaskEnvelope) -> ProcessResult`. Returns COMPLETED immediately (plan request sent, execution happens later via event-driven flow). **Logic** (per ADR-1.1.12): (1) `snapshot = await state_port.snapshot(envelope.context.get("required_sections", []))`. (2) `request_id = str(uuid4())`. (3) `plan_request = PlanRequest(request_id=request_id, intent=envelope.intent, constraints=envelope.constraints, context=snapshot, trace_id=envelope.trace_id)`. (4) `ack = await planner_port.request_plan(plan_request)`. If ack.status == "REJECTED" -> return FAILED. (5) Check pending_plans size < max_pending_plans (50): if exceeded -> return FAILED ("too many pending plans"). (6) `self.pending_plans[request_id] = PendingPlanContext(request_id, envelope, snapshot, time.time(), 45000)`. (7) Emit PLAN_REQUESTED event. (8) Return DEFERRED (plan in progress, not complete yet). **receive_plan() signature**: `async def receive_plan(self, plan: CommittedPlan) -> ProcessResult`. **receive_plan() logic**: (1) `if plan.plan_id in self.executed_plans: log.warn("duplicate plan"); return COMPLETED` (RACE-3 dedup). (2) `ctx = self.pending_plans.pop(plan.request_id, None)`. If None: try WAL recovery via bridge_port.read_wal(plan.plan_id), if WAL found -> resume from WAL, else log orphan + return FAILED (RACE-2). (3) `self.executed_plans[plan.plan_id] = time.time()`. Trim if len > 100 (evict oldest). (4) `fresh_snapshot = await state_port.snapshot([])` (RACE-1: re-read to avoid 20-60s staleness). (5) `validation = await constraint_resolver.validate(plan, fresh_snapshot)`. If not valid -> return FAILED with validation.issues. (6) `await dag_executor.execute(plan, fresh_snapshot)` -> AggregatedResult. (7) aggregate() + emit. **receive_plan_failed(event)**: pop pending_plans[event.request_id], emit FAILED AggregatedResult. **receive_plan_cancelled(event)**: pop pending_plans[event.request_id], emit CANCELLED AggregatedResult. **Timeout reaper**: `async def _reap_stale_contexts(self)` -- runs every 5s (via asyncio.create_task in startup). For each pending_plans entry: if now - created_at > timeout_ms/1000, pop it, call planner_port.cancel_plan(), emit FAILED result. Same for pending_hil. **Two-phase clarification**: dispatch_high() is phase-1 (fire-and-forget plan request, returns DEFERRED). receive_plan() is phase-2 (event-driven, triggers on PLAN_READY). Tests must exercise both phases separately -- see 7.3.2 wiring for the scripted test pattern. **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §10.1-10.2 define the full Planner-Orchestrator request protocol including PlanRequest wire-format schema and the request_id echo contract (PlanRequest.request_id MUST appear in CommittedPlan.request_id). WB §10.4 specifies PlanAck within 500ms. Reference JSON Schema in 1.2.26. |
| 2.1.5 | Implement aggregate() | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `def aggregate(self, step_results: List[StepResult], plan_id: Optional[str], compensations: List[CompensationRecord], trace_id: str) -> AggregatedResult`. **Pure function** (no I/O, no side effects). **Logic**: (1) Count by status: completed = len([r for r in step_results if r.status == COMPLETED]), etc. (2) success = (failed == 0 and cancelled == 0). (3) Construct AggregatedResult with all fields. **V1 scope reduction**: token/cost aggregation steps removed (tokens_consumed, cost_usd, budget_utilization) -- no provider populates these; token_budget_max param removed from signature. **Delivery** (separate from aggregation): caller (dispatch_medium / receive_plan) handles: (a) `await delta_port.emit(ORCH_DAG_COMPLETED, result.to_dict(), trace_id)`, (b) `await bridge_port.submit_audit(result.to_dict(), trace_id)`. Both fire-and-forget. **Gotcha**: aggregate() is called for both MEDIUM (1-2 steps, no plan_id) and HIGH (N steps, with plan_id). Factory methods on AggregatedResult handle the difference (see 1.2.4). |
| 2.1.6 | Implement save_workflow() handler | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def save_workflow(self, request: WorkflowSaveRequest) -> ProcessResult`. **Logic**: (1) Lookup CommittedPlan by request.committed_plan_id -- if this was a recently-executed plan, it may be in local cache (DAGExecutor retains last N plans for save_workflow lookups) or WAL. If not found -> return FAILED ("plan not found; can only save recently-executed plans"). (2) Build WorkflowSpec: `WorkflowSpec(workflow_id=uuid4(), name=request.workflow_name, source_plan_id=request.committed_plan_id, version="1.0.0", trigger=request.trigger_spec, steps=plan.steps, dependencies=plan.dependencies, active=True)`. (3) `await workflow_engine.registry.save(spec)`. Duplicate name -> return FAILED ("workflow name already exists"). (4) `await workflow_engine.scheduler.register_trigger(spec.workflow_id, request.trigger_spec)`. (5) Emit k1.orchestration.workflow.saved delta with {workflow_id, name, trigger_type}. (6) Return COMPLETED. **Gotcha**: save_workflow does not re-validate the plan against current capability registry. The plan was valid when it ran; gap detection (4.2.7) handles future drift. |
| 2.1.7 | Implement ErrorRouter | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/error_router.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort)`. Stateless service -- no internal state. **Signature**: `async def route_error(self, error: AdapterError, context: Dict[str, Any]) -> ErrorAction`. **ErrorAction** (add to types.py): `@dataclass(frozen=True)` with fields: action ("RETRY", "FALLBACK", "DEGRADE", "ABORT"), retry_count (int, 0 for non-retry), fallback_value (Optional[Any]), reason (str). **Logic**: match error.severity: RECOVERABLE -> if error.adapter_name in {"mailbox", "delta_emit", "bridge_write", "event_sub"}: return ErrorAction("RETRY", 1, None, "transient error"). DEGRADED -> match adapter: "fabric_gateway" -> ErrorAction("DEGRADE", 0, partial_result, "Fabric step failed"), "planner" -> ErrorAction("FALLBACK", 0, None, "degrade HIGH to MEDIUM"), "state_read" -> ErrorAction("DEGRADE", 0, empty_snapshot, "stale context"). TERMINAL -> ErrorAction("ABORT", 0, None, error.error_message). **After routing**: emit k1.orchestration.error.routed event with {adapter: error.adapter_name, severity, action, trace_id}. **Gotcha**: ErrorRouter does NOT execute the action -- it decides and returns. The calling service (OrchestratorService, DAGExecutor, StepRunner) executes the ErrorAction. This keeps ErrorRouter testable and side-effect-free. **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §7 defines 4 circuit breakers (CB_PLANNER, CB_FABRIC, CB_MCP, CB_BRIDGE) with specific failure thresholds and reset timeouts. WB §7.5 provides the full error classification matrix mapping adapter errors to circuit breaker actions -- implement the severity-to-CB mapping table from WB §7. Reference CircuitBreakerConfig/State types in 1.2.23. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: All OrchestratorService code in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py`. ErrorRouter in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/error_router.py`.
> - **Constructor dependency chain** for `OrchestratorService.__init__()` (12 deps, wired by OrchestratorFactory step 15 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `mailbox: IMailboxPort` -- from `MailboxAdapter` (6.1.1) or `TestMailboxAdapter` (6.1.8)
>   2. `dag_executor: DAGExecutor` -- constructed at factory step 13, depends on fabric_port + planner_port + delta_port + state_port + bridge_port + step_runner + error_router + guards
>   3. `constraint_resolver: ConstraintResolver` -- constructed at factory step 14, depends on fabric_port + delta_port + event_port
>   4. `workflow_engine: WorkflowEngine` -- constructed at factory step 14 (after constraint_resolver), depends on storage_port + fabric_port + delta_port + state_port + bridge_port
>   5. `connector_lifecycle: ConnectorLifecycleManager` -- constructed at factory step 15 (before OrchestratorService), depends on fabric_port + delta_port + event_port
>   6. `error_router: ErrorRouter` -- constructed at factory step 1 (first, no deps except delta_port)
>   7. `concurrency_guard: ConcurrencyGuard` -- constructed at factory step 2 (no deps)
>   8. `fabric_port: IFabricGatewayPort` -- adapted at factory step 3-10
>   9. `planner_port: IPlannerPort` -- adapted at factory step 3-10
>   10. `state_port: IStateReadPort` -- adapted at factory step 3-10
>   11. `delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort` -- adapted at factory step 3-10
>   12. `bridge_port: IBridgeWritePort` -- adapted at factory step 3-10
> - **Message routing call chain** (`_mailbox_loop` -> `_process_one` -> `process`):
>   1. `_mailbox_loop()` (6.2.5): `msg = self.mailbox.dequeue()` -> `await self._process_one(msg)`
>   2. `_process_one()`: extract trace_id, check ConcurrencyGuard, `await self.process(msg)`
>   3. `process(msg)`: isinstance dispatch: `TaskEnvelope` -> `route_task()`, `CommittedPlan` -> `receive_plan()`, `WorkflowRunRequest` -> `dispatch_workflow()`, `WorkflowSaveRequest` -> `save_workflow()`, `InterruptRequest` -> `handle_interrupt()`
>   4. `route_task(envelope)` (2.1.2): validate -> emit TASK_ACCEPTED -> branch: MEDIUM -> `dispatch_medium()`, HIGH -> `dispatch_high()`
>   5. `dispatch_medium(envelope)` (2.1.3): read safety_band via `state_port.read()` -> query registry via `fabric_port.query_registry()` -> build CapReqs -> `fabric_port.execute()` or `execute_batch()` -> wrap in StepResults -> `aggregate()` -> emit + return
>   6. `dispatch_high(envelope)` (2.1.4): `state_port.snapshot()` -> build PlanRequest -> `planner_port.request_plan()` -> store PendingPlanContext -> return DEFERRED
>   7. `receive_plan(plan)` (2.1.4): dedup check -> pop PendingPlanContext -> `state_port.snapshot()` (fresh) -> `constraint_resolver.validate()` -> `dag_executor.execute()` -> `aggregate()` -> emit
> - **ErrorRouter integration** (2.1.7): `error_router.route_error(adapter_error, context)` called by `process()` catch block, `dispatch_medium()`, `dispatch_high()`, `receive_plan()`. Returns `ErrorAction` -- caller executes the action (RETRY -> re-enqueue, DEGRADE -> return degraded, ABORT -> return FAILED). ErrorRouter is stateless, side-effect-free, and emits diagnostic deltas via its own delta_port.
> - **save_workflow() call chain** (2.1.6): `save_workflow(request)` -> lookup plan in DAGExecutor cache or WAL -> build WorkflowSpec -> `workflow_engine.registry.save(spec)` -> `workflow_engine.scheduler.register_trigger()` -> emit workflow_saved delta.
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (all message types), Epic 1.4 ports (all 8 port interfaces), Epic 2.2 DAGExecutor (for `receive_plan`), Epic 2.3 StepRunner (via DAGExecutor), Epic 3.1 ConstraintResolver (for `receive_plan`), Epic 4.1-4.2 WorkflowEngine (for save_workflow/dispatch_workflow), Epic 5.1 ConnectorLifecycleManager (for MCP discovery + registration into Fabric).
> - **Consumed by**: `_mailbox_loop()` (6.2.5) calls `process()`. `init()`/`shutdown()` (6.2.2/6.2.3) manage lifecycle. Tests (7.1.1, 7.3.1-7.3.4, 7.3A.1-7.3A.7) exercise all message paths.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: OrchestratorService does NOT call `fabric_port.execute()` directly for HIGH tier -- that path goes through DAGExecutor. OrchestratorService calls `fabric_port.execute()` directly ONLY for MEDIUM tier (2.1.3). Do NOT add LLM calls. Do NOT write to SessionState. Do NOT bypass the mailbox for message delivery.

### Epic 2.2: DAGExecutor

**Goal**: Implement the topological DAG walker that executes CommittedPlan in parallel waves.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 2.2.1 | Implement DAG Engine (topological sort) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, fabric_port: IFabricGatewayPort, planner_port: IPlannerPort, delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort, state_port: IStateReadPort, bridge_port: IBridgeWritePort, step_runner: StepRunner, param_resolver: ParamResolver, error_router: ErrorRouter, guards: List[DAGGuard])`. guards is an ordered list of M3 extension guards (empty in M2, populated by OrchestratorFactory). **ParamResolver dep**: Constructor-injected `ParamResolver` instance (from `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver.py`, spec: `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver_spec.md`). Called by `execute_wave()` (2.2.2) to resolve `$`-references before dispatching steps to Fabric. See 2.3.5 for update details. **Internal state**: `self.interrupt_flag: bool = False`, `self.merged_results: Dict[str, CapabilityResult] = {}` (shared result store per SEM-4, keyed by step_id). **V1 scope reduction**: token_tracker and cost_accumulator REMOVED -- no provider populates cost/token metadata. **Method**: `def build_waves(self, steps: List[PlanStep], dependencies: Dict[str, List[str]]) -> List[Wave]`. **Algorithm** (Kahn's): (1) Compute in_degree for each step from dependencies dict. (2) Initialize queue with steps having in_degree=0. (3) While queue non-empty: pop all zero-in_degree steps -> form Wave(wave_index, steps, {}). Decrement in_degree of dependents. Add newly-zero steps to queue for next wave. (4) If steps remain after queue empty: cycle detected -> raise CycleError (should not happen if Planner validated, but defensive). **Validation**: len(waves) <= max_waves_per_plan (20). Total steps <= max_steps_per_plan (50). **Returns**: List[Wave] ordered by wave_index. **Gotcha**: build_waves is pure (no I/O) -- unit-testable with just PlanStep lists. The dependencies dict from CommittedPlan is used directly (not re-derived from PlanStep.deps) for O(1) lookup. |
| 2.2.2 | Implement Parallel Executor | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py | **Primary method**: `async def execute(self, plan: CommittedPlan, snapshot: ContextSnapshot) -> AggregatedResult`. Orchestrates full DAG execution. **Logic**: (1) Reset state: interrupt_flag=False, merged_results={}. (2) `waves = self.build_waves(plan.steps, plan.dependencies)`. (3) WAL write: `await bridge_port.write_wal(plan.plan_id, "PLAN_START", plan.to_dict(), plan.trace_id)`. (4) For each wave in waves: `result = await self.execute_wave(wave, plan, snapshot)`. Collect into wave_results. If abort condition -> break. (5) Run guards post-wave (M3 extensions, no-op in M2). (6) Return aggregate(). **execute_wave signature**: `async def execute_wave(self, wave: Wave, plan: CommittedPlan, snapshot: ContextSnapshot) -> WaveResult`. **execute_wave logic**: (1) Safety band re-read: `band = await state_port.read("control.safety_band")`. If band.data.get("level") in {"RED", "BLACK"}: abort DAG immediately (return partial). (2) Resolve params for all steps in wave via `self.param_resolver.resolve(step, self.merged_results)` (see `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver_spec.md` for resolution rules, `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver.py` for existing impl). For each step: `(resolved_params, resolved_cap) = param_resolver.resolve(step, merged_results)`. If resolved_cap is not None: override step.capability with resolved_cap (meta-agent dynamic resolution per Fabric Epic 4.5.9). Populate `wave.resolved_params[step.id] = resolved_params`. (3) Build tasks: for each step, `asyncio.create_task(self._execute_step(step, resolved_params, plan.trace_id))`. Max 10 concurrent (asyncio.Semaphore(10)). (4) As each task completes (asyncio.as_completed): check interrupt_flag after EACH step (per SPEC-5). If set: cancel remaining tasks, break. Store result in merged_results[step.id]. (5) WAL write: wave_completion_marker. (6) Emit delta_port progress. (7) Return WaveResult. **_execute_step**: delegates to step_runner.run(), updates merged_results, token_tracker, cost_accumulator. |
| 2.2.3 | Implement Result Aggregator (within DAG) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py | **Signature**: `def collect_results(self, wave_results: List[WaveResult], plan: CommittedPlan, compensations: List[CompensationRecord]) -> AggregatedResult`. **Logic**: (1) Flatten all StepResults from all WaveResults into single list. (2) For cancelled/skipped dependents (from 2.2.4), create synthetic StepResult(status=CANCELLED/SKIPPED). (3) Compute totals: compute duration_ms (wall clock from DAG start to finish). (4) Delegate to OrchestratorService.aggregate() or construct AggregatedResult directly (prefer AggregatedResult.from_dag factory). **Gotcha**: wave_results may be incomplete if DAG was aborted mid-execution (interrupt, safety band). collect_results must handle partial wave_results gracefully -- steps not in any WaveResult get status=CANCELLED. |
| 2.2.4 | Implement dependent step cancellation | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py | **Signature**: `def cancel_dependents(self, failed_step_id: str, plan: CommittedPlan) -> List[str]`. **Returns**: list of step_ids that were cancelled (transitive closure). **Algorithm**: (1) Build reverse dependency map: for each step, which steps depend on it. (2) BFS from failed_step_id through reverse deps. (3) Mark all reachable steps as CANCELLED. (4) But: steps with NO dependency on failed_step are INDEPENDENT -- they CONTINUE (ORCH-07). **Invariant (ORCH-07)**: "dependent cancel on parent failure". If step s2 depends on s1 and s1 fails: s2 is CANCELLED. If s3 is independent of s1: s3 still executes. **Integration with execute_wave**: after a step fails, call cancel_dependents(). Remove cancelled step_ids from future waves before dispatch. **Gotcha**: if failed step is in wave 1 and dependent step is in wave 3, the dependent must be removed from wave 3's step list before wave 3 executes. DAGExecutor must maintain a `cancelled_steps: Set[str]` and filter each wave's steps against it. |
| 2.2.5 | Implement Saga Recovery (compensation) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py | When side-effect step fails after earlier side-effect succeeded: execute compensations in **REVERSE DECLARATION ORDER** within the CommittedPlan (per INV-2: s3 before s2 before s1, regardless of actual completion timing -- deterministic, reproducible compensation sequences). Sequential, not concurrent (per ADR-1.1.8). **Compensation lookup chain** (per PROD-3): (1) PlanStep.compensation field (Planner pre-resolves from capability contract), (2) If null: IFabricGatewayPort.query_registry(cap_name).compensation_capability (runtime lookup), (3) If still null: no compensation available -- log + dead-letter. Each compensation creates CompensationRecord (1.2.13). If compensation fails: dead-letter + telemetry alert + CompensationRecord status=DEAD_LETTERED. Report compensation status in AggregatedResult. **Meta-agent note**: For meta-agent creation DAGs (build_agent -> execute_agent), if execute fails after build_agent succeeded, compensation may include agent deregistration -- defined in build_agent's capability contract compensation section. Orchestrator doesn't special-case this; it follows the contract. |
| 2.2.6 | Implement WAL Interaction Protocol | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py | Consolidates all WAL writes into a coherent protocol using IBridgeWritePort. **Write methods** (all async, fire-and-forget): (1) `await bridge_port.write_wal(dag_id, "PLAN_START", {"plan": plan.to_dict()}, trace_id)` -- before wave 0. Full plan persisted for recovery. (2) `await bridge_port.write_wal(dag_id, "STEP_COMPLETE", {"step_id": step.id, "status": result.status.value, "result_summary": result.result.data if result.result else None}, trace_id)` -- after each step in wave completes. (3) `await bridge_port.write_wal(dag_id, "WAVE_COMPLETE", {"wave_index": wave.wave_index, "completed_steps": [s.id for s in wave.steps]}, trace_id)` -- after wave finishes. (4) `await bridge_port.write_wal(dag_id, "DAG_COMPLETE", {"status": "COMPLETED"}, trace_id)` + `await bridge_port.submit_audit(run_manifest, trace_id)` -- final. **Recovery protocol** (6.2.4 crash_recovery reads WAL): `entries = await bridge_port.read_wal(dag_id)`. Parse entries: find last WAVE_COMPLETE -> resume from wave_index + 1. If STEP_COMPLETE entries exist after last WAVE_COMPLETE -> some steps in current wave completed, but wave didn't finish -> re-execute entire wave (idempotent steps assumed). If no entries -> DAG never started, re-execute from scratch. **V1 limitation**: WAL writes are fire-and-forget to K0. If K0 offline during DAG, WAL entries are lost, recovery impossible. This is acceptable for Edge-First (local execution continues; audit is best-effort). Document clearly. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: All DAGExecutor code in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/dag_executor.py`.
> - **Constructor dependency chain** for `DAGExecutor.__init__()` (8 deps, wired by OrchestratorFactory step 13 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `fabric_port: IFabricGatewayPort` -- for `execute_batch()` parallel step dispatch in waves
>   2. `planner_port: IPlannerPort` -- for micro-replan only (via MicroReplanCheckpoint guard)
>   3. `delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort` -- for progress deltas after each wave
>   4. `state_port: IStateReadPort` -- for safety_band re-read at wave boundary (ORCH-07)
>   5. `bridge_port: IBridgeWritePort` -- for WAL writes (PLAN_START, WAVE_COMPLETE, STEP_COMPLETE, DAG_COMPLETE)
>   6. `step_runner: StepRunner` -- constructed at factory step 11, depends on fabric_port + error_router + policies
>   7. `param_resolver: ParamResolver` -- constructed at factory step 12 (after StepRunner). `from k1.orchestrator.orchestration.param_resolver import ParamResolver`. Requires `RegistryPort` (satisfied by `CapabilityRegistry` via adapter or `None` for POC mode). **Reference files**: implementation `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver.py` (274 lines, Fabric 4.5.9 origin), spec `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver_spec.md` (319 lines). Update plan: issue 2.3.5.
>   8. `error_router: ErrorRouter` -- from factory step 1
>   9. `guards: List[DAGGuard]` -- ordered list from factory step 12: [OutputSchemaGuard, ConditionalEdgeEvaluator, MicroReplanCheckpoint, ExecutionMonitor]. **V1 removed**: TokenBudgetTracker, QualityGate, CostBudgetGuard.
> - **DAGExecutor is called by** `OrchestratorService.receive_plan()` (2.1.4) for HIGH tier and `OrchestratorService.dispatch_workflow()` for workflow execution. NEVER called directly by mailbox loop.
> - **Execution call chain** (`execute` -> `execute_wave` -> `_execute_step`):
>   1. `execute(plan, snapshot)` (2.2.2): reset state -> `build_waves(plan.steps, plan.dependencies)` -> WAL write PLAN_START via `bridge_port.write_wal()` -> for each wave: `execute_wave()` -> run post-wave guards -> `collect_results()` -> WAL write DAG_COMPLETE
>   2. `build_waves(steps, dependencies)` (2.2.1): Kahn's topological sort. Input: `plan.dependencies` dict (step_id -> [dep_ids]). Output: `List[Wave]`. Pure function, no I/O. Validates: no cycles (defensive against Planner), len(waves) <= 20, total steps <= 50.
>   3. `execute_wave(wave, plan, snapshot)` (2.2.2): (a) `state_port.read("control.safety_band")` -- abort if RED/BLACK, (b) `ParamResolver.resolve()` for all steps using `self.merged_results`, (c) `asyncio.Semaphore(10)` + `asyncio.create_task(self._execute_step())` per step, (d) `asyncio.as_completed()` -- check `self.interrupt_flag` after EACH step, (e) store results in `self.merged_results[step.id]`, (f) WAL write WAVE_COMPLETE
>   4. `_execute_step(step, resolved_params, trace_id)`: pre-step guards -> `step_runner.run(step, resolved_params, self.merged_results, trace_id)` -> post-step guards -> `self.merged_results[step.id] = result`
> - **Guard pipeline integration**: Guards from factory step 12 are called at specific points:
>   - `before_wave()`: ConditionalEdgeEvaluator (prunes skipped steps from wave)
>   - `after_step()`: OutputSchemaGuard (validate output), ExecutionMonitor (check interrupt_flag)
>   - `after_wave()`: MicroReplanCheckpoint (check discoveries), ExecutionMonitor (emit progress + optional override)
> - **Saga compensation** (2.2.5): On step failure for steps with `has_side_effects=True`: collect all COMPLETED steps with `has_side_effects=True` -> sort in REVERSE declaration order (LIFO per ADR-1.1.8) -> for each: lookup compensation capability (PlanStep.compensation field -> fallback to `fabric_port.query_registry().compensation_capability` -> fallback to dead-letter) -> `fabric_port.execute(compensation_request)` -> create `CompensationRecord`.
> - **WAL interaction** (2.2.6): 4 write points: (1) PLAN_START before wave 0, (2) STEP_COMPLETE after each step, (3) WAVE_COMPLETE after each wave, (4) DAG_COMPLETE + submit_audit at end. All fire-and-forget via `bridge_port.write_wal()`.
> - **Dependent cancellation** (2.2.4): `cancel_dependents(failed_step_id)` BFS through reverse dependency graph. Returns cancelled step_ids. DAGExecutor maintains `self._cancelled_steps: Set[str]` -- future waves filter steps against this set before dispatch.
> - **Shared state**: `self.merged_results: Dict[str, CapabilityResult]` (key=step_id) is read by ParamResolver (2.3.5), ConditionalEdgeEvaluator (3.2.2), MicroReplanCheckpoint (3.2.5). `self.interrupt_flag: bool` is set by `OrchestratorService.handle_interrupt()` and checked by ExecutionMonitor (3.2.6) after each step.
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (CommittedPlan, PlanStep, Wave, WaveResult, StepResult, CompensationRecord), Epic 1.4 ports (IFabricGatewayPort, IStateReadPort, IDeltaEmitPort, IBridgeWritePort), Epic 2.3 StepRunner, Epic 3.2 guards (all 7 + ConcurrencyGuard).
> - **Consumed by**: OrchestratorService.receive_plan() (2.1.4), OrchestratorService.dispatch_workflow(), CrossWorkflowResolver (4.2.5) for nested DAG execution. Tests: 7.1.2, 7.3.2, 7.3A.1, 7.3A.5.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: DAGExecutor does NOT dequeue from mailbox -- that is `_mailbox_loop()` (6.2.5). DAGExecutor does NOT call `planner_port.request_plan()` -- that is `dispatch_high()` (2.1.4). DAGExecutor calls `planner_port.micro_replan()` ONLY via MicroReplanCheckpoint guard (3.2.5). Do NOT add LLM calls. Do NOT write to SessionState.

### Epic 2.3: StepRunner + ParamResolver

**Goal**: Implement single-step execution with retry policy and parameter reference resolution.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 2.3.1 | Implement StepRunner | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/step_runner.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, fabric_port: IFabricGatewayPort, error_router: ErrorRouter, policies: OrchestratorPolicies)`. policies provides retry limits, timeouts from policies.contract.yaml (1.3.3). **Primary method**: `async def run(self, step: PlanStep, resolved_params: Dict[str, Any], prior_results: Dict[str, CapabilityResult], trace_id: str) -> StepResult`. **Logic**: (1) Build CapabilityRequest: `CapabilityRequest(capability_name=step.capability, params=resolved_params, prompt_template=step.prompt_template, tier=Tier.HIGH.value, caller="orchestrator", caller_id=step.id, trace_id=trace_id, plan_id=step.id, step_id=step.id, timeout_ms=step.timeout_ms or policies.step_timeout_default_ms)`. If step.tools_granted: set in request. (2) Execute with retry loop (2.3.2). (3) Wrap result: `StepResult(step_id=step.id, capability_name=step.capability, status=StepStatus.COMPLETED if result.success else StepStatus.FAILED, result=result, duration_ms=..., retry_attempts=attempts, schema_retry=..., error_detail=result.error.message if result.error else None)`. **Gotcha**: step.capability may contain "$" prefix for meta-agent dynamic resolution -- ParamResolver handles this BEFORE StepRunner sees the step (step.capability is already resolved by the time run() is called). |
| 2.3.2 | Implement retry policy | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/step_runner.py | **Signature**: `async def _execute_with_retry(self, request: CapabilityRequest, step: PlanStep) -> Tuple[CapabilityResult, int, bool]`. Returns (result, retry_count, schema_retried). **State machine**: normal_attempts=0, schema_retried=False. **Loop**: (1) `result = await fabric_port.execute(request)`. (2) If result.success: check output schema (2.3.3). If pass: return. (3) If not result.success and result.error.retriable and normal_attempts < policies.normal_retries (2): normal_attempts += 1, retry (same request). (4) If result.success but schema_invalid and not schema_retried: schema_retried=True, retry with schema_hint (2.3.3). (5) Else: return result as-is (FAILED or COMPLETED). **Retry delay**: 0ms (immediate, per ORCH-06). No exponential backoff for step retries -- Fabric handles provider-level backoff. **V1 scope reduction**: quality_retry removed -- no provider populates quality_score. Max 3 calls per step (2 normal + 1 schema). **Gotcha**: should_retry() must check that the error is retriable (not all Fabric errors are) -- AdapterError(TERMINAL) is not retriable. |
| 2.3.3 | Implement retry_with_schema_hint | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/step_runner.py | **Signature**: `def _build_schema_retry_request(self, original_request: CapabilityRequest, step: PlanStep, schema_result: SchemaResult) -> CapabilityRequest`. **Precondition**: step.output_schema is not None (ORCH-15). **Logic**: (1) Validate result against step.output_schema using jsonschema.validate or equivalent. (2) If valid -> return SchemaResult(valid=True). If invalid: build schema_hint string from validation errors (e.g. "Expected field 'summary' of type string, got integer"). (3) Create new CapabilityRequest as copy of original but with `params["__schema_hint"] = schema_hint` (reserved key, Fabric ContextBuilder extracts). (4) Mark schema_retry budgeted separately from normal retries: max 1 schema retry per step (policies.schema_retries). **Validation function**: `def validate_output_schema(self, result: CapabilityResult, schema: Dict[str, Any]) -> SchemaResult`. Uses step.output_schema as JSON Schema. Validates result.data against it. Returns SchemaResult with errors list and human-readable suggestion. **Gotcha**: only triggers if step.output_schema is set (most tool-type steps don't have it; agent steps with structured output do). Schema retry adds tokens to INV-1 accounting. |
| 2.3.4 | ~~Implement retry_with_quality_hint~~ | REMOVED | ~~k1/orchestrator/orchestration/step_runner.py~~ | **V1 REMOVED**: Quality retry consumed quality_score from CapabilityResult.data dict, but no Fabric provider populates this field. QualityGate (3.2.4) that triggered this retry is also removed. Re-add in V2 when LLM providers report quality metadata. |
| 2.3.5 | Update ParamResolver | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver.py | **Existing implementation**: `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver.py` (274 lines, created during Fabric Epic 4.5.9 meta-agent creation). **Spec**: `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver_spec.md` (319 lines, full implementation spec with class design, resolution logic, exceptions, test scenarios, and end-to-end meta-agent creation pattern). **Current state**: Implementation uses raw `dict` for step and `Dict[str, Any]` for completed_results with method `resolve_step()`. Already implements: (a) param resolution for `$step_id.result.path` refs in `step["params"]`, (b) capability resolution for `$`-refs in `step["capability"]` with registry existence check, (c) 3 custom exceptions (`StepReferenceError`, `PathResolutionError`, `UnresolvedCapabilityError`), (d) `RegistryPort` Protocol for capability existence checks. **Update required**: Align with Orchestrator typed API. Change signature from `resolve_step(step: Dict, completed_results: Dict) -> Dict` to `resolve(step: PlanStep, prior_results: Dict[str, CapabilityResult]) -> Tuple[Dict[str, Any], Optional[str]]`. Specific changes: (1) Accept `PlanStep` dataclass (1.2.18) instead of raw dict -- access `step.capability`, `step.params`, `step.id`. (2) Accept `Dict[str, CapabilityResult]` instead of `Dict[str, Any]` -- enables typed traversal of `.success`, `.data`. (3) Return `Tuple[resolved_params, resolved_capability_name]` -- separate capability resolution output from params. resolved_capability_name is `None` for static capabilities, `str` for dynamic meta-agent resolution. (4) Add recursive dict walking for nested params (current impl is flat-only per spec). (5) Keep `resolve_step()` as backward-compat alias during transition (remove in M7). **Logic preserved from existing impl**: `_resolve_reference()` path traversal ($.step_id.result.path), `"result"` keyword mapping to `CapabilityResult.data`, `success=False` check on referenced step, dict key + attribute traversal. **New logic from spec**: recursive nested dict param walking (V1: dot-navigation only, no array indexing). **Validation**: if referenced step status is FAILED/CANCELLED -> raise `StepReferenceError` (existing exception, defensive guard -- should be caught by dependent cancellation 2.2.4). If path doesn't exist in result.data -> raise `PathResolutionError` (existing exception). **Gotcha**: params can contain non-string values (ints, lists, dicts) -- only resolve string values. Non-string values pass through unchanged. **Test reference**: spec defines ~20 test scenarios in `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_param_resolver_459.py` covering param resolution, capability resolution, end-to-end meta-agent pattern, and edge cases. |
| 2.3.6 | ~~Implement CostAccumulator integration in StepRunner~~ | REMOVED | ~~k1/orchestrator/orchestration/step_runner.py~~ | **V1 REMOVED**: CostAccumulator integration consumed cost_usd from CapabilityResult.data dict, but no Fabric provider populates this field. CostAccumulator type (1.2.16) and CostBudgetGuard (3.2.8) are also removed. Re-add in V2 when LLM providers report cost metadata. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: `StepRunner` in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/step_runner.py`. `ParamResolver` in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/param_resolver.py`.
> - **Constructor dependency chain** for `StepRunner.__init__()` (3 deps, wired by OrchestratorFactory step 11 in 6.2.1):

> - **StepRunner is called by** `DAGExecutor._execute_step()` (2.2.2) per step in a wave. ParamResolver is called by `DAGExecutor.execute_wave()` (2.2.2) before step dispatch.

>   1. `run(step, resolved_params, prior_results, trace_id)` (2.3.1): build `CapabilityRequest` from PlanStep fields -> `_execute_with_retry()` -> wrap in `StepResult`
>   2. `_execute_with_retry(request, step)` (2.3.2): loop: `result = await fabric_port.execute(request)` -> if success: check OutputSchemaGuard decision -> if RETRY: rebuild request with hint -> retry. State machine: normal_attempts (max 2), schema_retried (max 1). Max 3 calls per step.
>   3. `_build_schema_retry_request()` (2.3.3): validates `result.data` against `step.output_schema` (JSON Schema Draft 2020-12) -> on failure: `params["__schema_hint"] = validation_error_message` -> re-execute
>   4. ~~`_build_quality_retry_request()` (2.3.4)~~: **V1 REMOVED** -- no quality_score data source.

>   2. `resolve(step, prior_results)` (2.3.5): deep-copy `step.params` -> walk all string values recursively -> for each `$<step_id>.result.<path>`: lookup `prior_results[step_id]` -> navigate path through `result.data` -> replace value. Also resolves dynamic capability name if `step.capability` starts with `$`. **Existing impl** already handles: `_resolve_reference()` path traversal, `"result"` keyword -> `.data` mapping, `success=False` check, `StepReferenceError`/`PathResolutionError`/`UnresolvedCapabilityError` exceptions, `RegistryPort` Protocol for registry checks.
>   3. Returns `(resolved_params, resolved_capability_name)`. resolved_capability_name is `None` for static capabilities.
> - **CostAccumulator integration** (2.3.6): **V1 REMOVED** -- no cost_usd data source. CostAccumulator type (1.2.16) removed.
> - **Token accounting** (INV-1): **V1 simplified** -- token budget tracking removed. StepRunner executes with retry (max 3 calls per step: 2 normal + 1 schema).
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (PlanStep, StepResult, CapabilityRequest, CapabilityResult, SchemaResult), Epic 1.4 ports (IFabricGatewayPort), Epic 2.1 ErrorRouter.
> - **Consumed by**: DAGExecutor._execute_step() (2.2.2). Tests: 7.1.3.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: StepRunner calls `fabric_port.execute()` (single step), NEVER `execute_batch()` (that is DAGExecutor's job for parallel wave dispatch). StepRunner does NOT check interrupt_flag -- that is ExecutionMonitor's job after each step. StepRunner does NOT write WAL -- that is DAGExecutor's job. ParamResolver is a pure function with no I/O -- it does NOT call any port.

## Milestone 3: Constraint Resolution + Adaptive DAG Extensions

> **Goal**: Implement pre-execution validation and the 6 agent-aware extensions that make the DAG adaptive (all deterministic, NO LLM).
>
> **Prerequisite**: M2 complete (basic DAG execution working).

### Epic 3.1: Constraint Resolution Engine

**Goal**: Pre-execution validation of CommittedPlan. Ensures all capabilities exist, params satisfiable, safety OK.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 3.1.1 | Implement ConstraintResolver | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/constraint_resolver.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, fabric: IFabricGatewayPort, delta: IDeltaEmitPort, events: IEventSubscriptionPort, max_cycles: int = 3)`. **Types**: `@dataclass ValidationResult: valid: bool, errors: list[str], warnings: list[str], alternatives_applied: list[AlternativeMapping], time_pressure: bool = False, hil_required: bool = False`. `@dataclass AlternativeMapping: original_capability: str, replacement_capability: str, reason: str`. **Primary method**: `async def validate(self, plan: CommittedPlan) -> ValidationResult` -- orchestrates: (1) check_capabilities() for all steps, (2) find_alternatives() for missing, (3) resolve_iteratively() for remaining issues, (4) compute time budget estimation. Checks: all capabilities exist in Fabric Registry, params satisfiable, safety band OK, time within budget. **Time budget estimation** (per BUDGET-1): For HIGH tier (10s target), sums estimated_duration_ms from capability contracts for critical-path steps. If sum > 10s: emit WARN + set time_pressure=true on ValidationResult. DAGExecutor uses flag to skip optional steps proactively. Estimates are rough (p50 latency from contracts); V1: advisory only, not a hard gate. **Gotchas**: (1) Called by DAGExecutor.execute() before DAG walk starts, NOT directly by OrchestratorService. (2) Must clone CommittedPlan before substituting alternatives (original preserved for audit). (3) If hil_required=true, DAGExecutor returns control to OrchestratorService for async HIL parking via PendingHILContext. |
| 3.1.2 | Implement capability availability check | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/constraint_resolver.py | **Signature**: `async def check_capabilities(self, steps: list[PlanStep]) -> list[CapabilityCheck]`. `@dataclass CapabilityCheck: step_id: str, capability: str, available: bool, contract_entry: Optional[RegistryEntry], alternatives: list[str]`. **Logic**: (1) Collect unique capability names across all steps (dedup -- a 10-step plan may reference same capability multiple times). (2) Batch query: `{cap: await self.fabric.query_registry(cap) for cap in unique_caps}` -- one query per unique name, not per step. (3) For each step: build CapabilityCheck with available = (registry_entry is not None). (4) For unavailable: populate alternatives list from find_alternatives(). **Return**: only CapabilityChecks where available=false OR where contract_entry shows safety_band_min mismatch. Empty list = all capabilities valid. **Gotcha**: Uses the 14-field PlanStep (from 1.2.17), not the Fabric 6-field PlanStep. Import from k1.orchestrator.types, not k1.fabric.types. |
| 3.1.3 | Implement find_alternatives() | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/constraint_resolver.py | **Signature**: `async def find_alternatives(self, missing_capability: str, step: PlanStep) -> list[AlternativeCapability]`. `@dataclass AlternativeCapability: capability_id: str, score: float, param_mapping: dict[str, str], safety_band: SafetyBand`. **Logic**: (1) Query Fabric Registry for all capabilities in same category (derived from naming convention: tool.{type}.{domain}.*). (2) Filter: candidate safety_band_min <= step.safety_band_min (same or stricter only). (3) Score candidates: schema_overlap_ratio (weight 0.6, how many input/output params match by name+type) + safety_band_match (0.2, exact=1.0, stricter=0.8) + name_similarity (0.2, normalized edit distance on capability_id). (4) Sort descending, return top 3. (5) Build param_mapping: {original_param: alternative_param} for auto-substitution. **V1 scope**: Same-category alternatives only. Cross-category suggestions deferred to V2. **Gotcha**: Filter out alternatives with score < 0.3 -- too noisy to suggest. If no alternatives found (empty list), caller falls through to iteration cycle 3 (HIL fallback). |
| 3.1.4 | Implement iteration controller | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/constraint_resolver.py | **Signature**: `async def resolve_iteratively(self, plan: CommittedPlan, initial_checks: list[CapabilityCheck]) -> ResolutionResult`. `@dataclass ResolutionResult: resolved: bool, modified_plan: Optional[CommittedPlan], unresolved: list[CapabilityCheck], hil_requested: bool, cycles_used: int`. **State machine** (max 3 cycles): **Cycle 1 (auto-resolve)**: For each unavailable capability with alternatives (score >= 0.3): clone plan, apply best-scored AlternativeCapability substitution, mark step as AUTO_RESOLVED. **Cycle 2 (re-validate)**: Run check_capabilities() on modified plan. Substitutions may invalidate downstream steps (e.g., alternative has different output schema, breaking param resolution). If new issues, attempt one more find_alternatives() round. **Cycle 3 (HIL fallback)**: If unresolved issues remain: set hil_requested=true, return ResolutionResult with remaining issues. Caller (validate()) invokes trigger_hil_fallback(). **Early termination**: (a) All checks pass -> resolved=true, exit. (b) No alternatives found in cycle 1 -> skip cycle 2, jump straight to cycle 3. (c) Cycle 2 introduces MORE issues than cycle 1 resolved -> abort auto-resolve, go to HIL. **Gotcha**: Each cycle MUST re-run full check_capabilities() -- substitutions cascade (alternative A requires capability B that conflicts with alternative C applied in same cycle). |
| 3.1.5 | Implement HIL fallback trigger | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/constraint_resolver.py | **Signature**: `async def trigger_hil_fallback(self, unresolved: list[CapabilityCheck], plan: CommittedPlan) -> None` -- non-blocking, parks state and returns. **Helpers**: `def format_constraint_question(unresolved) -> str` -- human-readable summary of missing capabilities + available alternatives. `def build_options(unresolved) -> list[dict]` -- list of {label, value} for each unresolvable step: "skip step" / "choose alternative X" / "cancel plan". **Async pattern** (per ARCH-3/ADR-1.1.12): (1) Build HILRequest: `HILRequest(request_id=uuid4(), question=format_constraint_question(unresolved), options=build_options(unresolved), timeout_ms=60000)`. (2) Emit via `delta_port.emit_hil_request(hil_request, trace_id)`. (3) Park state: `self.service.pending_hil[request_id] = PendingHILContext(dag_state=plan, question=hil_request.question, created_at=time.time(), timeout_ms=60000, default_action=GRACEFUL_FAIL)`. (4) Return to mailbox loop -- DO NOT BLOCK. **receive_hil_response()**: pops PendingHILContext, applies user's choice (skip steps / apply chosen alternative / cancel plan), then calls validate() again to verify substitution. **Timeout**: 60s. On timeout: graceful failure with explanation ("User did not respond to constraint question within 60s"). **Gotcha**: After HIL response, MUST re-run full validate() -- user's chosen alternative may introduce new constraint violations. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: All ConstraintResolver code in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/constraint_resolver.py`.
> - **Constructor dependency chain** for `ConstraintResolver.__init__()` (3 deps, wired by OrchestratorFactory step 14 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `fabric: IFabricGatewayPort` -- for `query_registry()` capability existence checks in `check_capabilities()` (3.1.2)
>   2. `delta: IDeltaEmitPort` -- for HIL fallback request emission in `trigger_hil_fallback()` (3.1.5)
>   3. `events: IEventSubscriptionPort` -- for HIL response subscription in `trigger_hil_fallback()` (3.1.5)
>   4. `max_cycles: int = 3` -- policy value from `policies.contract.yaml` (1.3.3)
> - **ConstraintResolver is called by** `DAGExecutor.execute()` (2.2.2) BEFORE the wave loop starts. Call: `validation = await self.constraint_resolver.validate(plan, fresh_snapshot)`. If `not validation.valid`: DAGExecutor returns FAILED immediately without executing any wave.
> - **Validation call chain** (`validate` -> `check_capabilities` -> `find_alternatives` -> `resolve_iteratively` -> optional `trigger_hil_fallback`):
>   1. `validate(plan, snapshot)` (3.1.1): orchestrates 4 sub-steps: `check_capabilities()` -> if issues: `find_alternatives()` -> `resolve_iteratively()` -> if still unresolved: `trigger_hil_fallback()`
>   2. `check_capabilities(steps)` (3.1.2): dedup capability names across all steps -> batch `fabric.query_registry()` per unique name -> build `CapabilityCheck` per step with available/safety_band status
>   3. `find_alternatives(missing_capability, step)` (3.1.3): query Fabric Registry for same-category capabilities -> filter by safety_band -> score (0.6 schema overlap + 0.2 safety match + 0.2 name similarity) -> return top 3
>   4. `resolve_iteratively(plan, checks)` (3.1.4): 3-cycle state machine: Cycle 1 auto-substitute best alternatives -> Cycle 2 re-validate substituted plan -> Cycle 3 HIL fallback if still unresolved
>   5. `trigger_hil_fallback(unresolved, plan)` (3.1.5): build HILRequest -> `delta.emit_hil_request()` -> park state in `service_ref.pending_hil[request_id]` as PendingHILContext -> return to mailbox loop (NON-BLOCKING)
> - **HIL async pattern**: ConstraintResolver does NOT block waiting for user response. It parks the full DAG state (plan + already-resolved alternatives + remaining issues) in PendingHILContext (1.2.19) and returns. Response arrives via `IEventSubscriptionPort` on topic `k1.hil.fallback_response.v1` -> handler in OrchestratorService pops PendingHILContext -> applies user choice -> re-runs `validate()` to verify new state.
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (CommittedPlan, PlanStep, ContextSnapshot, ValidationResult, CapabilityCheck, ResolutionResult, HILRequest, PendingHILContext, RegistryEntry), Epic 1.4 ports (IFabricGatewayPort, IDeltaEmitPort, IEventSubscriptionPort).
> - **Consumed by**: DAGExecutor.execute() (2.2.2) pre-execution validation. Tests: 7.1.4, 7.3.2 (HIGH tier e2e), 7.4.3 (ORCH-05, ORCH-06).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: ConstraintResolver does NOT execute steps. It does NOT call `fabric_port.execute()` -- only `query_registry()`. It does NOT modify CommittedPlan in-place -- it clones before substituting alternatives (original preserved for audit). The 3-cycle limit is a HARD gate -- do NOT add more cycles. Cycle 3 (HIL) is the last resort.

### Epic 3.2: Adaptive DAG Extensions (6 Guards)

**Goal**: Implement the 6 deterministic extensions that handle the non-deterministic nature of LLM agent steps.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 3.2.1 | Implement Output Schema Guard (ORCH-15) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/output_schema_guard.py | **DAGGuard Protocol** (shared by all guards): `class DAGGuard(ABC)` with three hooks: `async def before_wave(self, wave: Wave, ctx: DAGContext) -> GuardDecision`, `async def after_step(self, step: PlanStep, result: StepResult, ctx: DAGContext) -> GuardDecision`, `async def after_wave(self, wave: Wave, results: list[StepResult], ctx: DAGContext) -> GuardDecision`. Default implementations return PASS. `GuardDecision = Enum: PASS, RETRY, SKIP, FAIL, HARD_STOP`. `@dataclass DAGContext: merged_results: dict[str, CapabilityResult], interrupt_flag: bool, safety_band: SafetyBand, dag_id: str, trace_id: str`. **V1 scope reduction**: token_budget_max, tokens_consumed, cost_accumulator fields REMOVED -- no provider populates cost/token metadata; re-add in V2. Place DAGGuard + DAGContext + GuardDecision in k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/**init**.py. **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §8 defines the canonical guard pipeline (G0-G10) with guard names, phases (pre-dispatch/pre-wave/post-step), and Protocol interfaces. Guard ordering: G0=SchemaGuard, G1=TierGuard (pre-dispatch), G2=RateLimitGuard, G3=CircuitBreakerGuard, G4=ConcurrencyGuard (pre-dispatch), G7=TimeoutGuard, G8=MicroReplanCheckpoint (pre-wave), G9=OutputSchemaGuard (post-step). **V1 removed**: G5=TokenBudgetGuard, G6=CostBudgetGuard, G10=QualityGuard -- phantom field consumers with no data source. Reference guard types in 1.2.24. --- **OutputSchemaGuard** `class OutputSchemaGuard(DAGGuard)`: **Constructor**: `def __init__(self)` (stateless). **Overrides**: after_step only. **Logic**: (1) If step.output_schema is None: return PASS. (2) Validate result.data against step.output_schema via `jsonschema.validate()` (Draft 2020-12). (3) Valid: PASS, proceed to param resolution. (4) Invalid + first attempt: RETRY -- StepRunner sees RETRY decision and re-invokes with `__schema_hint` param containing the validation error message (per 2.3.3). (5) Invalid + already retried (step.retry_context.schema_retried == true): FAIL -- step marked FAILED, cancel_dependents() invoked. **Gotchas**: (1) Schema validation is per-step (after_step), not per-wave. (2) Import jsonschema as dev dependency -- must be in requirements.txt. (3) Large result.data objects: set jsonschema format_checker=None to avoid slow format validation. |
| 3.2.2 | Implement Conditional Edge Evaluator (ORCH-16) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/conditional_eval.py | `class ConditionalEdgeEvaluator(DAGGuard)`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self)` (stateless, pure evaluator). **Overrides**: before_wave only. **Signature**: `async def before_wave(self, wave: Wave, ctx: DAGContext) -> GuardDecision`. **Logic**: For each step in wave.steps: if step.conditions is empty -> dispatch (PASS). Otherwise evaluate each Condition against ctx.merged_results. `@dataclass Condition: left: str, op: str, right: str, combinator: str = "AND"`. **Condition language**: left = `step_id.status` or `step_id.result.json_path`; op in {"==", "!=", ">", "<", ">=", "<=", "contains"}; right = literal value (string, int, float, bool). Multiple conditions combined via AND/OR/NOT (parsed left-to-right, no precedence grouping in V1). **Evaluation**: resolve left by looking up ctx.merged_results[step_id], then drill into `.status` or `.result.data` via json_path. Compare with op. **Result**: FALSE -> mark step SKIPPED (remove from wave dispatch list). TRUE -> PASS. All FALSE -> empty wave -> skip entire wave. **Shared result store** (per SEM-4): ctx.merged_results is the same dict used by MicroReplanCheckpoint (3.2.5). Populated by DAGExecutor after each step completes. **Gotchas**: (1) Missing step_id in merged_results (not yet executed) -> condition evaluates to FALSE (conservative skip). (2) Empty conditions list = always dispatch (not always skip). |
| 3.2.3 | ~~Implement Token Budget Tracker (ORCH-14)~~ | REMOVED | ~~k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/token_tracker.py~~ | **V1 REMOVED**: TokenBudgetTracker consumed tokens_consumed from CapabilityResult.data dict, but no Fabric provider populates this field. ORCH-14 invariant deferred to V2. Re-add when LLM providers report token usage metadata in CapabilityResult. |
| 3.2.4 | ~~Implement Quality Gate~~ | REMOVED | ~~k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/quality_gate.py~~ | **V1 REMOVED**: QualityGate consumed quality_score from CapabilityResult.data dict, but no Fabric provider populates this field. quality_retry in StepRunner (2.3.4) also removed. Re-add in V2 when LLM providers report quality metadata. |
| 3.2.5 | Implement Micro-Replan Checkpoint (ORCH-13) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/micro_replan.py | `class MicroReplanCheckpoint(DAGGuard)`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, planner: IPlannerPort, max_replans: int = 1)`. **State**: `self.replans_used: int = 0` (reset per DAG). **Overrides**: after_wave only. **Signature**: `async def after_wave(self, wave: Wave, results: list[StepResult], ctx: DAGContext) -> GuardDecision`. **Logic**: (1) Collect all CapabilityResult.discoveries[] from wave results. (2) Heuristic: for each discovery, check if discovery.field overlaps any param name in remaining (unexecuted) steps. Overlap = substring match or exact match on field name. (3) If overlap found AND self.replans_used < self.max_replans: (a) Build MicroReplanRequest with discoveries, remaining_steps, ctx.merged_results. (b) `new_plan = await self.planner.micro_replan(request)` with 10s timeout (per PROTOCOL-3). (c) If new_plan received: replace remaining waves with build_waves(new_plan.steps), self.replans_used += 1, return PASS. (d) If timeout or None: log warning + return PASS (continue with original remaining plan). (4) If no overlap or max_replans reached: PASS. **Result scope** (per SEM-4): replacement waves have access to ctx.merged_results containing all prior step results. Replacement steps can reference prior outputs via ParamResolver without re-computation. **Gotchas**: (1) Micro-replan is synchronous (10s block) unlike dispatch_high() async pattern -- acceptable because DAG is already mid-execution. (2) Only 1 replan per DAG in V1. (3) Planner must handle MicroReplanRequest quickly -- it receives existing merged_results as context, NOT full intent re-planning. **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §10.6 defines the MicroReplanRequest sync round-trip protocol including completed_results wire format, remaining_steps non-empty constraint, and the 10s timeout contract. Reference JSON Schema in 1.2.27. |
| 3.2.6 | Implement Execution Monitor | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/execution_monitor.py | `class ExecutionMonitor(DAGGuard)`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, delta: IDeltaEmitPort, service_ref: OrchestratorService)`. service_ref needed to park PendingHILContext for override handling. **Overrides**: after_step + after_wave. **after_step** (per SPEC-5): `if ctx.interrupt_flag: return HARD_STOP`. Checks interrupt flag at **per-step completion** (not just wave boundary). Better UX: user waits max one step duration, not entire wave. **after_wave**: (1) Emit k1.hil.progress.v1 via delta: `await self.delta.emit_progress(wave.wave_index, summary_of_results, ctx.trace_id)`. (2) If wave has user-facing steps: optionally emit override opportunity with 30s timeout. **Override handling** (per ARCH-3): (a) `request_id = uuid4()`. (b) Emit override prompt via `delta.emit_hil_request(HILRequest(request_id, "Continue or cancel?", ["CONTINUE", "CANCEL_DAG"], 30000), trace_id)`. (c) Park: `service_ref.pending_hil[request_id] = PendingHILContext(...)`. (d) Return to mailbox loop. Response arrives via IEventSubscriptionPort. **CANCEL_DAG or CONTINUE** (per SEM-2): MODIFY_PARAMS removed from V1 -- dangerously underspecified; if user wants different params, they cancel and re-issue. **Timeout**: 30s. On timeout: CONTINUE (default -- user silence = proceed). **Gotcha**: Override emitting is optional per wave -- only emit when step count > 3 or wave duration > 5s to avoid spamming user with "Continue?" for every tiny wave. |
| 3.2.7 | Implement Sub-Step Observability Pass-Through | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/execution_monitor.py | **Not a DAGGuard** -- event subscriber managed by ExecutionMonitor lifecycle. `class SubStepObserver`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, events: IEventSubscriptionPort, delta: IDeltaEmitPort, rate_limit_ms: int = 500)`. **State**: `self.step_agent_map: dict[str, str] = {}` (step_id -> agent_id), `self.last_emit: dict[str, float] = {}` (step_id -> last emit timestamp). **Methods**: `def register_step_agent(self, step_id: str, agent_id: str) -> None` -- called by StepRunner when CapabilityResult returns agent_id. `async def start(self, dag_id: str) -> None` -- subscribes to fabric.agent.*.tool_call.* and fabric.agent.*.llm_call.* via events port. `async def stop(self) -> None` -- unsubscribes all. `async def on_sub_step_event(self, event: dict) -> None` -- callback: (1) extract agent_id from event topic, (2) reverse-lookup step_id from step_agent_map, (3) check rate limit: `if now - self.last_emit.get(step_id, 0) < rate_limit_ms / 1000: return` (drop), (4) enrich event with step_id, (5) forward via `delta.emit_progress(step_id, event["summary"], trace_id)`, (6) update self.last_emit[step_id]. **Rate limiting** (per ADR-1.1.11 Q11): max 1 sub-step event per step per 500ms. Prevents DeltaBus flooding during chatty agent executions. **Gotchas**: (1) Must be started/stopped with DAG lifecycle (DAGExecutor.execute() calls start() before and stop() after). (2) Pass-through only -- no transformation, no LLM. (3) If agent_id not in step_agent_map (agent spawned sub-agent), log and forward with step_id="unknown". |
| 3.2.8 | ~~Implement Cost Budget Guard~~ | REMOVED | ~~k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/cost_guard.py~~ | **V1 REMOVED**: CostBudgetGuard consumed cost_usd from CapabilityResult.data dict via CostAccumulator, but no Fabric provider populates this field. CostAccumulator type (1.2.16) also removed. Re-add in V2 when LLM providers report cost metadata. |
| 3.2.9 | Implement DAG Concurrency Guard | DONE | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/concurrency_guard.py | **Not a DAGGuard** -- checked in OrchestratorService.process() before dispatching to DAGExecutor, not during DAG walk. `class ConcurrencyGuard`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self)`. **State**: `self._lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()`, `self._active: bool = False`. **Methods**: `async def acquire(self) -> bool` -- attempts non-blocking lock acquisition: `if self._lock.locked(): return False`. If acquired: `self._active = True; return True`. `def release(self) -> None` -- `self._active = False; self._lock.release()`. `@property def active(self) -> bool` -- returns self._active. **Usage in OrchestratorService.process()**: Before dispatching to DAGExecutor (in route_task and dispatch_workflow): `if not await self.concurrency_guard.acquire(): re-enqueue message to mailbox with DEFERRED status; return DEFERRED`. **Release**: in finally block of DAGExecutor.execute() wrapper -- covers completion, failure, interruption. Enforces single-DAG-at-a-time (V1, per ADR-1.1.5). **Gotchas**: (1) Use asyncio.Lock not threading.Lock -- Orchestrator is single-threaded async. (2) Must release in finally -- unhandled exception during DAG must not permanently lock. (3) DEFERRED messages re-enter mailbox at BACKGROUND priority (don't starve REALTIME). |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: Each guard in its own file under `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/`. Package init: `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/guards/__init__.py` exports `DAGGuard` ABC, `DAGContext`, `GuardDecision`, and all 4 concrete guards + ConcurrencyGuard. V1 removed: TokenBudgetTracker, QualityGate, CostBudgetGuard (phantom field consumers).
> - **Guard base types** (defined in `guards/__init__.py`):
>   - `class DAGGuard(ABC)`: 3 hooks: `before_wave()`, `after_step()`, `after_wave()`. Default impl returns PASS for all.
>   - `@dataclass DAGContext`: shared context passed to all guards. Fields: `merged_results`, `interrupt_flag`, `safety_band`, `dag_id`, `trace_id`. **V1 removed**: `token_budget_max`, `tokens_consumed`, `cost_accumulator` (no provider populates cost/token metadata).
>   - `GuardDecision = Enum: PASS, RETRY, SKIP, FAIL, HARD_STOP`.
> - **Guard construction order** (OrchestratorFactory step 12 in 6.2.1, ORDER MATTERS):
>   1. `OutputSchemaGuard()` -- stateless, no deps. File: `guards/output_schema_guard.py` (3.2.1)
>   2. `ConditionalEdgeEvaluator()` -- stateless, no deps. File: `guards/conditional_eval.py` (3.2.2)
>   3. `MicroReplanCheckpoint(planner_port, max_replans=1)` -- needs planner_port. File: `guards/micro_replan.py` (3.2.5)
>   4. `ExecutionMonitor(delta_port, service_ref)` -- needs delta_port + OrchestratorService backreference. File: `guards/execution_monitor.py` (3.2.6)
>   - **V1 REMOVED**: TokenBudgetTracker (3.2.3), QualityGate (3.2.4), CostBudgetGuard (3.2.8) -- phantom field consumers with no data source.
> - **Guard execution by DAGExecutor** (hook call sites in `dag_executor.py`):
>   - **`before_wave(wave, ctx)`**: Called in `execute_wave()` BEFORE step dispatch. ConditionalEdgeEvaluator evaluates conditions and removes steps from wave (SKIP).
>   - **`after_step(step, result, ctx)`**: Called in `_execute_step()` AFTER `step_runner.run()` returns. OutputSchemaGuard validates output -> RETRY triggers re-execution with schema_hint via StepRunner (2.3.3). ExecutionMonitor checks interrupt_flag -> HARD_STOP if set.
>   - **`after_wave(wave, results, ctx)`**: Called in `execute()` AFTER wave completes. MicroReplanCheckpoint checks discoveries -> may call `planner_port.micro_replan()` (synchronous, 10s timeout). ExecutionMonitor emits progress delta + optional override.
> - **Guard decision handling by DAGExecutor**: PASS -> continue. RETRY -> `_execute_step()` re-invokes `step_runner.run()` with modified request. SKIP -> step marked SKIPPED, skip reason in `StepResult.error_detail`. FAIL -> step marked FAILED, trigger `cancel_dependents()` (2.2.4). HARD_STOP -> break wave loop, skip remaining waves, return partial results.
> - **ConcurrencyGuard** (3.2.9) is NOT a DAGGuard. It is checked in `OrchestratorService._process_one()` (6.2.5) BEFORE dispatching to DAGExecutor. File: `guards/concurrency_guard.py`. Uses `asyncio.Lock`. acquire() before DAG, release() in finally.
> - **SubStepObserver** (3.2.7) is NOT a DAGGuard. Managed by ExecutionMonitor lifecycle. File: `guards/execution_monitor.py` (same file). Subscribes to `fabric.agent.*.tool_call.*` and `fabric.agent.*.llm_call.*` via `IEventSubscriptionPort`. Rate limited: max 1 event per step per 500ms. Started by `DAGExecutor.execute()` before wave loop, stopped after.
> - **All guards are deterministic**: NO LLM calls. JSON Schema validation (OutputSchemaGuard), boolean evaluation (ConditionalEdgeEvaluator), substring heuristic (MicroReplanCheckpoint), flag check (ExecutionMonitor), lock (ConcurrencyGuard). **V1 removed**: counter arithmetic (TokenBudgetTracker, CostBudgetGuard), float comparison (QualityGate).
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (PlanStep, StepResult, Wave, CapabilityResult, DAGContext, GuardDecision, MicroReplanRequest, HILRequest, PendingHILContext, SchemaResult, ConditionExpr), Epic 1.4 ports (IPlannerPort for MicroReplan, IDeltaEmitPort for ExecutionMonitor, IEventSubscriptionPort for SubStepObserver). **V1 removed**: QualityDecision type, CostAccumulator type, IDeltaEmitPort usage by TokenBudget/CostBudget guards.
> - **Consumed by**: DAGExecutor (2.2.1-2.2.2) calls guard hooks. OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1 step 12) constructs ordered guard list. Tests: 7.2.1-7.2.8.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Guard construction order in factory step 12 defines execution order. Do NOT reorder guards without understanding cascading effects (e.g., OutputSchemaGuard must run before MicroReplanCheckpoint because schema failures should be retried first). Do NOT add new guards without extending the DAGGuard ABC. ConcurrencyGuard is NOT in the guard list -- it wraps the entry point, not the DAG walk. ExecutionMonitor's `service_ref` is a backreference to OrchestratorService for PendingHILContext storage -- this creates a circular dependency resolved by lazy init in OrchestratorFactory. **V1 removed guards**: TokenBudgetTracker, QualityGate, CostBudgetGuard -- phantom field consumers with no data source in Fabric CapabilityResult.
