## Milestone 4: Workflow System

> **Goal**: Implement the User Workflow System -- saved plans as scheduled/event-driven tasks.
>
> **Prerequisite**: M2 complete (DAG execution works).

### Epic 4.1: Workflow Registry + Compiler

**Goal**: Store and rehydrate frozen plans.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 4.1.1 | Implement WorkflowRegistry | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_registry.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, storage: IWorkflowStoragePort)`. Thin service layer over IWorkflowStoragePort. **Methods**: `async def save(self, spec: WorkflowSpec) -> str` -- validates WorkflowSpec (name non-empty, steps non-empty, trigger valid), calls storage.save_workflow(spec), returns workflow_id. Raises ValueError on duplicate name. `async def get(self, workflow_id: str) -> Optional[WorkflowSpec]` -- returns None if not found. `async def list_active(self) -> list[WorkflowSpec]` -- filters by active=True. `async def delete(self, workflow_id: str) -> None` -- soft-delete (sets active=False, preserves for audit). Hard-delete requires explicit `purge(workflow_id)`. `async def update_trigger(self, workflow_id: str, trigger: TriggerSpec) -> None` -- updates trigger for existing workflow, bumps version. `async def bump_version(self, workflow_id: str, new_steps: list[PlanStep], new_deps: dict) -> str` -- creates new version with updated steps/deps, sets as active version, returns new version string ("1.0.0" -> "2.0.0", monotonic major). **Version management**: Active version pointer per workflow via WorkflowVersionPointer (4.1.4). Previous versions retained for audit, not deleted. **Gotchas**: (1) Registry is stateless (no in-memory cache in V1) -- all reads go through IWorkflowStoragePort. Simple and correct; cache if perf issues arise. (2) save() and bump_version() are NOT idempotent -- calling save() twice with same name raises duplicate error. |
| 4.1.2 | Implement WorkflowSpec + TriggerSpec types | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_types.py | `@dataclass(frozen=True) WorkflowSpec: workflow_id: str, name: str, source_plan_id: str, version: str, trigger: TriggerSpec, steps: list[PlanStep], dependencies: dict[str, list[str]], active: bool = True, created_at: float = field(default_factory=time.time), updated_at: float = field(default_factory=time.time), created_by: str = "system"`. Validation in `__post_init__`: name non-empty, steps non-empty, version matches semver pattern. `@dataclass(frozen=True) TriggerSpec: type: TriggerType, schedule: Optional[str] = None, timezone: str = "UTC", event_topic: Optional[str] = None, enabled: bool = True`. `TriggerType = Enum: CRON, EVENT, MANUAL`. Validation: CRON requires schedule (croniter-parseable), EVENT requires event_topic, MANUAL requires neither. `@dataclass(frozen=True) DynamicExpr: raw: str, namespace: str, field: str, offset: Optional[str] = None`. Syntax: `${<namespace>.<field> [+/- <duration>]}`. V1 namespaces: date (today, now), user (timezone, locale). Extensible via resolver registry. Parser: `@staticmethod def parse(value: str) -> Optional[DynamicExpr]` -- returns None if not a dynamic expression (plain value). Regex: `\$\{(\w+)\.(\w+)(?:\s*([+-])\s*(\d+[dhms]))?\}`. **Gotchas**: (1) WorkflowSpec is frozen -- bump_version() creates new instance. (2) steps[] uses the 14-field Orchestrator PlanStep (1.2.17), not Fabric PlanStep. (3) DynamicExpr appears inside PlanStep.params values -- WorkflowCompiler resolves before execution. |
| 4.1.3 | Implement WorkflowCompiler | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_compiler.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, fabric: IFabricGatewayPort, delta: IDeltaEmitPort, storage: IWorkflowStoragePort, state_port: IStateReadPort, clock: SystemClock)`. **Primary method**: `async def compile(self, spec: WorkflowSpec) -> CompilationResult`. `@dataclass CompilationResult: success: bool, compiled_plan: Optional[CommittedPlan], gaps: list[ProactiveGap], auto_resolved: list[str]`. **Logic**: (1) Deep-copy spec.steps to avoid mutating frozen WorkflowSpec. (2) **Resolve DynamicExpr**: walk all step params, for each DynamicExpr.parse(value) that returns non-None: resolve via namespace resolvers -- date.today -> clock.utc_today(), date.now -> clock.utc_now(), user.timezone -> read from SessionState via state_port.read("user.preferences"), apply offset if present. (3) **Validate capabilities**: for each step, query Fabric Registry via fabric.query_registry(step.capability). (4) **Gap detection**: compare current capability contract against the contract at time of workflow creation (stored hash in WorkflowSpec). **Gap classification** (per SPEC-9): **SMALL** = new optional field added, field renamed (auto-map by name similarity > 0.8), field type widened (int -> float). Action: auto-fill default + emit notification, status=AUTO_RESOLVED. **LARGE** = required field added, field removed, type changed incompatibly, capability removed entirely, safety_band_min changed. Action: store ProactiveGap via storage.save_gap(), pause workflow (set active=false), emit HIL notification via delta. (5) Build CommittedPlan from resolved steps + deps. (6) Compute compiled_hash (SHA-256 of serialized plan) for RunManifest. **Gotchas**: (1) compile() is called at execution time (not at save time) -- ensures dynamic values are current. (2) If ALL gaps are SMALL and auto-resolved, execution proceeds. If ANY gap is LARGE, compilation fails. (3) DynamicExpr resolution must be idempotent -- calling compile() twice with same clock time produces same result. (4) state_port is used ONLY for user.timezone resolution in DynamicExpr -- no other reads needed during compile. |
| 4.1.4 | Implement WorkflowVersionPointer | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_registry.py | `@dataclass WorkflowVersionPointer: workflow_id: str, active_version: str, version_history: list[VersionEntry]`. `@dataclass VersionEntry: version: str, created_at: float, source_plan_id: str, step_count: int, change_summary: str`. **Logic**: embedded in WorkflowRegistry. `def bump(self, new_plan_id: str, new_steps: list[PlanStep], summary: str) -> str` -- increments major version ("1.0.0" -> "2.0.0"), appends VersionEntry to history, returns new version string. Version format: semver major-only (1.0.0, 2.0.0, ...) for simplicity in V1 -- minor/patch reserved for future partial updates. **Storage**: version_history stored as JSON array in WorkflowSpec.metadata or as separate column in SQLite workflows table. **Audit use**: WorkflowRunSupervisor records which version was executed in RunManifest.version. ProactiveGapDetector can compare current vs. previous version to detect drift. **Gotcha**: Version bumps are monotonic and append-only -- no rollback to previous version in V1. If user wants older version, they re-save the workflow from a new plan execution (creates new version with old-style steps). |
| 4.1.5 | Implement WorkflowPersistenceAdapter (SQLite) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/persistence/sqlite_adapter.py | `class SQLiteWorkflowAdapter(IWorkflowStoragePort)`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, db_path: str)`. Opens SQLite in WAL mode (`PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL`). Creates tables if not exist on init. **SQL Schema**: `CREATE TABLE workflows (workflow_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, name TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL, spec_json TEXT NOT NULL, active INTEGER DEFAULT 1, version TEXT NOT NULL, created_at REAL, updated_at REAL)`. `CREATE TABLE triggers (trigger_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, workflow_id TEXT REFERENCES workflows, type TEXT NOT NULL, schedule TEXT, timezone TEXT DEFAULT 'UTC', event_topic TEXT, enabled INTEGER DEFAULT 1, next_fire_time REAL, last_fire_time REAL)`. `CREATE TABLE runs (run_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, workflow_id TEXT REFERENCES workflows, version TEXT, compiled_hash TEXT, trigger_type TEXT, status TEXT, started_at REAL, completed_at REAL, result_json TEXT)`. `CREATE TABLE gaps (gap_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, workflow_id TEXT REFERENCES workflows, capability TEXT, gap_type TEXT, description TEXT, status TEXT DEFAULT 'PENDING', detected_at REAL, resolved_at REAL)`. **Migration**: version table (`schema_version INTEGER`). On init: check version, apply migrations sequentially. V1 schema = version 1. **Edge-First**: always available without K0 (per ADR-1.1.9). SQLite file in K1 data directory (configurable via config). **Serialization**: WorkflowSpec serialized to JSON via `json.dumps(asdict(spec))`, deserialized via `WorkflowSpec(**json.loads(row))`. PlanStep list serialized as nested JSON. **Gotchas**: (1) WAL mode enables concurrent reads during workflow execution. (2) Use `with self.conn:` for transaction safety. (3) All datetime stored as float (time.time()) not ISO string -- simpler, timezone-agnostic. |
| 4.1.6 | Implement IWorkflowStoragePort | DONE | k1/orchestrator/ports/workflow_storage_port.py | `class IWorkflowStoragePort(Protocol)`. **8th port** for Orchestrator hexagonal architecture (workflow-specific persistence). **All methods async** (SQLite adapter uses aiosqlite or run_in_executor). **Methods**: `async def save_workflow(self, spec: WorkflowSpec) -> None` -- upsert by workflow_id. `async def get_workflow(self, workflow_id: str) -> Optional[WorkflowSpec]`. `async def list_workflows(self, active_only: bool = True) -> list[WorkflowSpec]`. `async def delete_workflow(self, workflow_id: str) -> None` -- soft delete (set active=False). `async def save_trigger(self, workflow_id: str, trigger: TriggerSpec) -> None`. `async def get_due_triggers(self, now: float) -> list[tuple[str, TriggerSpec]]` -- returns (workflow_id, trigger) pairs where next_fire_time <= now AND enabled=True. `async def update_trigger_state(self, workflow_id: str, next_fire: float, last_fire: float) -> None`. `async def save_run(self, manifest: RunManifest) -> None`. `async def get_runs(self, workflow_id: str, limit: int = 10) -> list[RunManifest]` -- newest first. `async def save_gap(self, gap: ProactiveGap) -> None`. `async def get_pending_gaps(self) -> list[ProactiveGap]` -- status=PENDING. **Test adapter**: `class InMemoryWorkflowAdapter(IWorkflowStoragePort)` -- dict-based, created in M6. **Gotchas**: (1) get_due_triggers() is called every 1s by WorkflowScheduler tick -- must be efficient (index on next_fire_time). (2) Port does NOT define transaction boundaries -- adapter decides (SQLite uses connection-level transactions). |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: All workflow types in `k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_types.py` (4.1.2 + 4.1.4). WorkflowRegistry in `k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_registry.py` (4.1.1 + 4.1.4). WorkflowCompiler in `k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_compiler.py` (4.1.3). SQLite adapter in `k1/orchestrator/workflows/persistence/sqlite_adapter.py` (4.1.5). Port definition in `k1/orchestrator/ports/workflow_storage_port.py` (4.1.6).
> - **Constructor dependency chain for WorkflowRegistry** (OrchestratorFactory step 14 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `storage: IWorkflowStoragePort` -- for all persistence operations (save, get, list, delete, bump_version)
> - **Constructor dependency chain for WorkflowCompiler** (OrchestratorFactory step 14 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `fabric: IFabricGatewayPort` -- for `query_registry()` capability validation during compile (4.1.3 step 3)
>   2. `delta: IDeltaEmitPort` -- for emitting HIL notification when LARGE gap detected (4.1.3 step 4)
>   3. `storage: IWorkflowStoragePort` -- for `save_gap()` when LARGE gap detected (4.1.3 step 4)
>   4. `clock: SystemClock` -- for DynamicExpr resolution (date.today, date.now) (4.1.3 step 2)
> - **WorkflowSpec lifecycle call chain**:
>   1. User sends `WorkflowSaveRequest` -> `OrchestratorService.process()` (2.1.1) routes to `save_workflow()`
>   2. `save_workflow()` builds `WorkflowSpec` (4.1.2) from request + original `CommittedPlan`
>   3. `WorkflowRegistry.save(spec)` (4.1.1) validates spec -> calls `storage.save_workflow(spec)` (4.1.6)
>   4. On next scheduler tick: `WorkflowScheduler._tick()` (4.2.1) calls `storage.get_due_triggers(now)` (4.1.6)
>   5. Trigger fires -> `WorkflowRunSupervisor.start_run()` (4.2.3) calls `WorkflowCompiler.compile(spec)` (4.1.3)
>   6. `compile()` resolves DynamicExpr -> validates capabilities via `fabric.query_registry()` -> builds `CommittedPlan`
>   7. Compiled `CommittedPlan` fed to `DAGExecutor.execute()` (2.2.2) for standard wave-by-wave execution
> - **SQLite adapter** (4.1.5) is the **production** backend for IWorkflowStoragePort. `TestWorkflowStorageAdapter` (6.1.16) is the **test** backend (dict-based in-memory). Both implement the same Protocol.
> - **IWorkflowStoragePort** (4.1.6) is the **8th port** of the Orchestrator hexagonal architecture. Wired by OrchestratorFactory step 3 (production: `SQLiteWorkflowAdapter(db_path)`, testing: `TestWorkflowStorageAdapter()`).
> - **WorkflowVersionPointer** (4.1.4) is embedded IN WorkflowRegistry -- not a separate service. `registry.bump_version()` creates new `VersionEntry`, increments major semver, persists updated `WorkflowSpec` via `storage.save_workflow()`.
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (CommittedPlan, PlanStep, SafetyBand), Epic 1.4 ports (IFabricGatewayPort, IDeltaEmitPort, IWorkflowStoragePort), SystemClock (4.2.2).
> - **Consumed by**: WorkflowScheduler (4.2.1) queries registry + storage for due triggers. WorkflowRunSupervisor (4.2.3) calls compiler + registry. CrossWorkflowResolver (4.2.5) queries registry. ProactiveGapDetector (4.2.7) calls compiler for dry-run validation. Tests: 7.1.5, 7.3.3, 7.3A.3, 7.5.3.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: WorkflowRegistry is STATELESS (no in-memory cache) -- all reads go through IWorkflowStoragePort. WorkflowCompiler compile() is called at EXECUTION time (not save time) -- DynamicExpr resolves to current values. WorkflowSpec is FROZEN (immutable dataclass) -- bump_version() creates NEW instance. Do NOT add in-memory caching to WorkflowRegistry in V1.

### Epic 4.2: Workflow Scheduler + Supervisor + Cross-Workflow

**Goal**: Trigger workflows on schedule/events and manage execution lifecycle.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 4.2.1 | Implement WorkflowScheduler | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_scheduler.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, storage: IWorkflowStoragePort, mailbox: IMailboxPort, state_port: IStateReadPort, clock: SystemClock, tick_interval_s: float = 1.0)`. **Primary method**: `async def start(self) -> None` -- starts periodic tick loop via `asyncio.create_task(self._tick_loop())`. `async def stop(self) -> None` -- cancels tick task. `async def _tick_loop(self) -> None` -- `while True: await self._tick(); await asyncio.sleep(self.tick_interval_s)`. `async def _tick(self) -> None` -- (1) `now = self.clock.utc_now()`. (2) `due = await self.storage.get_due_triggers(now)`. (3) For each (workflow_id, trigger): build `WorkflowRunRequest(workflow_id=workflow_id, trigger_type=trigger.type, triggered_at=now, trace_id=uuid4())`. (4) Enqueue to mailbox with **priority=INTERACTIVE** (per SEM-3: REALTIME reserved for live user utterances; scheduled workflows use INTERACTIVE to avoid starving interactive tasks). (5) `await self.storage.update_trigger_state(workflow_id, next_fire=compute_next_fire(trigger, now), last_fire=now)`. **Cron evaluation**: `compute_next_fire(trigger, now) -> float` -- uses croniter library for CRON triggers; for EVENT triggers, next_fire=MAX_FLOAT (event-driven, no schedule); for MANUAL, next_fire=MAX_FLOAT. **User timezone**: reads from state_port.read("user.preferences") for timezone-aware cron evaluation. **Crash recovery** (per ADR-1.1.9): on startup, _tick() immediately checks for missed fires (next_fire_time < now). Fires immediately, then computes next. Prevents silent skips after restart. **Gotchas**: (1) tick_interval=1s means cron precision is +/- 1s (acceptable for V1). (2) get_due_triggers() must be indexed on next_fire_time in SQLite for O(log n) query. (3) If mailbox is full (MailboxFullError from enqueue), log error and retry on next tick (don't lose trigger). |
| 4.2.2 | Implement System Clock | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/system_clock.py | `class SystemClock`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self)` (no deps -- trivial utility). **Methods**: `def utc_now(self) -> float` -- `return time.time()`. `def utc_today(self) -> str` -- `return datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")`. `def device_local_time(self, timezone: str = "UTC") -> datetime` -- converts UTC to device-local timezone via `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo(timezone)`. `def utc_now_datetime(self) -> datetime` -- returns `datetime.now(timezone=timezone.utc)` for DynamicExpr date math. **Independently instantiated** (per SPEC-8): Orchestrator and Concierge each create their own SystemClock instance. Not shared via import or port (over-engineered for a clock). If extracting to shared location: k1/shared/system_clock.py. Otherwise duplicate is acceptable (< 20 lines). **Testability**: in tests, subclass with `class FrozenClock(SystemClock)` that returns fixed timestamps. Inject via constructor wherever SystemClock is used (WorkflowScheduler, WorkflowCompiler). **Gotcha**: Use `zoneinfo` (stdlib Python 3.9+), not `pytz`. If Python 3.8 support needed: add `backports.zoneinfo` to requirements. |
| 4.2.3 | Implement WorkflowRunSupervisor | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_supervisor.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, registry: WorkflowRegistry, compiler: WorkflowCompiler, storage: IWorkflowStoragePort, delta: IDeltaEmitPort, bridge: IBridgeWritePort)`. **Primary method**: `async def start_run(self, request: WorkflowRunRequest) -> RunManifest`. **Logic**: (1) `spec = await self.registry.get(request.workflow_id)`. If None or not active -> raise WorkflowNotFoundError. (2) **Concurrent run check** (per ADR-1.1.11 Q5): `active_runs = await self.storage.get_runs(request.workflow_id, limit=1)`. If latest run.status == RUNNING: abort it -- `await self._abort_run(active_run)` (compensate via Saga 2.2.5, emit ABORTED status), then proceed with new run. Single active run per workflow in V1. (3) `compilation = await self.compiler.compile(spec)`. If not compilation.success: store RunManifest(status=FAILED, gaps=compilation.gaps), return. (4) `manifest = RunManifest(run_id=uuid4(), workflow_id=spec.workflow_id, version=spec.version, compiled_hash=compilation.compiled_hash, trigger_type=request.trigger_type, status=RUNNING, started_at=time.time())`. (5) `await self.storage.save_run(manifest)`. (6) Return manifest + compiled_plan to OrchestratorService for DAG execution. (7) On DAG completion: update manifest status (COMPLETED/FAILED), set completed_at. **No-session result handling** (per PROD-4): `async def deliver_result(self, manifest: RunManifest, result: AggregatedResult) -> None` -- if session active (check via state_port): emit via delta_port. If no session (cron at 3 AM): persist via `bridge.submit_deferred_result(result)`. Concierge checks for pending results on next session start (documented in cross-component contract 1.3.4). **Gotchas**: (1) _abort_run() must be idempotent -- if DAG already completed between check and abort, abort is a no-op. (2) Manifest.compiled_hash enables dedup -- if hash unchanged from previous run, skip compilation (optimization, not correctness requirement). |
| 4.2.4 | Implement RunManifest | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/workflow_types.py | `@dataclass RunManifest: run_id: str, workflow_id: str, version: str, compiled_hash: str, trigger_type: str, status: RunStatus, started_at: float, completed_at: Optional[float] = None, result_summary: Optional[dict] = None, error_message: Optional[str] = None, steps_completed: int = 0, steps_total: int = 0`. `RunStatus = Enum: RUNNING, COMPLETED, FAILED, ABORTED`. **Factory**: `@staticmethod def create(workflow_id: str, version: str, compiled_hash: str, trigger_type: str, total_steps: int) -> RunManifest` -- sets run_id=uuid4(), status=RUNNING, started_at=time.time(). **Status transitions**: RUNNING -> COMPLETED (DAG finished successfully), RUNNING -> FAILED (DAG failed or compilation failed), RUNNING -> ABORTED (concurrent run policy aborted this run). No transition from terminal states (COMPLETED, FAILED, ABORTED are final). `def complete(self, result: AggregatedResult) -> RunManifest` -- returns new instance with status=COMPLETED, completed_at=time.time(), result_summary=result.to_summary_dict(). `def fail(self, error: str) -> RunManifest` -- returns new instance with status=FAILED, error_message=error. `def abort(self) -> RunManifest` -- returns new instance with status=ABORTED. **Gotcha**: RunManifest should be frozen (immutable) -- status transitions create new instances. Storage adapter handles upsert by run_id. |
| 4.2.5 | Implement CrossWorkflowResolver | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/cross_workflow_resolver.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, registry: WorkflowRegistry, compiler: WorkflowCompiler, depth_guard: WorkflowDepthGuard)`. **Primary method**: `async def resolve(self, step: PlanStep, parent_params: dict, current_depth: int) -> Optional[CommittedPlan]`. **Logic**: (1) Check if step.capability matches pattern `workflow.run.*` -- if not, return None (not a sub-workflow step). (2) Extract workflow_id from capability name: `workflow.run.{workflow_id}`. (3) `self.depth_guard.check(current_depth + 1)` -- raises MaxDepthError if > 3. (4) `spec = await self.registry.get(workflow_id)`. If None -> return None (WorkflowNotFoundError surfaced as step failure). (5) **Parameter override** (per ADR-1.1.11 Q6): merge params: `merged = {**spec.default_params(), **step.params, **parent_params.get(step.step_id, {})}`. Parent overrides take precedence, sub-workflow spec defaults fill gaps. (6) `compilation = await self.compiler.compile(spec)`. If gaps: propagate as step failure. (7) Return compilation.compiled_plan with merged params applied. **Integration with DAGExecutor**: StepRunner detects `workflow.run.*` capability, delegates to CrossWorkflowResolver instead of IFabricGatewayPort. Resolved CommittedPlan is executed recursively by a nested DAGExecutor.execute() call. **Gotchas**: (1) Recursive DAGExecutor calls share the ConcurrencyGuard -- sub-workflow does NOT acquire a second lock (already inside DAG execution context). (2) Sub-workflow results are wrapped in a single StepResult for the parent DAG. (3) Cycle detection is pre-validated by WorkflowDepthGuard (4.2.6) at resolve time, not at save time. |
| 4.2.6 | Implement WorkflowDepthGuard | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/cross_workflow_resolver.py | `class WorkflowDepthGuard`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, max_depth: int = 3)`. **Methods**: `def check(self, current_depth: int) -> None` -- raises `MaxDepthError(f"Workflow nesting depth {current_depth} exceeds max {self.max_depth}")` if current_depth > max_depth (ORCH-08). `def detect_cycle(self, workflow_id: str, ancestor_ids: set[str]) -> None` -- raises `WorkflowCycleError` if workflow_id in ancestor_ids. Called by CrossWorkflowResolver before resolve(). **Depth tracking**: CrossWorkflowResolver passes current_depth through recursive calls. Root workflow = depth 1. Each sub-workflow increments by 1. Max 3 means: root -> sub -> sub-sub (3 levels). **~~Token budget isolation~~** (per BUDGET-2): ~~Each workflow (including sub-workflows) gets its own independent token budget from its CommittedPlan.token_budget_max. No cross-workflow token sharing in V1. TokenBudgetTracker is instantiated per DAGExecutor.execute() call (one per workflow level). Simplifies reasoning, prevents parent starvation from chatty sub-workflow. Future: optional shared-pool mode.~~ **V1 DEFERRED** -- TokenBudgetTracker removed; token budget isolation deferred to V2 when providers report token usage. **Gotchas**: (1) Cycle detection requires ancestor_ids propagation through resolve() chain -- CrossWorkflowResolver maintains `visited: set[str]` per resolution chain. (2) MaxDepthError and WorkflowCycleError are both subclasses of AdapterError(TERMINAL) -- non-recoverable. (3) Depth guard is separate from ConcurrencyGuard -- depth limits nesting, concurrency limits parallelism. |
| 4.2.7 | Implement ProactiveGapDetector | DONE | k1/orchestrator/workflows/gap_detector.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, registry: WorkflowRegistry, compiler: WorkflowCompiler, storage: IWorkflowStoragePort, events: IEventSubscriptionPort, delta: IDeltaEmitPort)`. **Startup**: `async def start(self) -> None` -- subscribes to k1.fabric.capability.contract_updated.v1 via events port: `await self.events.subscribe("k1.fabric.capability.contract_updated.v1", self._on_contract_updated)`. **Handler**: `async def _on_contract_updated(self, event: dict) -> None` -- (1) Extract capability_name from event. (2) `affected = [spec for spec in await self.registry.list_active() if any(s.capability == capability_name for s in spec.steps)]`. (3) For each affected WorkflowSpec: run `self.compiler.compile(spec)` in dry-run mode (validate only, don't build CommittedPlan). (4) Classify gaps per SPEC-9 criteria (same as WorkflowCompiler 4.1.3): **SMALL** gaps -> auto-fill defaults + emit notification via delta: `await self.delta.emit("k1.orchestration.gap.auto_resolved", {workflow_id, capability, change_summary}, trace_id)`. Status=AUTO_RESOLVED. **LARGE** gaps -> `await self.storage.save_gap(ProactiveGap(gap_id=uuid4(), workflow_id=spec.workflow_id, capability=capability_name, gap_type="LARGE", description=..., status="PENDING", detected_at=time.time()))`. Pause workflow: `spec.active = False`, save. Surface to user when free via `delta.emit("k1.orchestration.gap.detected", {...}, trace_id)`. (5) Follows K0 P06 curiosity pattern: proactive detection, non-blocking, user surfaces at convenient time. **Gotchas**: (1) `_on_contract_updated` may fire frequently during K0 sync -- debounce: if same capability updated within 5s window, process only latest. (2) Dry-run compilation must NOT modify stored WorkflowSpecs -- use deep copy. (3) If no active workflows reference the updated capability, handler is a no-op (fast path). |

>   2. `mailbox: IMailboxPort` -- for enqueuing `WorkflowRunRequest` when trigger fires (4.2.1)

> - **Constructor dependency chain for WorkflowRunSupervisor** (OrchestratorFactory step 14 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `registry: WorkflowRegistry` -- for `get(workflow_id)` (4.1.1)
>   2. `compiler: WorkflowCompiler` -- for `compile(spec)` (4.1.3)
>   3. `storage: IWorkflowStoragePort` -- for `save_run()`, `get_runs()` (4.1.6)
>   4. `delta: IDeltaEmitPort` -- for result delivery when session active
>   5. `bridge: IBridgeWritePort` -- for `submit_deferred_result()` when no session (PROD-4)
> - **Constructor dependency chain for CrossWorkflowResolver** (OrchestratorFactory step 14 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `registry: WorkflowRegistry` -- for `get(workflow_id)` to load sub-workflow spec (4.1.1)
>   2. `compiler: WorkflowCompiler` -- for `compile(spec)` to compile sub-workflow plan (4.1.3)
>   3. `depth_guard: WorkflowDepthGuard(max_depth=3)` -- for `check()` and `detect_cycle()` (4.2.6)
> - **Scheduler tick -> execution call chain**:
>   1. `WorkflowScheduler._tick()` (4.2.1): calls `storage.get_due_triggers(clock.utc_now())`
>   2. For each due trigger: builds `WorkflowRunRequest(workflow_id, trigger_type, triggered_at, trace_id)`
>   3. Enqueues to `mailbox.enqueue(request, priority=INTERACTIVE)` -- NOT REALTIME (REALTIME reserved for live user)
>   4. Updates trigger state: `storage.update_trigger_state(wf_id, next_fire=compute_next_fire(), last_fire=now)`
>   5. OrchestratorService._mailbox_loop() dequeues -> `_process_one()` routes to `execute_workflow()`
>   6. `execute_workflow()` calls `WorkflowRunSupervisor.start_run(request)` (4.2.3)
>   7. Supervisor: concurrent run check -> `compiler.compile(spec)` -> build `RunManifest` -> `storage.save_run(manifest)`
>   8. Returns compiled `CommittedPlan` to OrchestratorService -> feeds into `DAGExecutor.execute()`
> - **CrossWorkflowResolver integration with StepRunner** (2.3.3):
>   1. `StepRunner.run(step)` checks if `step.capability` matches pattern `workflow.run.*`
>   2. If match: delegates to `CrossWorkflowResolver.resolve(step, parent_params, current_depth)`
>   3. Resolver: `depth_guard.check(current_depth + 1)` -> `registry.get(workflow_id)` -> `compiler.compile(spec)`
>   4. Returns compiled `CommittedPlan` -> StepRunner creates nested `DAGExecutor.execute()` call
>   5. Nested DAGExecutor shares ConcurrencyGuard (already inside DAG context -- NO second lock acquisition)
>   6. Sub-workflow results wrapped as single `StepResult` for parent DAG
> - **ProactiveGapDetector event-driven lifecycle**:
>   1. On `init()` (6.2.2 step 8): subscribes to `k1.fabric.capability.contract_updated.v1` via `IEventSubscriptionPort`
>   2. On event: `_on_contract_updated()` -> query `registry.list_active()` for affected workflows
>   3. For each affected: dry-run `compiler.compile(spec)` -> classify gaps per SPEC-9
>   4. SMALL gap: auto-resolve + emit notification. LARGE gap: save gap + pause workflow + emit HIL notification
>   5. Debounce: same capability updated within 5s window -> process only latest (prevents K0 sync flood)
> - **Concurrent run policy** (per ADR-1.1.11 Q5): `WorkflowRunSupervisor.start_run()` checks `storage.get_runs(wf_id, limit=1)`. If latest run status == RUNNING: `_abort_run(active_run)` triggers saga compensation (2.2.5) for in-flight steps, emits ABORTED status, then proceeds with new run.
> - **No-session result handling** (PROD-4): `WorkflowRunSupervisor.deliver_result()` checks session status via `state_port`. If session active: emit via `delta_port`. If no session (cron at 3 AM): persist via `bridge.submit_deferred_result()`. Concierge checks on next session start.
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.2 types (CommittedPlan, PlanStep, TaskEnvelope, WorkflowRunRequest, AggregatedResult, CompensationRecord, RunManifest, WorkflowSpec, TriggerSpec, DynamicExpr, RunStatus, ProactiveGap), Epic 1.4 ports (all 8 ports used collectively by workflow subsystems), WorkflowRegistry + Compiler (4.1.1 + 4.1.3), SystemClock (4.2.2).
> - **Consumed by**: OrchestratorService `execute_workflow()` (2.1.1, message routing). StepRunner (2.3.3) for cross-workflow detection. OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1 step 14) constructs all workflow components. Tests: 7.1.5, 7.3.3, 7.3.4, 7.3A.3, 7.3A.6, 9.1.3, 9.2.5.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: WorkflowScheduler enqueues at INTERACTIVE priority, NOT REALTIME. CrossWorkflowResolver does NOT acquire a second ConcurrencyGuard lock -- sub-workflows execute inside the parent's guard scope. ProactiveGapDetector dry-run compile() does NOT modify stored WorkflowSpecs -- uses deep copy. RunManifest is FROZEN -- status transitions create new instances (like WorkflowSpec). SystemClock is trivially instantiated (no port, no injection) -- WorkflowFactory creates it directly. WorkflowDepthGuard max_depth=3 is a HARD gate -- do NOT increase without ADR update.

---

## Milestone 5: Connector Lifecycle (Thin Discovery Layer)

> **Goal**: Discover MCP servers from config and register their tools into Fabric. Orchestrator does NOT own provider creation, execution, health monitoring, sandboxing, circuit breakers, or output validation -- Fabric owns ALL of that (see Fabric M3: MCPProvider 3.3.2, ProviderFactory 3.1.4, CircuitBreaker 3.4.1-3.4.3, HealthChecker 3.6.1, AvailabilityTracker 3.6.2, OutputValidationPipeline 3.5.1-3.5.5).
>
> **Prerequisite**: M2 complete (Fabric integration working).
>
> **Fabric Redundancy Audit (resolved)**:
>
> The original M5 (7 issues, ~35 tests) duplicated massive chunks of Fabric:
>
> | Original Issue | What It Did | Already In Fabric | Resolution |
> |---------------|-------------|-------------------|------------|
> | 5.1.3 MCPProviderFactory | Created LocalMCPProvider/RemoteMCPProvider | MCPProvider (3.3.2) handles stdio/SSE/HTTP. ProviderFactory (3.1.4) instantiates all provider types. | **REMOVED** -- Fabric owns provider creation. Registration via register_from_dict() (2.3.4) creates ProviderConfig that Fabric's ProviderFactory uses. |
> | 5.1.4 ConnectorSandbox | Timeout, output size, schema validation | CircuitBreaker (3.4.1) wraps every provider call with per-type timeout. OutputValidationPipeline (3.5.1-3.5.5) validates structural + schema + semantic. | **REMOVED** -- Fabric owns execution safety. |
> | 5.1.6 MCPReDiscoveryService (health portion) | CB state monitoring, active heartbeat | HealthChecker (3.6.1) does periodic health checks. AvailabilityTracker (3.6.2) tracks ONLINE/DEGRADED/OFFLINE. Bidirectional CB wiring (3.6.3). | **SLIMMED** -- Orchestrator only subscribes to Fabric's k1.fabric.provider.health.changed.v1 to trigger re-discovery. No health monitoring, no CB tracking, no heartbeat (Fabric owns all of that). |
> | 5.1.5 ConnectorManager (provider storage) | Stored dict[str, MCPProvider] for direct lookup | ProviderRegistry (3.1.1) maps provider_id to ProviderConfig. Fabric resolve() handles all lookups. | **SLIMMED** -- ConnectorManager tracks server_id -> list[capability_id] mapping for unregister/refresh operations only. No provider instances stored. |
>
> **Remaining scope** (4 issues, ~20 tests): Discovery (genuinely new), Registration bridge (thin translation), K0 Proxy (genuinely new), Lifecycle manager (thin orchestration).

### Epic 5.1: MCP Discovery & Registration

**Goal**: Discover MCP servers from config and register their tools into Fabric's registry via `register_from_dict()`. All execution, health, timeout, sandboxing, and circuit breaker concerns belong to Fabric.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 5.1.1 | Implement MCP Tool Discovery | DONE | k1/orchestrator/connectors/mcp_discovery.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, config_path: str = "k1/connectors/mcp_servers.yaml")`. **Types**: `@dataclass MCPServerConfig: id: str, type: str, endpoint: str, critical: bool = False` (type in {"local", "remote"}). `@dataclass DiscoveredTool: server_id: str, name: str, description: str, input_schema: dict, output_schema: Optional[dict]`. **Methods**: `async def discover_all(self) -> list[DiscoveredTool]` -- reads config, iterates servers, calls `discover_server()` per entry, returns flat list. `async def discover_server(self, server: MCPServerConfig) -> list[DiscoveredTool]` -- (1) Local (stdio): spawn subprocess, send `tools/list` JSON-RPC, parse response. (2) Remote (SSE/HTTP): POST to `{endpoint}/tools/list`, parse response. (3) For each tool in response: build DiscoveredTool with server_id, name, schemas extracted from MCP tool definition. (4) On error: log warning, return empty list for that server (don't fail entire discovery). `def load_config(self) -> list[MCPServerConfig]` -- reads YAML, validates schema. **Discovery source** (per SPEC-6): V1 reads static config file. V2: dynamic registration API. Config format: `servers: [{id, type, endpoint, critical}]`. **Gotchas**: (1) Local stdio discovery must have subprocess timeout (5s per server). (2) Remote discovery must have HTTP timeout (10s). (3) Discovery is idempotent -- calling twice returns same tool set (unless server changed). (4) YAML config is NOT hot-reloaded in V1. Re-discovery re-reads config on each cycle. |
| 5.1.2 | Implement MCP Registration Bridge | DONE | k1/orchestrator/connectors/mcp_registrar.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, fabric: IFabricGatewayPort, delta: IDeltaEmitPort)`. **Types**: `@dataclass RegistrationResult: registered: int, skipped: int, errors: list[str]`. **Primary method**: `async def register_tools(self, tools: list[DiscoveredTool]) -> RegistrationResult`. **Logic**: (1) For each DiscoveredTool: build capability_id = `tool.{infer_type(tool)}.{tool.server_id}.{tool.name}` (per SPEC-7: server_id prefix prevents naming collisions). (2) Check Fabric Registry via `fabric.query_registry(capability_id)`: if exists -> skip (collision), log warning, increment skipped count. (3) Build contract_dict compatible with Fabric's `register_from_dict()` (2.3.4): `{"name": capability_id, "version": "1.0.0", "description": tool.description, "input": tool.input_schema, "output": tool.output_schema, "provider_type": "MCP", "provider_config": {"endpoint": server.endpoint, "transport": server.type, "server_id": tool.server_id, "tool_name": tool.name}, "safety_band_min": "GREEN", "domain": ["mcp", tool.server_id]}`. (4) Emit `k1.orchestration.mcp.tool_registered.v1` via delta_port -- Fabric subscribes to this event (Fabric 5.4.4) and calls `register_from_dict(contract_dict)` internally. Fabric then creates the MCPProvider instance via its own ProviderFactory. (5) Return RegistrationResult. **infer_type(tool)**: heuristic from tool.name pattern -- "query"/"search"/"list"/"get" prefix -> "read", "create"/"update"/"delete" -> "execute", default -> "execute". **Naming**: `tool.{type}.{server_id}.{name}` e.g., tool.execute.google_cal.create_event. **What this does NOT do** (Fabric owns these): No provider creation. No sandbox wrapping. No circuit breaker setup. No health monitoring. No output validation. Fabric handles all of that when it processes the register_from_dict() call. **Gotchas**: (1) On collision: reject duplicate, keep first. (2) Registration is event-driven (not direct Fabric API call) for hexagonal separation. (3) Unregister before re-register if server tool schema changed (handled by ConnectorLifecycleManager.refresh). |
| 5.1.3 | Implement ConnectorLifecycleManager | DONE | k1/orchestrator/connectors/connector_lifecycle.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, discovery: MCPToolDiscovery, registrar: MCPRegistrationBridge, events: IEventSubscriptionPort, delta: IDeltaEmitPort)`. **State**: `self.server_capabilities: dict[str, list[str]] = {}` (server_id -> list of registered capability_ids -- for unregister/refresh tracking). No provider instances stored (Fabric owns those). No health state tracked (Fabric owns that via HealthChecker + AvailabilityTracker). **Methods**: `async def discover_and_register(self) -> RegistrationResult` -- orchestrates: `tools = await self.discovery.discover_all()` -> `result = await self.registrar.register_tools(tools)` -> update self.server_capabilities mapping -> return result. `async def start_lifecycle_monitoring(self) -> None` -- subscribe to `k1.fabric.provider.health.changed.v1` (Fabric's event, NOT a custom Orchestrator event). On provider recovery (status OFFLINE -> ONLINE for MCP-type provider): trigger `await self.refresh(server_id)` to re-discover tools (server may have new/removed tools after recovery). `async def refresh(self, server_id: str) -> None` -- (1) Get old capability_ids from self.server_capabilities[server_id]. (2) For each old_cap: emit unregister event (`k1.orchestration.mcp.tool_unregistered.v1`). (3) Re-discover: `tools = await self.discovery.discover_server(server_config)`. (4) Re-register: `await self.registrar.register_tools(tools)`. (5) Update self.server_capabilities[server_id]. Active workflows using removed tools fail at ConstraintResolver -> ProactiveGap. `async def unregister_server(self, server_id: str) -> None` -- emit unregister events for all capabilities of server. Remove from self.server_capabilities. **Gotchas**: (1) ConnectorLifecycleManager is the ONLY Orchestrator entry point for MCP operations. (2) It does NOT store provider instances, does NOT track health, does NOT do circuit breaking. It only manages the discovery -> registration -> Fabric pipeline. (3) Lifecycle monitoring subscribes to Fabric's existing health events -- no custom heartbeat or CB tracking needed because Fabric's HealthChecker (3.6.1) already does periodic health checks bidirectionally wired with circuit breakers (3.6.3). |
| 5.1.4 | Implement K0 Connector Proxy Client | DONE | k1/orchestrator/connectors/k0_proxy_client.py | **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, bridge: IBridgeWritePort, proxy_endpoint: Optional[str] = None)`. If proxy_endpoint is None: client is disabled (Edge-First degradation). **Types**: `@dataclass ProxyRequest: server_id: str, tool_name: str, params: dict, auth_context: Optional[dict]`. `@dataclass ProxyResponse: data: dict, cached: bool, cache_ttl_s: int`. **Methods**: `async def invoke_via_proxy(self, request: ProxyRequest) -> ProxyResponse` -- (1) If self.proxy_endpoint is None: raise ProxyUnavailableError (caller falls back to direct MCP invoke). (2) POST to `{proxy_endpoint}/mcp/invoke` with request body. (3) K0 proxy handles: auth token injection (from K0 credential vault), rate limiting aggregation (K0 enforces per-account limits across all K1 instances), response caching (K0 caches for configured TTL). (4) Parse response. On HTTP 401: trigger token refresh via bridge.submit_token_refresh(server_id). On HTTP 429: return ProxyRateLimitedError with retry_after_s. `async def check_proxy_health(self) -> bool` -- lightweight GET to proxy_endpoint/health. `def is_available(self) -> bool` -- returns self.proxy_endpoint is not None. **Integration with Fabric MCPProvider**: K0ProxyClient is registered as a port/adapter that Fabric's MCPProvider can use for remote servers. On Fabric's MCPProvider.execute() for remote servers: if K0ProxyClient.is_available() -> route through proxy. If not -> direct HTTP. This integration is wired via Fabric's ProviderConfig.proxy_endpoint field set during register_from_dict(). **Gotchas**: (1) K0 proxy is OPTIONAL -- entirely absent in standalone/edge mode. (2) Auth tokens are managed by K0, never stored in K1 (security boundary). (3) Cache responses from proxy include cache_ttl_s -- K1 does NOT implement its own MCP response cache (K0 owns caching). (4) Rate limiting: proxy returns 429 with retry_after; K1 backs off and retries. No local rate tracking. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: All connector code in `k1/orchestrator/connectors/`. MCPToolDiscovery in `mcp_discovery.py` (5.1.1). MCPRegistrationBridge in `mcp_registrar.py` (5.1.2). ConnectorLifecycleManager in `connector_lifecycle.py` (5.1.3). K0ConnectorProxyClient in `k0_proxy_client.py` (5.1.4). Config: `k1/connectors/mcp_servers.yaml`.
> - **Constructor dependency chain for ConnectorLifecycleManager** (OrchestratorFactory step 15 in 6.2.1):
>   1. `discovery: MCPToolDiscovery(config_path)` -- reads `mcp_servers.yaml` (5.1.1)
>   2. `registrar: MCPRegistrationBridge(fabric_port, delta_port)` -- translates discovered tools to Fabric contract dicts (5.1.2)
>   3. `delta: IDeltaEmitPort` -- for emitting registration events
>   4. `events: IEventSubscriptionPort` -- for subscribing to Fabric health events
>   5. Wire: `ConnectorLifecycleManager(discovery, registrar, events, delta)` (5.1.3)
> - **K0 Connector Proxy constructor** (optional, may be None):
>   1. `bridge: IBridgeWritePort` -- for token refresh via `bridge.submit_token_refresh()` (5.1.4)
>   2. `proxy_endpoint: Optional[str]` -- from config; None in standalone/edge mode (5.1.4)
> - **Discovery -> Registration -> Fabric pipeline** (during `init()` step 4 in 6.2.2):
>   1. `discovery.discover_all()` -> reads YAML config -> iterates servers -> sends `tools/list` per server (local: subprocess stdio, remote: HTTP POST) -> returns `list[DiscoveredTool]`
>   2. `registrar.register_tools(tools)` -> for each tool: build capability_id -> check registry for collision via `fabric.query_registry()` -> if new: build contract_dict -> `delta.emit(ORCH_MCP_TOOL_REGISTERED, contract_dict, trace_id)` -> Fabric receives event (5.4.4) -> `register_from_dict(contract_dict)` -> Fabric creates MCPProvider via its ProviderFactory -> Fabric sets up CircuitBreaker wrapping -> Fabric HealthChecker starts monitoring
>   3. ConnectorLifecycleManager stores `server_capabilities[server_id] = [capability_ids]` for future unregister/refresh
>   4. Return `RegistrationResult(registered, skipped, errors)`
> - **MCP tool invocation call chain** (during DAG step execution -- Orchestrator has NO role here):
>   1. `StepRunner.run(step)` (2.3.3) builds `CapabilityRequest` -> sends to `fabric_port.execute()`
>   2. Fabric resolves capability to MCPProvider (3.3.2) via Resolver (3.1.5)
>   3. CircuitBreaker (3.4.1) wraps the call with per-provider timeout
>   4. MCPProvider.execute() handles stdio/SSE/HTTP transport
>   5. OutputValidationPipeline (3.5.5) validates result before returning
>   6. Orchestrator receives CapabilityResult -- zero MCP-specific code in this path
> - **Lifecycle monitoring** (background, started in init() step 9 in 6.2.2):
>   1. Subscribes to `k1.fabric.provider.health.changed.v1` via `IEventSubscriptionPort` -- this is Fabric's existing event from HealthChecker (3.6.1)
>   2. On MCP provider recovery (OFFLINE -> ONLINE): `connector_lifecycle.refresh(server_id)` re-discovers + re-registers (server may have new/removed tools after restart)
>   3. On MCP provider failure (ONLINE -> OFFLINE): emit `k1.mcp.server.unavailable` delta for observability. No action needed -- Fabric's CircuitBreaker already blocks execution and AvailabilityTracker already marks OFFLINE
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.4 ports (IFabricGatewayPort for registry query, IDeltaEmitPort for registration events, IEventSubscriptionPort for Fabric health events, IBridgeWritePort for K0 proxy). Fabric's register_from_dict() (Fab 2.3.4) and event handler (Fab 5.4.4) for the registration pipeline.
> - **Consumed by**: OrchestratorService.init() (6.2.2 step 4) calls `discover_and_register()`. ConstraintResolver (3.1.2) queries capabilities registered by this pipeline (via Fabric's registry). ProactiveGapDetector (4.2.7) reacts to capability changes via Fabric's `k1.fabric.capability.contract_updated.v1` event. Tests: 7.1.6, 7.3A.4, 9.2.6.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: ConnectorLifecycleManager does NOT store provider instances (Fabric owns those). Does NOT track health (Fabric HealthChecker does). Does NOT do circuit breaking (Fabric CircuitBreaker does). Does NOT sandbox execution (Fabric output validation + CB timeout does). Does NOT do active heartbeat (Fabric HealthChecker does periodic checks). Orchestrator's only MCP responsibilities are: (1) knowing WHICH servers exist (config), (2) discovering WHAT tools they expose (tools/list), (3) telling Fabric about them (register_from_dict via event), (4) re-discovering on recovery (lifecycle monitoring). Everything else is Fabric's job.

## Milestone 6: Adapters & Factory

> **Goal**: Implement infrastructure bindings for all 8 ports (16 adapters: 8 prod + 8 test), OrchestratorFactory, lifecycle, and concurrency model.
>
> **Prerequisite**: M1 complete (port interfaces defined in Epic 1.4 + IWorkflowStoragePort in 4.1.6).
>
> **Pattern**: Same as Fabric M5 (adapters, factory, events).

### Epic 6.1: Adapters (Production + Test)

**Goal**: Implement infrastructure bindings for all 8 ports.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 6.1.1 | MailboxAdapter (prod) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/adapters/mailbox_adapter.py | `class MailboxAdapter(IMailboxPort)`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, max_depth: int = 100, wfq_weights: dict[str, float] = {"REALTIME": 0.6, "INTERACTIVE": 0.3, "BACKGROUND": 0.1})`. **State**: 3 internal queues: `self._queues: dict[str, deque] = {"REALTIME": deque(), "INTERACTIVE": deque(), "BACKGROUND": deque()}`. `self._total_depth: int = 0`. `self._wfq_credits: dict[str, float]` initialized from weights. **enqueue(message, priority)**: (1) If self._total_depth >= max_depth: raise `MailboxFullError("Mailbox at capacity: {max_depth}")`. Caller (Concierge) catches this to show "system busy" feedback. (2) Validate priority in REALTIME/INTERACTIVE/BACKGROUND. (3) Append to appropriate queue. (4) Increment_total_depth. (5) Return queue position (index within that priority queue). **dequeue()**: WFQ across 3 priority classes. Algorithm: (1) Refill credits: for each class, add weight (0.6/0.3/0.1). (2) Select highest-credit class that has messages. (3) Deduct 1.0 credit, popleft from that queue. (4) Decrement_total_depth. (5) Return message or None if all queues empty. Guarantees: REALTIME gets ~60% of dequeue slots, INTERACTIVE ~30%, BACKGROUND ~10%. **depth()**: `return self._total_depth`. **peek_priority()**: returns priority class of next message (by WFQ logic) without removing. **Gotchas**: (1) All methods are sync (in-process, no I/O per IMailboxPort contract). (2) deque is O(1) append/popleft. (3) WFQ credits refill every dequeue call -- ensures fair scheduling even under burst. (4) MailboxFullError maps to RECOVERABLE in ErrorRouter (re-enqueue once after brief delay). **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §9 defines mailbox durability requirements including deduplication (envelope_id-based), backpressure behavior (MailboxFullError -> Concierge shows 'system busy'), and TaskAck response contract (1.2.20 item 14). Mailbox depth monitoring feeds into admin health status (1.2.25). |
| 6.1.2 | FabricGatewayAdapter (prod) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/adapters/fabric_gateway_adapter.py | `class FabricGatewayAdapter(IFabricGatewayPort)`. **Fabric alignment**: Wraps the `Fabric` facade class (`from k1.fabric.fabric import Fabric`). The Fabric class is the unified entry point to L2.5, providing `execute()`, `execute_batch()`, and `lookup()` methods. **Import**: `from k1.fabric.fabric import Fabric, BatchStrategy`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, fabric: Fabric)`. fabric is the fully-constructed Fabric instance (created by FabricFactory 5.3.1 and injected by OrchestratorFactory). **execute(request)**: `async def execute(self, request: CapabilityRequest) -> CapabilityResult` -- (1) `result = await self.fabric.execute(request)` (Fabric.execute delegates to CapabilityFabric facade). (2) Return CapabilityResult directly (Fabric already returns this type). (3) On Fabric error: map to AdapterError -- FabricError/timeout -> AdapterError("fabric_gateway", DEGRADED, "Fabric timeout"), connection error -> AdapterError("fabric_gateway", DEGRADED, "Fabric unreachable"), unknown -> AdapterError("fabric_gateway", TERMINAL, str(e)). **execute_batch(requests)**: `results = await self.fabric.execute_batch(requests, BatchStrategy.PARALLEL)` -- Fabric has native batch support via CapabilityFabric.execute_batch(). Falls back to `asyncio.gather(*[self.execute(r) for r in requests], return_exceptions=True)` if Fabric batch raises. Order preserved. **query_registry(capability_name)**: `contract = self.fabric.lookup(capability_name)` -- Fabric.lookup() delegates to CapabilityRegistryAPI.lookup() which is O(1) exact match. Returns CapabilityContract (25+ field dataclass) or None. Adapter maps to RegistryEntry (Orchestrator's lightweight view): `RegistryEntry(name=contract.name, provider_type=contract.provider_type, safety_band_min=contract.safety_band_min, availability=..., compensation_capability=..., estimated_duration_ms=...)`. **CB_FABRIC ownership** (per PROTOCOL-4): Orchestrator does NOT own circuit breaker. Concierge owns CB_FABRIC (30s timeout, 5 failures/min). FabricGatewayAdapter is pure pass-through -- translates errors, no circuit breaking. **Gotchas**: (1) execute_batch uses Fabric's native batch API -- partial failures return mixed list of CapabilityResult (some with success=False). (2) No retry logic in adapter -- retries are StepRunner's responsibility. (3) query_registry maps CapabilityContract -> RegistryEntry, filtering to fields Orchestrator needs. (4) Fabric instance must be fully initialized before adapter creation (health_checker started, registry loaded). |
| 6.1.3 | PlannerAdapter (prod) | DONE | k1/orchestrator/adapters/planner_adapter.py | `class PlannerAdapter(IPlannerPort)`. **Constructor**: `def __init__(self, planner_mailbox: Any, cb_planner: CircuitBreaker)`. cb_planner: CircuitBreaker(timeout_s=45, failure_threshold=2, reset_interval_s=60). **request_plan(request)**: (1) Check CB: if OPEN -> raise AdapterError("planner", DEGRADED, "CB_PLANNER open"). (2) Enqueue PlanRequest to planner_mailbox. (3) Return PlanAck(ACCEPTED). On enqueue failure: PlanAck(REJECTED, reason). CB records success/failure. **cancel_plan(request_id)**: `await planner_mailbox.send_cancel(request_id)`. Best-effort. **micro_replan(request)**: `async def micro_replan(self, request: MicroReplanRequest) -> Optional[CommittedPlan]` -- synchronous call with 10s timeout: `try: return await asyncio.wait_for(planner_mailbox.micro_replan(request), timeout=10.0) except asyncio.TimeoutError: return None`. CB_PLANNER records timeout as failure. **Error mapping**: CB OPEN -> AdapterError(DEGRADED) -> ErrorRouter returns FALLBACK (degrade HIGH->MEDIUM). Planner rejection -> AdapterError(RECOVERABLE). Planner timeout -> AdapterError(DEGRADED). **Gotchas**: (1) PlannerAdapter OWNS CB_PLANNER (created internally as CircuitBreaker instance from 1.2.23). This is correct because Orchestrator is the direct caller to Planner's mailbox -- the CB wraps outbound calls from Orchestrator to Planner. FabricGatewayAdapter does NOT own CB_FABRIC (Concierge's FabricOrchestratorAdapter owns it per PROTOCOL-4, because tier degradation decisions happen at the Concierge level). (2) micro_replan() is sync with timeout -- blocks caller for up to 10s. Acceptable because DAGExecutor is already mid-execution. (3) On CB_PLANNER OPEN: PlannerAdapter raises AdapterError("planner", DEGRADED, "CB_PLANNER open") -> ErrorRouter classifies as CIRCUIT_OPEN -> error returned to Concierge which decides tier degradation (HIGH->MEDIUM). Orchestrator does NOT make the degradation decision itself -- it reports the error severity. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: All adapter files in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/`. Production adapters: 7 files (6.1.1-6.1.7) + 1 workflow storage alias (6.1.15). Test adapters: 8 files (6.1.8-6.1.14) + 1 test workflow storage (6.1.16). Total: 16 adapter files.
> - **Port -> Production Adapter -> Test Adapter mapping** (OrchestratorFactory steps 3-10 in 6.2.1):
>
>   | Port (1.4.x) | Prod Adapter (6.1.x) | Test Adapter (6.1.x) | Factory Step | External Dep |
>   |---|---|---|---|---|
>   | IMailboxPort (1.4.1) | MailboxAdapter (6.1.1) | TestMailboxAdapter (6.1.8) | Step 3 | None (in-process) |
>   | IFabricGatewayPort (1.4.2) | FabricGatewayAdapter (6.1.2) | MockFabricAdapter (6.1.9) | Step 4 | k1.fabric client |
>   | IPlannerPort (1.4.3) | PlannerAdapter (6.1.3) | MockPlannerAdapter (6.1.10) | Step 5 | k1.planner mailbox |
>   | IStateReadPort (1.4.4) | StateReadAdapter (6.1.4) | MockStateReadAdapter (6.1.11) | Step 6 | k1.sessionstate |
>   | IDeltaEmitPort (1.4.5) | DeltaEmitAdapter (6.1.5) | TestDeltaAdapter (6.1.12) | Step 7 | K1 event_bus + delta_bus |
>   | IBridgeWritePort (1.4.6) | BridgeWriteAdapter (6.1.6) | MockBridgeAdapter (6.1.13) | Step 8 | K0 Bridge client |
>   | IEventSubscriptionPort (1.4.7) | EventSubscriptionAdapter (6.1.7) | TestEventAdapter (6.1.14) | Step 9 | K1 event_bus |
>   | IWorkflowStoragePort (4.1.6) | WorkflowStorageAdapter (6.1.15) | TestWorkflowStorageAdapter (6.1.16) | Step 10 | SQLite (local file) |
>
> - **Adapter selection by factory method**:
>   - `create_production(config)`: ALL production adapters wired. External deps injected from config/DI container.
>   - `create_standalone()`: ALL test adapters wired. Zero external deps. Must complete in < 10ms.
>   - `create_for_testing(overrides)`: ALL test adapters by default. Caller can override specific ports via `overrides` dict. Event capture enabled on TestEventAdapter.
>   - `create_with_ports(**ports)`: Caller provides exact adapter instances for each port. Used for cross-subsystem integration tests (7.5.x) mixing real + test adapters.
> - **Adapter error contract**: Production adapters MUST translate external failures to `AdapterError(adapter_name, severity, message)`. Severity levels: RECOVERABLE (retry-safe), DEGRADED (CB or partial), TERMINAL (non-recoverable). Test adapters NEVER raise AdapterError unless explicitly scripted.
> - **Fire-and-forget adapters**: DeltaEmitAdapter (6.1.5) and BridgeWriteAdapter (6.1.6) write methods NEVER raise -- they log warnings and return silently. Only BridgeWriteAdapter.read_wal() can raise (blocks crash recovery). This asymmetry is intentional.
> - **CB ownership split**: PlannerAdapter (6.1.3) OWNS CB_PLANNER (created internally -- Orchestrator is the direct caller to Planner's mailbox, so CB wraps outbound calls). FabricGatewayAdapter (6.1.2) does NOT own CB_FABRIC (Concierge's FabricOrchestratorAdapter owns it per PROTOCOL-4 because tier degradation decisions happen at Concierge level). CB_MCP is owned by Fabric's per-provider CircuitBreaker (3.4.1). CB_BRIDGE ownership TBD (V1: BridgeWriteAdapter fire-and-forget writes don't need CB; read_wal is failure-surfacing). Orchestrator configures ONLY CB_PLANNER in OrchestratorConfig (1.2.22).
> - **Depends on**: Epic 1.4 (all 8 port Protocol definitions), Epic 1.2 (types used in adapter signatures: CapabilityRequest, CapabilityResult, AdapterError, PlanRequest, PlanAck, etc.).
> - **Consumed by**: OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1) injects adapters during construction. All tests (7.x) use adapters via factory. Contract tests (7.4.2) verify adapters implement port Protocols.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Adapters are THIN wrappers -- they translate external interfaces to port Protocols, nothing more. Do NOT add business logic to adapters. Do NOT add retry logic to adapters (retries are StepRunner's job). Do NOT add caching to adapters (V1 has no adapter-level cache). Test adapters are NOT mocks -- they are real implementations with in-memory state. The naming (MockFabricAdapter etc.) is for disambiguation from production adapters.

### Epic 6.2: OrchestratorFactory + Lifecycle

**Goal**: Composition root that wires all services, ports, and adapters. Lifecycle management.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 6.2.1 | Implement OrchestratorFactory | TODO | k1/orchestrator/factory.py | **Composition root**. No constructor -- static factory methods only. **Factory methods**: `@staticmethod async def create_production(config: OrchestratorConfig) -> OrchestratorService` -- builds full production stack. `@staticmethod async def create_standalone() -> OrchestratorService` -- all test adapters, no external deps (for standalone testing). `@staticmethod async def create_for_testing(overrides: dict[str, Any] = {}) -> OrchestratorService` -- test adapters + event capture + optional overrides for specific ports/services. `@staticmethod async def create_with_ports(**ports: Any) -> OrchestratorService` -- inject custom adapters (for integration tests mixing real + mock). **OrchestratorConfig**: `@dataclass OrchestratorConfig: mailbox_depth: int = 100, max_pending_plans: int = 50, max_waves: int = 20, max_steps: int = 50, max_workflow_depth: int = 3, mcp_config_path: str = "k1/connectors/mcp_servers.yaml", db_path: str = "k1/data/workflows.db", k0_proxy_endpoint: Optional[str] = None`. **15-step dependency-safe construction in `_construct_orchestrator(config, adapters)`**: Step 1: ErrorRouter(delta_port). Step 2: ConcurrencyGuard(). Step 3-10: Create/inject all 8 port adapters (mailbox through workflow_storage). Step 11: StepRunner(fabric_port, error_router). Step 12: guards = [OutputSchemaGuard(), ConditionalEdgeEvaluator(), TokenBudgetTracker(), QualityGate(), MicroReplanCheckpoint(planner_port), ExecutionMonitor(delta_port, orchestrator_ref), CostBudgetGuard(delta_port), ConcurrencyGuard (already created)]. Step 13: DAGExecutor(fabric_port, planner_port, delta_port, state_port, bridge_port, step_runner, error_router, guards). Step 14: ConstraintResolver(fabric_port, delta_port, event_port). WorkflowEngine (registry, compiler, scheduler, supervisor, gap_detector -- all wired with storage_port, fabric_port, delta_port, state_port). Step 15: ConnectorLifecycleManager(discovery, registrar, events, delta_port). Wire OrchestratorService with all deps. **Event wiring**: register all IEventSubscriptionPort subscriptions (per 1.4.7 spec). **Gotchas**: (1) Construction order matters -- guards reference services that must exist. Use placeholder/lazy init for circular ref (ExecutionMonitor needs orchestrator_ref). (2) create_standalone() is the test entry point -- must be fast (< 10ms, no I/O). (3) create_production() calls init() lifecycle automatically; create_for_testing() does NOT (tests control lifecycle). **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §8.5 defines canonical guard wiring order (G0-G10) -- the guards list in_construct_orchestrator() MUST match the ordering from WB §8 (G0=SchemaGuard through G9=OutputSchemaGuard, excluding V1-removed G5/G6/G10) unless overridden by OrchestratorConfig.guard_order (1.2.22). Reference guard types in 1.2.24. |
| 6.2.2 | Implement init() lifecycle | TODO | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def init(self) -> None`. **10-step sequence**: (1) Validate all 8 ports injected (non-None). Log port types for diagnostics. (2) Connect ports that need initialization (event_port may need connection setup). (3) Acquire concurrency guard initial state (assert not locked). (4) `registration = await self.connector_lifecycle.discover_and_register()`. Log: "{registration.registered} MCP tools registered in Fabric, {registration.skipped} skipped". (5) Tools registered in Fabric are now available for DAG execution. (6) Load active workflows: `workflows = await self.workflow_engine.registry.list_active()`. Log count. (7) Start WorkflowScheduler: `await self.workflow_engine.scheduler.start()`. (8) Subscribe to all consumed events per 1.4.7 spec: k1.planner.plan.ready.v1 -> route to mailbox as CommittedPlan, k1.planner.plan.failed.v1 / k1.planner.plan.cancelled.v1 -> route as PlanFailedEvent/PlanCancelledEvent, k1.hil.override_response/fallback_response -> route to PendingHILContext resolver, k1.fabric.capability.contract_updated.v1 -> route to GapDetector, fabric.agent.*.tool_call/llm_call.* -> route to ExecutionMonitor sub-step observer. (9) Start connector lifecycle monitoring: `self._lifecycle_task = asyncio.create_task(self.connector_lifecycle.start_lifecycle_monitoring())`. Start timeout reaper: `self._reaper_task = asyncio.create_task(self._reap_stale_contexts())`. (10) Start mailbox processing loop: `self._loop_task = asyncio.create_task(self._mailbox_loop())`. **Max init time**: 500ms target. **Async MCP handling** (per SPEC-10): init() proceeds after 500ms regardless. If MCP discovery is slow, continue in background. Tools registered as discovered. If workflow fires before MCP tool ready: WorkflowCompiler waits up to 2s for pending discovery, then treats as capability gap (ProactiveGap). **Gotcha**: init() must be idempotent -- calling twice should be safe (guard with self._initialized flag). **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §6 defines health/ready probe methods that must be operational after init() -- liveness at /health/live (always 200 if process running), readiness at /health/ready (200 only after init completes with all ports connected + mailbox loop started). init() completion is the readiness gate. Reference admin types in 1.2.25 and Epic 6.3. |
| 6.2.3 | Implement shutdown() lifecycle | TODO | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def shutdown(self) -> None`. **9-step sequence**: (1) Set `self._running = False` to stop mailbox loop (loop checks this flag each iteration). (2) Stop connector lifecycle monitoring: `self.connector_lifecycle.stop()`. Cancel lifecycle_task. (3) Wait for active DAG completion: `if self.concurrency_guard.active: await asyncio.wait_for(self._dag_complete_event.wait(), timeout=30.0)`. If timeout: log warning "DAG still active after 30s, force-compensating". (4) If DAG forced: invoke Saga.compensate() for all completed side-effect steps (per 2.2.5). Emit ABORTED AggregatedResult. (5) Stop WorkflowScheduler: `await self.workflow_engine.scheduler.stop()`. (6) Unsubscribe all events: iterate self._subscriptions and call event_port.unsubscribe() for each. (7) Persist workflow trigger states: `for wf in workflows: await storage.update_trigger_state(wf.id, scheduler.get_next_fire(wf.id), scheduler.get_last_fire(wf.id))`. Ensures crash recovery can resume triggers from correct state. (8) Final audit write: `await self.bridge_port.submit_audit({"event": "orchestrator_shutdown", "pending_plans": len(self.pending_plans), "pending_hil": len(self.pending_hil)}, trace_id)`. (9) Disconnect ports in LIFO order (reverse of init): event_sub -> bridge -> delta -> state -> planner -> fabric -> mailbox -> workflow_storage. Cancel reaper_task. **Gotchas**: (1) shutdown() must handle partial init (some ports may not have been connected). Use try/except per step. (2) Step 7 (trigger persistence) is critical for cron workflows -- without it, cron fires missed after restart. (3) Pending plans/HIL contexts are orphaned on shutdown -- log count as warning for diagnostics. |
| 6.2.4 | Implement crash_recovery() lifecycle | TODO | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def crash_recovery(self) -> RecoveryResult`. `@dataclass RecoveryResult: recovered_dags: int, failed_recoveries: int, skipped: int`. Called during init() before mailbox loop starts (between steps 2 and 3). **Logic**: (1) Read WAL entries: `for dag_id in await self.bridge_port.list_wal_ids():` (requires list_wal_ids() on IBridgeWritePort or bridge_port.read_wal with scan). (2) For each dag_id with WAL: `entries = await self.bridge_port.read_wal(dag_id)`. (3) Parse entries to determine last completed wave: find highest WAVE_COMPLETE entry. (4) If DAG_COMPLETE found: DAG already finished, clean up WAL entry, skip (increment skipped). (5) If only PLAN_START found: DAG never started execution. Reconstruct CommittedPlan from WAL PLAN_START payload. Resume from wave 0. (6) If WAVE_COMPLETE(N) found: resume from wave N+1. Reconstruct merged_results from STEP_COMPLETE entries. (7) Feed recovered plan + state into DAGExecutor.execute(plan, snapshot, resume_from_wave=N+1, prior_results=recovered_merged_results). (8) On recovery error: log error, increment failed_recoveries, continue with next dag_id. **Bootstrap**: After recovery, proceed with normal init() steps (MCP discovery, scheduler start, etc.). **Gotchas**: (1) If K0 is offline (bridge_port.read_wal raises DEGRADED): crash recovery is impossible. Log warning "K0 unavailable, skipping crash recovery", proceed with clean start. Documented V1 limitation. (2) Recovered DAGs use fresh state_port.snapshot() (stale context from crash time is lost). (3) crash_recovery() must be called BEFORE mailbox loop starts -- prevents race with new incoming messages. |
| 6.2.5 | Implement mailbox processing loop | TODO | k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py | **Signature**: `async def _mailbox_loop(self) -> None`. **Core loop**: `while self._running: msg = self.mailbox.dequeue(); if msg is None: await asyncio.sleep(0.001); continue; await self._process_one(msg)`. **_process_one(msg)**: (1) Extract trace_id from msg (all MailboxMessage types have trace_id). (2) Log: message type, priority, trace_id. (3) **Concurrency check**: if isinstance(msg, InterruptRequest): bypass concurrency guard (interrupts always processed). Else: if self.concurrency_guard.active AND msg requires DAG (TaskEnvelope, CommittedPlan, WorkflowRunRequest): re-enqueue msg to mailbox at BACKGROUND priority, log "DAG active, deferring {type}", return. (4) `result = await self.process(msg)`. (5) Log result status. (6) If result.status == DEFERRED: no further action (plan in flight). If FAILED: emit error delta. If COMPLETED: emit completion delta (already handled in process()). **Single-threaded guarantee**: only ONE message processed at a time. Parallelism occurs WITHIN DAG waves (via asyncio.gather in DAGExecutor), not at mailbox level. **Polling interval**: 1ms sleep when empty. Yields to event loop (asyncio.sleep(0.001) allows event handlers, timers, and scheduled tasks to run). **Gotchas**: (1) InterruptRequest MUST bypass concurrency guard -- user cancellation during active DAG. process() handles InterruptRequest by setting DAGExecutor.interrupt_flag=True. (2) Re-enqueued messages go to BACKGROUND priority to prevent starvation of new REALTIME messages. (3) The 1ms poll is a V1 simplification. V2: use asyncio.Event notification from enqueue() for zero-latency wake-up. (4)_mailbox_loop catches all exceptions per iteration (never crashes the loop). Unhandled exception -> log error + continue. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: OrchestratorFactory in `k1/orchestrator/factory.py` (6.2.1). All lifecycle methods (init, shutdown, crash_recovery,_mailbox_loop) in `k1/orchestrator/orchestration/orchestrator_service.py` (6.2.2-6.2.5). OrchestratorConfig in `k1/orchestrator/config/orchestrator_config.py` (or inline in factory.py).
> - **OrchestratorFactory is the COMPOSITION ROOT** -- the single place where ALL dependencies are wired. No other file creates adapters or services directly.
> - **15-step dependency-safe construction order** (in `_construct_orchestrator(config, adapters)`):
>   - **Steps 1-2** (Infrastructure): `ErrorRouter(delta_port)` + `ConcurrencyGuard()` -- no service deps, created first.
>   - **Steps 3-10** (Ports): Create/inject all 8 adapters per factory method (see Epic 6.1 port mapping table). Production: external client injection. Testing: in-memory adapters.
>   - **Step 11** (StepRunner): `StepRunner(fabric_port, error_router)` -- needs fabric_port (step 4) + error_router (step 1).
>   - **Step 12** (Guards): Ordered list (ORDER MATTERS): `[OutputSchemaGuard(), ConditionalEdgeEvaluator(), MicroReplanCheckpoint(planner_port, 1), ExecutionMonitor(delta_port, orchestrator_ref)]` + `ConcurrencyGuard` (step 2, NOT in guard list but referenced). **V1 removed**: TokenBudgetTracker, QualityGate, CostBudgetGuard (phantom field consumers). ExecutionMonitor needs `orchestrator_ref` (CIRCULAR -- resolved via lazy init: pass placeholder, set after OrchestratorService created).
>   - **Step 13** (DAGExecutor): `DAGExecutor(fabric_port, planner_port, delta_port, state_port, bridge_port, step_runner, error_router, guards)` -- 8 deps from prior steps.
>   - **Step 14** (Workflow + Constraint): `ConstraintResolver(fabric_port, delta_port, event_port)`. `WorkflowRegistry(storage_port)`. `WorkflowCompiler(fabric_port, delta_port, storage_port, state_port, SystemClock())`. `WorkflowScheduler(storage_port, mailbox_port, state_port, SystemClock(), 1.0)`. `WorkflowRunSupervisor(registry, compiler, storage_port, delta_port, bridge_port)`. `CrossWorkflowResolver(registry, compiler, WorkflowDepthGuard(3))`. `ProactiveGapDetector(registry, compiler, storage_port, event_port, delta_port)`.
>   - **Step 15** (Connectors): `ConnectorLifecycleManager(MCPToolDiscovery(config.mcp_config_path), MCPRegistrationBridge(fabric_port, delta_port), event_port, delta_port)`. Optional: `K0ConnectorProxyClient(bridge_port, config.k0_proxy_endpoint)`. No MCPProviderFactory, no ConnectorSandbox, no MCPReDiscoveryService -- Fabric owns provider creation, sandboxing, health monitoring, and circuit breakers.
>   - **Final**: `OrchestratorService(mailbox, fabric_port, planner_port, state_port, delta_port, bridge_port, event_port, storage_port, dag_executor, constraint_resolver, error_router, concurrency_guard, connector_lifecycle, workflow_engine, ...)`. Set `orchestrator_ref` on ExecutionMonitor (resolve lazy init).
> - **Lifecycle method call sequence**:
>   - `create_production(config)` -> `_construct_orchestrator()` -> `service.init()` (auto-start). Returns ready-to-use service.
>   - `create_for_testing(overrides)` -> `_construct_orchestrator()` -> returns service WITHOUT calling `init()`. Tests control lifecycle.
>   - `create_standalone()` -> `_construct_orchestrator()` with all test adapters -> returns service WITHOUT calling `init()`. Must complete < 10ms.
> - **init() 10-step sequence** (6.2.2): validate ports -> connect ports -> crash_recovery() -> MCP discover_and_register() -> load workflows -> start scheduler -> subscribe events -> start re-discovery -> start reaper -> start mailbox loop. **500ms target**. MCP async: proceeds regardless after 500ms.
> - **shutdown() 9-step sequence** (6.2.3): stop loop -> stop re-discovery -> wait active DAG (30s) -> force-compensate if timeout -> stop scheduler -> unsubscribe events -> persist trigger states -> final audit -> disconnect ports LIFO.
> - **crash_recovery()** (6.2.4): Called inside init() between steps 2-3. Reads WAL via bridge_port -> reconstructs DAG state -> resumes via DAGExecutor. K0 offline -> skip recovery, clean start.
> - **_mailbox_loop()** (6.2.5): `while _running`: dequeue -> _process_one() -> sleep(1ms) if empty. InterruptRequest bypasses ConcurrencyGuard. DAG-requiring messages re-enqueued at BACKGROUND if guard active.
> - **Depends on**: EVERYTHING. Factory is the composition root -- it imports and wires all modules from M1-M5.
> - **Consumed by**: All tests (7.x) via factory methods. Production startup script calls `OrchestratorFactory.create_production(config)`. Cross-subsystem tests (7.5.x) use `create_with_ports()`.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: OrchestratorFactory has NO constructor -- static factory methods ONLY. Do NOT instantiate OrchestratorFactory. Construction order is STRICT -- later steps depend on earlier steps (no reordering). ExecutionMonitor circular dependency is resolved via lazy init (placeholder -> set after service created) -- do NOT use import-time singletons. `create_production()` calls `init()` automatically; `create_for_testing()` and `create_standalone()` do NOT. `_mailbox_loop` is 1ms polling in V1 -- do NOT use asyncio.Queue or threading (single-threaded async model). shutdown() disconnect order is LIFO (reverse of init connection order) -- do NOT disconnect in arbitrary order.

### Epic 6.3: Admin API (Lightweight HTTP)

**Goal**: Implement a lightweight admin HTTP interface for operational visibility and control. Provides health probes, DAG inspection, circuit breaker management, and graceful drain -- all pre-defined in Schema Whiteboard §5 (18 endpoints) and §6 (health check probes).

> **Reference**: Schema Whiteboard §5 (Admin API -- 18 endpoints), §6 (Health Check Probes). Admin types defined in 1.2.25.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|

(2) **DAG Inspection**: `async def list_active_dags(self) -> List[ActiveDAGInfo]`, `async def get_dag_detail(self, dag_id: str) -> Optional[dict]`, `async def cancel_dag(self, dag_id: str) -> bool`. (3) **Circuit Breakers**: `async def list_circuit_breakers(self) -> Dict[str, CircuitBreakerState]`, `async def set_cb_state(self, name: str, state: str) -> bool` (force OPEN/CLOSED/HALF_OPEN). (4) **Scheduler**: `async def list_triggers(self) -> List[dict]`, `async def get_trigger(self, workflow_id: str) -> Optional[dict]`. (5) **Drain**: `async def drain(self, timeout_ms: int = 30000) -> DrainResult`. (6) **Config**: `async def get_config(self) -> dict` (read-only OrchestratorConfig view). **Import**: References HealthStatus, ActiveDAGInfo, DrainResult, CircuitBreakerState from types.py (1.2.25, 1.2.23). **Anti-hallucination**: Protocol only -- no implementation. Add to ports/**init**.py re-exports. |

Wraps OrchestratorService internal state for read-only admin access. **HTTP server**: aiohttp lightweight (NOT full web framework). `self._app = web.Application()`. `self._runner: Optional[web.AppRunner] = None`. **Route registration**: `_register_routes()` maps 18 endpoints per WB §5: GET /health/live, GET /health/ready, GET /health/status, GET /admin/dags, GET /admin/dags/{dag_id}, POST /admin/dags/{dag_id}/cancel, GET /admin/circuit-breakers, POST /admin/circuit-breakers/{name}/state, GET /admin/scheduler/triggers, GET /admin/scheduler/triggers/{workflow_id}, POST /admin/drain, GET /admin/config, GET /admin/mailbox/depth, GET /admin/mailbox/stats, GET /admin/mcp/servers, POST /admin/mcp/rediscover, GET /admin/metrics, GET /admin/version. **Lifecycle**: `async def start(self, port: int) -> None` and `async def stop(self) -> None`. Started by init() step 9.5 (after mailbox loop start). **All responses**: JSON with trace_id header. **Auth**: None in V1 (localhost-only, per WB §5 security note). **Gotcha**: admin server runs on separate port (default 8081) from main service -- does NOT share the mailbox loop. Admin requests are handled by aiohttp's own event loop integration. |

(2) Add to init() lifecycle: between current step 9 and step 10, add `if config.admin_enabled: await admin_adapter.start(config.admin_port)`. (4) Update create_standalone() and create_for_testing(): skip admin server (admin_enabled=False in test config). **Config fields used**: admin_port (int, default 8081), admin_enabled (bool, default True for prod, False for test). **Gotcha**: admin adapter has circular dependency on OrchestratorService (needs reference to read internal state) -- same lazy-init pattern as ExecutionMonitor. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: IAdminPort in `k1/orchestrator/ports/admin_port.py` (6.3.1). AdminHttpAdapter in `k1/orchestrator/adapters/admin_http_adapter.py` (6.3.2). Factory changes in `k1/orchestrator/factory.py` (6.3.3).
> - **Dependency**: aiohttp library (add to requirements.txt if not already present). Lightweight usage -- only web.Application, web.AppRunner, web.RouteTableDef.
> - **Port → Adapter mapping**: IAdminPort (6.3.1) -> prod: AdminHttpAdapter (6.3.2) / test: TestAdminAdapter (direct method calls, no HTTP -- add to test adapters in a follow-up issue or use AdminHttpAdapter with test client).
> - **Consumed by**: OrchestratorFactory (6.3.3) for wiring, health check tests (8.1.x), operational runbook procedures (9.3.3), production Kubernetes probes (liveness/readiness).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Admin API is READ-ONLY for inspection plus targeted WRITE operations (cancel, drain, CB state). It does NOT process TaskEnvelopes or interact with the mailbox queue directly. All data is read from OrchestratorService's existing internal state. Do NOT build a full REST framework -- aiohttp lightweight is sufficient.

### Epic 6.4: K1 Kernel Wiring Checklist

**Goal**: Register the Orchestrator in `k1/kernel/kernel.md` so the kernel bootstrap knows how to create, wire, and lifecycle-manage the Orchestrator alongside Bus, Fabric, and SessionState.

> **Reference**: `k1/kernel/kernel.md` is the single reference for wiring all K1 components into a unified runtime. Bus, Fabric, and SessionState already have full wiring specs there. Orchestrator must be added as the fourth component with the same level of detail.
