Update Kernel section to reflect 4 managed components (was 3). |
| 6.4.2 | Add Orchestrator to kernel.md Dependency Graph | TODO | k1/kernel/kernel.md (Section 3) | Extend the dependency graph pseudocode. Orchestrator depends on: Bus (via FabricBusAdapter for events/deltas), Fabric (via FabricGatewayAdapter wrapping Fabric facade), SessionState (via StateReadAdapter wrapping SessionStateReaderAdapter). Bootstrap order: Bus -> SessionState -> Fabric -> **Orchestrator** (Phase 4). Orchestrator creation calls `OrchestratorFactory.create_production(config)` which internally wires all 8 ports using already-created Bus, Fabric, and SessionState instances. |
| 6.4.3 | Add Orchestrator Port Wiring Matrix | TODO | k1/kernel/kernel.md (new Section 4.3) | Document all 8 Orchestrator ports with: Port name, Protocol type, Wired Adapter in kernel bootstrap, Source Module, and Notes. Key wiring decisions: (1) `IFabricGatewayPort` -> `FabricGatewayAdapter(fabric)` wraps the Fabric facade created in Phase 3. (2) `IStateReadPort` -> `StateReadAdapter(state_reader)` wraps the same `SessionStateReaderAdapter` already created for Fabric. (3) `IEventSubscriptionPort` -> `EventSubscriptionAdapter(event_port)` wraps the same `FabricBusAdapter(bus)` IEventPort created in Phase 3. (4) `IDeltaEmitPort` -> `DeltaEmitAdapter(event_port, delta_bus)` wraps the same bus adapter instances. (5) `IMailboxPort` -> `MailboxAdapter(config.mailbox_depth)` standalone, no external dep. (6) `IPlannerPort` -> `PlannerAdapter(planner_mailbox, cb_planner)` requires Planner module mailbox. (7) `IBridgeWritePort` -> `BridgeWriteAdapter(bridge_client)` requires Bridge connector. (8) `IWorkflowStoragePort` -> `WorkflowStorageAdapter(config.workflow_db_path)` standalone SQLite. |

Factory internally creates all 8 adapters using Bus, Fabric, SessionState from earlier phases. `init()` is called automatically. Updated order: Orchestrator -> Fabric -> SessionState -> Bus -> MailboxRouter. |
| 6.4.5 | Add Orchestrator Wiring Validation Checklist | TODO | k1/kernel/kernel.md (Section 10) | Add Orchestrator-specific validation checks (W-11 through W-20): W-11: OrchestratorFactory.create_production() completes < 500ms. W-12: All 8 ports injected (non-None) after factory. W-13: FabricGatewayAdapter wraps real Fabric instance (execute() returns CapabilityResult). W-14: StateReadAdapter reads real SessionState (read_section returns data after session.start()). W-15: EventSubscriptionAdapter receives events through Bus (subscribe to k1.planner.plan.ready.v1, fire event, assert handler called). W-16: DeltaEmitAdapter emits events visible on Bus (emit, subscribe on Bus, assert received). W-17: Orchestrator processes TaskEnvelope end-to-end (enqueue -> mailbox -> process -> emit dag events). W-18: Shutdown order correct (Orchestrator before Fabric before SessionState before Bus). W-19: No circular imports (`python -c "from k1.orchestrator.factory import OrchestratorFactory"` succeeds). (2) Planner plan.ready.v1 event -> Bus -> EventSubscriptionAdapter -> Orchestrator mailbox -> DAGExecutor. (3) Orchestrator delta emit -> DeltaEmitAdapter -> Bus -> Concierge subscriber. These show how Orchestrator participates in the kernel event flow alongside the existing 3 components. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: All changes in `k1/kernel/kernel.md`. No code changes -- this is documentation that guides the kernel bootstrap implementation.
> - **Depends on**: Epic 6.1 (all 16 adapters defined), Epic 6.2 (OrchestratorFactory and lifecycle), kernel.md existing sections (Bus, Fabric, SessionState wiring already documented).
> - **Consumed by**: Kernel bootstrap implementation (`k1/kernel/bootstrap.py`). Integration tests (7.5.x) that wire Orchestrator with real Bus/Fabric/SessionState. Production deployment scripts.
> - **Blocking**: Without kernel.md wiring spec, the kernel bootstrap cannot wire Orchestrator correctly. Missing wiring = Orchestrator running in isolation (test adapters only), unable to participate in real K1 runtime.
> - **Pattern**: Same documentation pattern as existing Bus/Fabric/SessionState sections. Each component has: inventory row, dependency graph node, port wiring matrix, bootstrap phase, shutdown ordering, validation checks, event flow examples.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: kernel.md is DOCUMENTATION -- it does NOT contain implementation code. The bootstrap pseudocode in Section 7 is a guide, not executable. Actual bootstrap code lives in `k1/kernel/bootstrap.py` (TODO). Orchestrator reuses existing bus adapters (FabricBusAdapter) rather than creating new ones -- the Bus is shared infrastructure, not duplicated per component.

## Milestone 7: Testing

> **Goal**: Comprehensive test suite following Fabric's no-mock testing philosophy.
>
> **Philosophy**: Real adapters, real execution paths, real concurrency. NO MOCKS except for error boundary testing.
>
> **Test Location**: `tests/k1/orchestrator/`

**Goal**: Set up test fixtures and helpers.

Scope: `orchestrator_standalone` is function-scoped (each test gets fresh instance), `sample_*` fixtures are session-scoped (immutable data). All using real test adapters from M6 (6.1.8-6.1.16), NO MOCKS. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: `tests/k1/orchestrator/conftest.py` (7.0.1). `tests/k1/orchestrator/fixtures/` directory for JSON samples (7.0.2). `tests/k1/orchestrator/helpers.py` (7.0.3).
> - **conftest.py** (7.0.1) wires test infrastructure using OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() (6.2.1). ALL test fixtures flow through the factory -- no direct service construction.
> - **Fixture -> Factory method mapping**:
>   - `orchestrator_standalone()` -> `OrchestratorFactory.create_standalone()` -- all test adapters, no init(). Function-scoped.
>   - `orchestrator_for_testing()` -> `OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()` -- test adapters + event capture on TestEventAdapter (6.1.14). Function-scoped.
>   - `orchestrator_with_ports(**overrides)` -> `OrchestratorFactory.create_with_ports()` -- for cross-subsystem tests (7.5.x) mixing real + test adapters.
>   - `sample_*` fixtures -> session-scoped (immutable data), loaded from `tests/k1/orchestrator/fixtures/*.json` (7.0.2).
> - **Sample messages** (7.0.2) are real JSON payloads validated against contracts (1.2.x). Used by unit tests (7.1.x), integration tests (7.3.x), and cross-subsystem tests (7.5.x).
> - **Test helpers** (7.0.3) use TestEventAdapter.captured_events (6.1.14) for event assertions and TestDeltaAdapter (6.1.12) for delta assertions. `assert_invariant_orch()` dispatches to per-invariant check functions for ORCH-01 through ORCH-16.
> - **Depends on**: OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1), all test adapters (6.1.8-6.1.16), contract schemas (1.2.x).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: conftest.py NEVER imports individual services directly -- all access through factory. Sample fixtures are JSON files loaded via `json.load()`, NOT constructed in Python (ensures contract schema compliance). `wait_for_event()` helper uses polling with timeout -- NOT asyncio.wait_for (test adapter is sync capture).

Routing actions: (8) TRANSIENT -> route to RETRY (re-enqueue step), (9) PERMANENT -> route to ABORT (fail step immediately), (10) CIRCUIT_OPEN -> route to DEGRADE (tier degradation), (11) NOT_FOUND -> route to CONSTRAINT (trigger ConstraintResolver), (12) INTERNAL -> route to ABORT with diagnostic delta. Error severity classification (Orchestrator classifies, Concierge decides degradation per concierge.mmd TIER_DEGRADATION): (13) AdapterError(CIRCUIT_OPEN) from planner_port -> classify as CIRCUIT_OPEN severity, emit diagnostic delta, return error to caller (Concierge FabricOrchestratorAdapter decides HIGH->MEDIUM degradation), (14) AdapterError(CIRCUIT_OPEN) from fabric_port -> classify as CIRCUIT_OPEN severity, emit diagnostic delta, return error to caller (Concierge decides degradation), (15) AdapterError(DEGRADED) from any adapter -> classify severity, emit diagnostic delta with adapter name + error context, (16) multiple adapter errors in same DAG -> accumulate error context in AggregatedResult.error_details. NOTE: Orchestrator does NOT make tier degradation decisions (HIGH->MEDIUM->LOW). Tier degradation is Concierge FabricOrchestratorAdapter's responsibility. Orchestrator REPORTS error severity; Concierge ACTS on it. Error delta emission: (17) every error classification emits diagnostic delta via delta_port, (18) delta includes error_code, adapter, trace_id. **Invariants verified**: ORCH-09 (trace_id on error events). ~25 tests. |

> - All unit tests **access services through** OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() (6.2.1). Each test gets a fresh Orchestrator instance via `orchestrator_for_testing` fixture (7.0.1).

>   - **OrchestratorService tests** (7.1.1): Injects messages via `TestMailboxAdapter.inject()` (6.1.8). Scripts Fabric responses via `MockFabricAdapter.script_result()` (6.1.9). Verifies routing via `TestEventAdapter.assert_published()` (6.1.14). Verifies deltas via `TestDeltaAdapter.assert_emitted()` (6.1.12).
>   - **DAGExecutor tests** (7.1.2): Scripts step results via `MockFabricAdapter.script_result()` (6.1.9). Injects WAL state via `MockBridgeAdapter.inject_wal()` (6.1.13). Sets safety_band via `MockStateReadAdapter.set_safety_band()` (6.1.11). Verifies compensation via `MockBridgeAdapter.assert_wal_written()` (6.1.13).
>   - **StepRunner tests** (7.1.3): Scripts Fabric responses via `MockFabricAdapter` (6.1.9) with success/error/timeout. Verifies cost via `TestDeltaAdapter` (6.1.12).
>   - **ConstraintResolver tests** (7.1.4): Scripts registry via `MockFabricAdapter.register_capability()` (6.1.9). Verifies HIL path via `TestDeltaAdapter.assert_hil_requested()` (6.1.12). Fires HIL responses via `TestEventAdapter.fire()` (6.1.14).
>   - **WorkflowEngine tests** (7.1.5): Injects workflows via `TestWorkflowStorageAdapter.inject_workflow()` (6.1.16). Verifies triggers via `TestWorkflowStorageAdapter.assert_run_saved()` (6.1.16). Uses real asyncio timers for scheduler timing.
>   - **ConnectorLifecycleManager tests** (7.1.6): Scripts Fabric registry queries via `MockFabricAdapter` (6.1.9). K0 proxy via `MockBridgeAdapter` (6.1.13). Verifies registration events emitted, NOT provider creation (Fabric owns that).
>   - **ErrorRouter tests** (7.1.7): Verifies delta emission via `TestDeltaAdapter.assert_emitted()` (6.1.12) with error classification payloads.
> - **Pattern**: NO MOCKS. All test adapters are real implementations with in-memory state. Tests verify behavior through adapter capture logs, not through internal state inspection.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Unit tests do NOT directly construct DAGExecutor, StepRunner, or other internal services. All access is through `orchestrator.process(message)` or through the factory-provided OrchestratorService instance. The phrase "unit test" here means "test one service's behavior" -- NOT "test one function in isolation". Test adapters have script_*() and assert_*() methods for setup and verification.

> - Guard tests verify the **5-guard pipeline** (4 DAGGuards + ConcurrencyGuard) wired into DAGExecutor (2.2.1) via the guards list in OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1 step 12). V1 removed: TokenBudgetTracker, QualityGate, CostBudgetGuard.
> - **Guard test setup pattern**: Each test uses `orchestrator_for_testing` fixture (7.0.1) -> accesses guard pipeline through `orchestrator.process(committed_plan)` execution -> verifies guard effects via captured events/deltas/results.
> - **Per-guard adapter dependency**:
>   - **OutputSchemaGuard** (7.2.1): Post-step guard. Script step results via `MockFabricAdapter.script_result()` (6.1.9) with schema-violating data. Verify retry via `MockFabricAdapter.assert_called(capability, times=2)`.
>   - **ConditionalEdgeEvaluator** (7.2.2): Pre-wave guard. Uses `sample_committed_plan` fixtures with `ConditionExpr` on edges. Verify pruning via step status in `AggregatedResult`.
>   - ~~**TokenBudgetTracker** (7.2.3) + **CostBudgetGuard** (7.2.7)~~: **V1 REMOVED** -- phantom field consumer guards.
>   - **MicroReplanCheckpoint** (7.2.5): Post-wave guard. Script `MockPlannerAdapter.script_micro_replan()` (6.1.10) with revised steps. Verify max-1-replan via second trigger returning SKIP.
>   - **ExecutionMonitor** (7.2.6): Post-wave guard. Fire sub-step events via `TestEventAdapter.fire()` (6.1.14). Verify deltas via `TestDeltaAdapter.assert_progress()` (6.1.12). Verify rate limiting (max 1 per step per 500ms).
>   - **ConcurrencyGuard** (7.2.8): NOT in guard pipeline -- wraps `_process_one()`. Test via `orchestrator.process(task1)` then `orchestrator.process(task2)` while task1 active. Verify re-enqueue via `TestMailboxAdapter.assert_enqueued()` (6.1.8).
> - **Guard ordering verification**: Tests verify that guard execution order matches factory step 12 order (OutputSchemaGuard first, ExecutionMonitor last among DAGGuards). A test triggers multiple guard effects in one DAG and verifies the cascade.
> - **Pattern**: Each guard tested with `build_dag_context()` helper (7.0.3). No mocks -- guards interact with real test adapters.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Guard tests do NOT import guard classes directly -- they verify guard behavior through the OrchestratorService.process() -> DAGExecutor pipeline. ConcurrencyGuard is tested via mailbox message dispatch, NOT via direct acquire()/release() calls. The term "guard test" means testing guard EFFECTS on DAG execution results, not testing guard methods in isolation.

### Epic 7.3: Integration Tests

**Goal**: Test end-to-end message flows through Orchestrator's public API.

> **MANDATORY**: All tests use OrchestratorFactory. All mutations/reads through OrchestratorService.process(). No direct subsystem access.
>
> **HARDCORE INTEGRATION TEST REQUIREMENTS**:
>
> 1. ALWAYS use OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() or create_standalone()
> 2. All message dispatch through OrchestratorService.process() -- NEVER direct DAGExecutor/StepRunner access
> 3. All workflow triggers through OrchestratorService.process(WorkflowRunRequest) -- NEVER direct scheduler access
> 4. Real adapters only (test adapters are real -- they are NOT mocks)
> 5. Verify events via TestEventAdapter capture mode
> 6. Verify all 16 ORCH invariants hold during execution

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_medium_tier_e2e.py` (7.3.1), `test_high_tier_e2e.py` (7.3.2), `test_workflow_e2e.py` (7.3.3), `test_cross_workflow_e2e.py` (7.3.4).
> - Integration tests verify **end-to-end wiring** through OrchestratorService.process() (2.1.1). NEVER bypass the mailbox/routing layer.
> - **Per-test adapter setup pattern**:
>   - **MEDIUM tier** (7.3.1): `orchestrator = await OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()`. Script N capabilities via `mock_fabric.script_result(cap_name, success_result)`. Call `result = await orchestrator.process(TaskEnvelope(tier=MEDIUM, capabilities=[...]))`. Assert `result.status == COMPLETED`. Assert `mock_fabric.assert_called(cap_name, times=1)` per capability. Assert `test_delta.assert_emitted("k1.hil.progress.v1")`. Assert `test_event.assert_published("k1.orchestration.task.completed.v1")`.
>   - **HIGH tier** (7.3.2): Script `mock_planner.script_plan(request_id, committed_plan)`. Call `result = await orchestrator.process(TaskEnvelope(tier=HIGH))`. Verify PendingPlanContext created. Fire `test_event.fire("k1.planner.plan.ready.v1", committed_plan_payload)`. Verify DAGExecutor wave-by-wave execution via `mock_fabric.call_log`. Assert `mock_bridge.assert_wal_written(dag_id, "PLAN_START")`. Assert `mock_bridge.assert_wal_written(dag_id, "DAG_COMPLETE")`.
>   - **Workflow** (7.3.3): Inject workflow via `test_storage.inject_workflow(spec)`. Call `result = await orchestrator.process(WorkflowRunRequest(workflow_id))`. Verify compile + execute. Assert `test_storage.assert_run_saved(workflow_id, "COMPLETED")`.
>   - **Cross-workflow** (7.3.4): Inject parent + child workflows. Script capability results for both. Process parent -> verify child triggered -> verify depth tracked -> verify results aggregated.
> - **All tests** use OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() with capture-mode adapters. Factory method returns tuple `(orchestrator, adapters)` where adapters is a namespace with named references to all test adapters for direct assertion access.
> - **Pattern**: Each test creates a full Orchestrator via factory, processes messages through public API, asserts via captured events + results + deltas.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Integration tests NEVER call `dag_executor.execute()` directly. NEVER call `step_runner.run()` directly. NEVER access internal state (pending_plans dict, pending_hil dict) except through public methods or events. Test assertions use adapter capture logs (emitted, call_log, published) -- NOT internal service state.

Verify AggregatedResult contains correct error_code + severity for Concierge to act on (Orchestrator does NOT decide tier degradation).

>   - **MCP re-discovery** (7.3A.4): Toggle `mock_fabric.register_capability()` availability -> fire `test_event.fire("k1.mcp.circuit_breaker.state_changed")` -> verify ConstraintResolver gap detection.

Verifies WB §10.10 edge case.
> - **Pattern**: Each test creates full Orchestrator, configures adapter behaviors, processes through public API, asserts via events + results + deltas + state inspection.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Tier degradation tests (7.3A.2) span the Concierge/Orchestrator boundary -- they test the Orchestrator's RESPONSE to CB states, NOT Concierge's CB management. MCP re-discovery tests (7.3A.4) simulate CB events via TestEventAdapter, NOT by actually crashing an MCP server. Interrupt tests (7.3A.5) use asyncio.create_task for concurrent message injection -- NOT threading.

>   - **Module contract** (7.4.1): Parse `k1/contracts/modules/orchestrator/module.contract.yaml` -> for each export name: `importlib.import_module("k1.orchestrator")` -> `getattr(module, export_name)` -> verify exists. For each type: verify instantiable with dummy args. For each port: verify has all declared methods via `hasattr()`. ~30 assertions.
>   - **Wiring contract** (7.4.2): Parse `k1/contracts/modules/orchestrator/wiring.contract.yaml` -> For each file path: `os.path.exists()` -> verify exists. For each port-adapter pair: `isinstance(adapter_instance, PortProtocol)` via `runtime_checkable Protocol`. For factory: `orch = await OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()` -> verify all 8 ports non-None. Forbidden pattern scan: `grep -r "IStateWritePort" k1/orchestrator/` returns empty (ORCH-01). ~40 assertions.
>   - **Invariant tests** (7.4.3): 16 tests, one per ORCH invariant. Each creates Orchestrator via factory -> exercises specific scenario -> asserts invariant holds. ORCH-01: verify port set has no write port. ORCH-02: concurrent messages -> only 1 DAG active. ORCH-09: trace_id on ALL events. ORCH-15: schema violation triggers retry. Etc.
>   - **Event catalog** (7.4.4): Parse event constants from `k1/orchestrator/events.py` (1.2.17) -> for each emitted event: run trigger scenario -> `test_event.assert_published(topic)`. For each consumed event: `test_event.fire(topic, payload)` -> verify handler invoked. ~35 bidirectional assertions.
> - **Pattern**: Contract tests are the **compliance gateway** -- they prove the implementation matches the declared contracts. Run in CI as blocking gate before merge.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Contract tests parse YAML contract files at runtime -- they do NOT hardcode contract values in Python. If the contract YAML changes, tests automatically verify the new contract. Invariant tests use the `assert_invariant_orch()` helper (7.0.3) which dispatches to per-invariant check functions. Event catalog tests verify BIDIRECTIONAL wiring (emitter -> subscriber AND subscriber -> handler).

>
> - **File locations**: `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_fabric_integration.py` (7.5.1), `test_sessionstate_integration.py` (7.5.2), `test_workflow_storage_integration.py` (7.5.3).
> - Cross-subsystem tests verify **real module boundaries** by replacing test adapters with real module instances.
> - **Per-test adapter replacement pattern** (uses `OrchestratorFactory.create_with_ports()`):
>   - **Fabric integration** (7.5.1): `real_fabric = await FabricFactory.create_for_testing()`. `orch = await OrchestratorFactory.create_with_ports(fabric_port=real_fabric)`. All other ports remain test adapters. Verifies CapabilityRequest/Result flow across real Fabric module boundary. Real provider resolution, real policy enforcement, real context build.
>   - **SessionState integration** (7.5.2): `real_state = await SessionStateFactory.create_for_testing()`. `orch = await OrchestratorFactory.create_with_ports(state_port=real_state)`. Verifies read-only context flow. Pre-populate real SessionState with beliefs/persona/safety_band data. Verify `snapshot()` returns real data in DAGContext.
>   - **WorkflowStorage integration** (7.5.3): `real_storage = WorkflowStorageAdapter(db_path=tmp_path / "test.db")`. `orch = await OrchestratorFactory.create_with_ports(workflow_storage_port=real_storage)`. Verifies SQLite persistence roundtrip. Uses pytest `tmp_path` fixture for isolated DB files.
> - **Pattern**: Tests use OrchestratorFactory.create_with_ports() to inject ONE real adapter while keeping others as test adapters. This isolates the cross-module boundary being tested.
> - **Depends on**: External module factories: `FabricFactory.create_for_testing()` (Fabric M6), `SessionStateFactory.create_for_testing()` (SessionState), `WorkflowStorageAdapter` (6.1.15 with real SQLite).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Cross-subsystem tests replace EXACTLY ONE adapter per test. Do NOT replace multiple real adapters simultaneously (too many variables). The real adapter must expose the same Protocol as the test adapter -- type safety enforced at `create_with_ports()`. SQLite integration tests use `tmp_path` (pytest built-in) for DB isolation -- do NOT use `:memory:` (WAL mode requires file).

### Epic 7.6: Lifecycle Tests

**Goal**: Test init, shutdown, and crash recovery sequences end-to-end.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 7.6.1 | Init lifecycle tests | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_lifecycle.py | Test 10-step init sequence from 6.2.2. Scenarios: (1) clean init with all ports -> all 10 steps complete, mailbox loop running, scheduler started, (2) verify port validation: remove one port -> init fails with clear error, (3) MCP discovery during init -> tools registered in Fabric via fabric_port, (4) MCP discovery slow (>500ms) -> init proceeds anyway (SPEC-10), tools registered async, (5) init with existing workflows -> list_active returns them, scheduler picks up triggers, (6) init idempotency: call init() twice -> second call is no-op (self._initialized guard), (7) init with crash recovery: WAL entries exist -> crash_recovery() runs before mailbox loop, (8) event subscription: verify all 12 consumed events subscribed via event_port. **Invariants verified**: ORCH-10 (WAL checked during init). ~18 tests. |
| 7.6.2 | Shutdown lifecycle tests | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_lifecycle.py | Test 9-step shutdown sequence from 6.2.3. Scenarios: (1) clean shutdown with no active DAG -> all ports disconnected in LIFO order, (2) shutdown with active DAG -> wait up to 30s for DAG completion, then proceed, (3) shutdown with DAG that exceeds 30s timeout -> force-compensate via saga, emit ABORTED result, (4) shutdown persists trigger state: verify next_fire/last_fire saved for all cron workflows, (5) shutdown with pending HIL contexts -> logged as warning (orphaned), (6) shutdown unsubscribes all events: verify event_port.unsubscribe() called for each subscription, (7) shutdown with partial init (some ports not connected) -> graceful handling (try/except per step), (8) final audit write: verify bridge_port.submit_audit() called with shutdown event. **Invariants verified**: ORCH-08 (saga compensation on forced shutdown). ~15 tests. |
| 7.6.3 | Crash recovery tests | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_lifecycle.py | Test crash_recovery() from 6.2.4. Scenarios: (1) no WAL entries -> clean start (skipped=0, recovered=0), (2) WAL with PLAN_START only (crash before any wave) -> resume from wave 0, (3) WAL with PLAN_START + WAVE_COMPLETE(0) -> resume from wave 1, (4) WAL with DAG_COMPLETE -> already finished, clean up WAL entry (skipped=1), (5) multiple WAL entries (3 crashed DAGs) -> recover all, report RecoveryResult(recovered=2, skipped=1, failed=0), (6) recovery error on one DAG -> log error, continue with others (failed_recoveries=1), (7) K0 offline (bridge_port.read_wal raises DEGRADED) -> skip recovery, log warning, proceed with clean start, (8) recovered DAG uses fresh state_port.snapshot() (stale crash-time context discarded), (9) crash_recovery runs BEFORE mailbox loop -> no race with incoming messages. **Invariants verified**: ORCH-10 (WAL recovery). ~15 tests. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: All lifecycle tests in `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_lifecycle.py` (7.6.1, 7.6.2, 7.6.3 share one file with test classes per lifecycle phase).
> - Lifecycle tests verify the **startup/shutdown/recovery sequences** defined in OrchestratorFactory (6.2.1) and OrchestratorService (6.2.2-6.2.4).
> - **Per-lifecycle adapter interaction**:
>   - **Init tests** (7.6.1): `orch = await OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()` (no auto-init). Call `await orch.init()` explicitly. Verify: (1) `test_event.assert_subscribed("k1.planner.plan.ready.v1")` -- all 12 consumed events subscribed. (2) `mock_fabric.assert_called("register_capability")` -- MCP discovery ran. (3) `test_storage.list_workflows()` returns pre-injected workflows. Remove one port -> `await orch.init()` raises ValueError with port name.
>   - **Shutdown tests** (7.6.2): Init first, then `await orch.shutdown()`. Verify: (1) `test_event.subscriptions` dict is empty (all unsubscribed). (2) `test_storage.trigger_state[wf_id]` updated (trigger persistence). (3) `mock_bridge.assert_audit_written(1)` (final audit). Active DAG during shutdown: script slow Fabric response -> verify 30s wait -> force-compensate -> `mock_bridge.assert_wal_written(dag_id, "ABORTED")`.
>   - **Crash recovery tests** (7.6.3): Pre-populate WAL: `mock_bridge.inject_wal(dag_id, [{"entry_type": "PLAN_START", ...}, {"entry_type": "WAVE_COMPLETE", "wave": 0, ...}])`. Call `await orch.init()` (crash_recovery runs inside init). Verify `mock_fabric.call_log` shows step execution starting from wave 1 (not wave 0). K0 offline: `mock_bridge.read_wal = raises AdapterError(DEGRADED)` -> verify recovery skipped, init proceeds.
> - **Init test ordering**: Port validation (step 1) -> crash_recovery (step 2-3) -> MCP discovery (step 4) -> workflow load (step 6) -> scheduler start (step 7) -> event subscribe (step 8) -> background tasks (step 9) -> mailbox loop (step 10). Tests verify this order via event/call log timestamps.
> - **Pattern**: Tests use OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() and manually call init()/shutdown()/crash_recovery() to verify each lifecycle phase independently.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: `create_for_testing()` does NOT call init() -- tests must call it explicitly. `create_production()` DOES call init() (not used in lifecycle tests). Shutdown with partial init uses try/except per step -- tests verify that partial shutdown doesn't crash even if some ports weren't connected. Crash recovery mock requires `inject_wal()` BEFORE init() call (recovery runs inside init step 2-3).

Fabric + Planner). 13 individual baselines from orchestrator.mmd measured separately: (1) mailbox dequeue + routing < 1ms -- time from dequeue to dispatch method entry, (2) DAG wave construction < 5ms -- time for Kahn's sort + wave grouping on 10-step plan, (3) CapReq construction < 1ms/step -- time to build CapabilityRequest from PlanStep, (4) param resolution < 1ms/step -- time to resolve $step_N.result references, (5) output schema validation < 1ms/step (ORCH-15) -- JSON schema validate time, (6) conditional edge eval < 1ms/wave (ORCH-16) -- edge condition evaluation time, (7) ~~token budget check < 0.1ms/wave (ORCH-14)~~ V1 REMOVED, (8) ~~quality gate check < 0.1ms/step~~ V1 REMOVED, (9) discovery heuristic < 1ms/wave (ORCH-13) -- micro-replan decision time, (10) result aggregation < 2ms -- merge N CapabilityResults into AggregatedResult, (11) progress delta emission < 1ms -- delta construction + emit, (12) constraint validation < 5ms -- full ConstraintResolver pass, (13) total overhead (sum of above) < 18ms P99. Methodology: 100 iterations, measure P50/P95/P99. Use OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() with zero-latency MockFabricAdapter (instant return). All benchmarks use real adapters. ~20 tests. |
| 7.7.2 | DAG wave construction benchmark | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_performance.py | Isolated wave construction benchmarks at increasing plan sizes. (1) 10-step plan: topological sort < 2ms, wave grouping < 1ms, total < 5ms P99, (2) 25-step plan: topological sort < 5ms, wave grouping < 2ms, total < 10ms P99, (3) 50-step plan: topological sort < 10ms, wave grouping < 5ms, total < 20ms P99. Per-step benchmarks: (4) param resolution < 1ms/step at all plan sizes, (5) wave dispatch overhead < 1ms (asyncio.gather setup time). Memory: (6) 50-step plan DAGContext memory < 1MB. Methodology: generate random DAG with N steps and random (acyclic) dependencies. 50 iterations per size, report P50/P95/P99. ~15 tests. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: All performance tests in `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_performance.py` (7.7.1 + 7.7.2 share one file with benchmark functions).
> - Performance tests validate **SLI targets** from orchestrator.mmd against real wired subsystems.
> - **Benchmark isolation pattern**:
>   - **Overhead benchmark** (7.7.1): `orch = await OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()`. Configure `MockFabricAdapter` to return INSTANTLY (0ms latency via pre-scripted results). Measure `time_start = time.perf_counter_ns()` -> `await orch.process(task_envelope)` -> `elapsed = time.perf_counter_ns() - time_start`. Elapsed = pure Orchestrator overhead (routing + DAG build + guard pipeline + aggregation). Assert `elapsed_p99 < 18_000_000` (18ms in nanoseconds). Repeat 100 iterations per scenario.
>   - **Wave construction benchmark** (7.7.2): Generate synthetic CommittedPlans with `_generate_random_dag(n_steps)` helper. Steps: 10, 25, 50. Measure DAGExecutor wave construction only (Kahn's sort + wave grouping). Targets: 10-step < 5ms, 25-step < 10ms, 50-step < 20ms P99. Memory: `tracemalloc.get_traced_memory()` after 50-step DAGContext build -> assert < 1MB.
> - **All benchmarks** use OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() with real adapters. Results feed into Epic 8.1 SLI compliance.
> - **Depends on**: SLI definitions (8.1.1) for target values. MockFabricAdapter (6.1.9) for zero-latency isolation.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Performance tests measure WALL CLOCK time via `time.perf_counter_ns()`, NOT CPU time. The 18ms target is for Orchestrator OVERHEAD only -- Fabric and Planner latency is excluded by using zero-latency mock adapters. Do NOT use `time.time()` (insufficient resolution for sub-ms measurement). P99 is computed from 100+ iterations sorted -- do NOT use mean (hides tail latency). Memory measurement uses `tracemalloc` -- do NOT estimate from object count.

## Milestone 8: SLO/SLI + Observability

> **Goal**: Define SLIs/SLOs, implement metrics collection, tracing, and alerting. These are the measurement infrastructure that validates all 13 latency baselines from orchestrator.mmd and operational health of the Orchestrator.
>
> **Pattern**: Same approach as Fabric M7.

### Epic 8.1: SLI Definition

**Goal**: Define measurable service level indicators and objectives for all Orchestrator critical paths.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 8.1.1 | Define Orchestrator SLIs | TODO | k1/orchestrator/docs/sli.md | Define all measurable SLIs sourced from orchestrator.mmd. **13 latency SLIs** (system-owned baselines): (1) mailbox dequeue + routing < 1ms P99, (2) DAG wave construction < 5ms P99, (3) CapReq construction < 1ms/step P99, (4) param resolution < 1ms/step P99, (5) output schema validation < 1ms/step P99 (ORCH-15), (6) conditional edge eval < 1ms/wave P99 (ORCH-16), (7) ~~token budget check < 0.1ms/wave P99 (ORCH-14)~~ V1 REMOVED, (8) ~~quality gate check < 0.1ms/step P99~~ V1 REMOVED, (9) discovery heuristic < 1ms/wave P99 (ORCH-13), (10) result aggregation < 2ms P99, (11) progress delta emission < 1ms P99, (12) constraint validation < 5ms P99, (13) total Orchestrator overhead < 18ms P99 (sum of above, excl. Fabric + Planner). **Budget envelope SLIs**: MEDIUM tier e2e < 10s (incl. Fabric), HIGH tier e2e < 45s (incl. Planner + Fabric). **Operational SLIs**: step success rate (target > 95%), DAG completion rate (target > 90%), retry rate (target < 10% of steps), saga compensation rate (target < 5% of DAGs), workflow trigger precision (cron fires within 1s of scheduled time), HIL response rate (target > 80% within 30s timeout). Format: YAML section in policies equivalent or standalone sli.md. Each SLI includes: name, description, target, measurement_point, unit. |
| 8.1.2 | Implement SLI measurement hooks | TODO | k1/orchestrator/metrics.py | **Class**: `OrchestratorMetrics` with context manager timing helpers. **Instrumentation points**: (1) `time_dequeue()` wraps mailbox.dequeue() in _mailbox_loop, (2) `time_route()` wraps _process_one() dispatch logic, (3) `time_dag_build()` wraps DAGExecutor wave construction (Kahn's sort + grouping), (4) `time_wave_dispatch()` wraps asyncio.gather for each wave, (5) `time_step_execution()` wraps StepRunner.execute() per step, (6) `time_guard_pipeline()` wraps pre/post guard chain per step/wave, (7) `time_constraint_validation()` wraps ConstraintResolver.resolve(), (8) `time_aggregation()` wraps result merge in AggregatedResult construction, (9) `time_param_resolution()` wraps $step_N.result resolution, (10) `time_total_overhead()` wraps full process() call (excl. adapter latency via subtraction). **Pattern**: context managers record start/end timestamps, emit to metrics collector. Zero-overhead when metrics disabled (check flag before entering context). **Gotcha**: time_total_overhead must subtract adapter wait time (Fabric + Planner latency) to measure pure Orchestrator overhead. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: `k1/orchestrator/docs/sli.md` (8.1.1), `k1/orchestrator/metrics.py` (8.1.2).
> - SLI definitions (8.1.1) are the **source of truth** for performance test targets (7.7.x), alert thresholds (8.4.1), and SLO compliance validation (9.4.1).
> - **Measurement hook -> SLI mapping** (8.1.2 wraps these hot paths):
>   - `time_dequeue()` -> wraps `IMailboxPort.dequeue()` in `OrchestratorService._mailbox_loop()` (2.1.1) -> feeds SLI #1 (< 1ms).
>   - `time_route()` -> wraps `_process_one()` dispatch logic in OrchestratorService (2.1.1) -> feeds SLI #1.
>   - `time_dag_build()` -> wraps `DAGExecutor._build_waves()` Kahn's sort (2.2.1) -> feeds SLI #2 (< 5ms).
>   - `time_wave_dispatch()` -> wraps `asyncio.gather(*step_coros)` in DAGExecutor (2.2.1) -> feeds SLI #4.
>   - `time_step_execution()` -> wraps `StepRunner.execute()` (2.2.3) -> feeds SLI #3, #5, #8.
>   - `time_guard_pipeline()` -> wraps `_run_guards(guards, context)` pre/post chains (3.2.x) -> feeds SLI #7, #8, #9.
>   - `time_constraint_validation()` -> wraps `ConstraintResolver.resolve()` (3.1.1) -> feeds SLI #12 (< 5ms).
>   - `time_aggregation()` -> wraps `AggregatedResult` merge in OrchestratorService (2.1.1) -> feeds SLI #10 (< 2ms).
>   - `time_param_resolution()` -> wraps `$step_N.result` resolution in StepRunner (2.2.3) -> feeds SLI #4 (< 1ms/step).
>   - `time_total_overhead()` -> wraps full `process()` call, subtracts adapter wait -> feeds SLI #13 (< 18ms).
> - **OrchestratorMetrics constructor deps**: (1) `MetricsConfig` (from config.py -- flag to enable/disable), (2) `MetricsCollector` (histogram/counter/gauge backend). Injected at factory step (after OrchestratorService, before lifecycle init).
> - **Zero-overhead guard**: Each `time_*()` context manager checks `self._enabled` flag BEFORE entering `time.perf_counter_ns()`. When disabled, context manager is a no-op (no timing, no recording).
> - **Consumed by**: Performance benchmarks (7.7.x) for target validation, alerting rules (8.4.1) for threshold triggering, dashboards for operational visibility.
> - **Depends on**: All core services (M2-M5) must be implemented before instrumentation wiring.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: `time_total_overhead()` MUST subtract Fabric + Planner adapter latency. Implementation: record adapter call start/end separately, subtract from total. Do NOT measure wall clock end-to-end and claim it is overhead. SLI doc format is YAML-like with `name`, `target`, `measurement_point`, `unit` fields per SLI -- do NOT invent new formats.

### Epic 8.2: Metrics Emission

**Goal**: Implement counters, histograms, and gauges for operational monitoring.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 8.2.1 | Implement Orchestrator metrics | TODO | k1/orchestrator/metrics.py | **Histograms** (latency distributions): (1) `orchestrator.dag.duration_ms` -- total DAG execution time (incl. Fabric), tagged by tier (MEDIUM/HIGH), (2) `orchestrator.dag.overhead_ms` -- Orchestrator-only overhead per DAG (excl. adapter latency), (3) `orchestrator.step.duration_ms` -- per-step execution time, tagged by step_type (agent/tool/prompt), (4) `orchestrator.wave.construction_ms` -- wave build time (Kahn's sort), (5) `orchestrator.constraint.resolution_ms` -- ConstraintResolver pass time. **Counters** (cumulative events): (6) `orchestrator.dag.completed_total` -- DAGs completed (tagged success/failed/interrupted), (7) `orchestrator.step.retry_total` -- step retries (tagged by reason: transient/schema/quality), (8) `orchestrator.saga.compensation_total` -- saga compensations triggered, (9) `orchestrator.workflow.trigger_total` -- workflow triggers fired (tagged cron/event/manual), (10) `orchestrator.error.total` -- errors by classification (transient/permanent/circuit_open/not_found/internal), (11) `orchestrator.hil.request_total` -- HIL requests (tagged responded/timed_out), (12) `orchestrator.mailbox.processed_total` -- messages processed by type. **Gauges** (current state): (13) `orchestrator.dag.active` -- 0 or 1 (concurrency guard state), (14) `orchestrator.mailbox.depth` -- current mailbox queue depth, (15) `orchestrator.workflow.active_count` -- number of enabled workflows, (16) `orchestrator.mcp.registered_capabilities` -- number of MCP tool capabilities registered via ConnectorLifecycleManager's thin discovery layer (tracks Orchestrator's registration state, NOT Fabric's provider health -- Fabric's HealthChecker owns connection tracking), (17) `orchestrator.pending_plans` -- number of PendingPlanContexts awaiting Planner response, (18) `orchestrator.pending_hil` -- number of PendingHILContexts awaiting user response. **Emission pattern**: metrics collected in-process via OrchestratorMetrics singleton, exportable via pull (Prometheus-compatible) or push (structured log). |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/orchestrator/metrics.py` (extends OrchestratorMetrics from 8.1.2 with full emission).
> - **Histogram -> Hook mapping** (8.2.1 items 1-5):
>   - `orchestrator.dag.duration_ms` <- recorded at `DAGExecutor.execute()` completion (2.2.1), tagged `tier=MEDIUM|HIGH`.
>   - `orchestrator.dag.overhead_ms` <- recorded by `time_total_overhead()` (8.1.2), adapter latency subtracted.
>   - `orchestrator.step.duration_ms` <- recorded by `time_step_execution()` (8.1.2), tagged `step_type=agent|tool|prompt`.
>   - `orchestrator.wave.construction_ms` <- recorded by `time_dag_build()` (8.1.2).
>   - `orchestrator.constraint.resolution_ms` <- recorded by `time_constraint_validation()` (8.1.2).
> - **Counter -> Emission point mapping** (8.2.1 items 6-12):
>   - `orchestrator.dag.completed_total` <- incremented at `DAGExecutor.execute()` return, tagged `status=success|failed|interrupted`.
>   - `orchestrator.step.retry_total` <- incremented at `StepRunner._retry()` (2.2.3), tagged `reason=transient|schema|quality`.
>   - `orchestrator.saga.compensation_total` <- incremented at `SagaCompensator.compensate()` (2.3.2).
>   - `orchestrator.workflow.trigger_total` <- incremented at `WorkflowScheduler._fire_trigger()` (4.2.1), tagged `type=cron|event|manual`.
>   - `orchestrator.error.total` <- incremented at `ErrorRouter.classify()` (2.3.1), tagged by classification.
>   - `orchestrator.hil.request_total` <- incremented at HIL request creation in ConstraintResolver (3.1.3), tagged `outcome=responded|timed_out`.
>   - `orchestrator.mailbox.processed_total` <- incremented at `OrchestratorService._process_one()` return, tagged by message type.
> - **Gauge -> State transition mapping** (8.2.1 items 13-18):
>   - `orchestrator.dag.active` <- `ConcurrencyGuard.before_wave()` sets 1, `after_wave()` (last wave) sets 0.
>   - `orchestrator.mailbox.depth` <- `IMailboxPort.enqueue()` increments, `dequeue()` decrements.
>   - `orchestrator.workflow.active_count` <- `WorkflowRegistry.save()` increments, `delete()` decrements.
>   - `orchestrator.mcp.registered_capabilities` <- `ConnectorLifecycleManager.discover_and_register()` increments per registered capability_id, `unregister_server()` decrements. Tracks Orchestrator's registration state (how many tools were bridged to Fabric), NOT Fabric's provider connection state.
>   - `orchestrator.pending_plans` <- `PendingPlanContext` creation increments, removal (plan received or timeout) decrements.
>   - `orchestrator.pending_hil` <- `PendingHILContext` creation increments, removal (response or timeout) decrements.
> - **Injection pattern**: OrchestratorMetrics singleton created at factory step (between port wiring and lifecycle init). Injected into OrchestratorService, DAGExecutor, StepRunner, ErrorRouter, SagaCompensator, WorkflowScheduler, ConnectorLifecycleManager via constructor parameter.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Metrics must use EXACTLY the metric names listed above (dot-separated, snake_case). Tags are keyword args to record/increment calls. Histogram records MILLISECONDS (float), not nanoseconds. Counters are monotonic (never reset except process restart). Gauges can go up and down. Do NOT create new metric names beyond the 18 defined here without updating sli.md.

### Epic 8.3: Structured Tracing

**Goal**: Implement distributed tracing and structured logging for request correlation.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 8.3.1 | Implement cognitive_trace_id propagation | TODO | k1/orchestrator/tracing.py | Enforce ORCH-09 (all events carry trace_id). **Propagation chain**: (1) TaskEnvelope arrives with cognitive_trace_id -> stored in processing context, (2) PlanRequest emitted to Planner carries trace_id, (3) CommittedPlan received -> correlated by plan_id + trace_id, (4) each CapabilityRequest to Fabric carries trace_id, (5) each CapabilityResult from Fabric carries trace_id, (6) AggregatedResult carries trace_id, (7) all deltas (progress, error, diagnostic) carry trace_id, (8) WAL entries carry trace_id (for crash recovery correlation), (9) workflow events carry trace_id (WorkflowRunRequest generates fresh trace_id if triggered by cron, propagates parent trace_id if triggered by parent workflow). **Structured log format**: JSON per operation: `{"timestamp": ..., "level": ..., "component": "orchestrator.{phase}", "trace_id": ..., "request_id": ..., "tier": ..., "step_id": ..., "wave": ..., "duration_ms": ..., "success": ...}`. **Phases logged**: dequeue, route, dispatch_medium, dispatch_high, dag_start, wave_start, step_start, step_end, wave_end, dag_end, aggregate, error, interrupt, workflow_compile, workflow_execute, constraint_resolve, guard_evaluate. **Gotcha**: Workflow cron triggers have no parent trace_id -- generate fresh UUID. Cross-workflow triggers propagate parent trace_id as `parent_trace_id` field. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/orchestrator/tracing.py` (8.3.1).
> - **cognitive_trace_id propagation chain** (8.3.1) -- each step passes trace_id to next:
>   1. `OrchestratorService.process(envelope)` extracts `envelope.cognitive_trace_id` -> stores in `ProcessingContext.trace_id`.
>   2. `dispatch_medium()` / `dispatch_high()` pass `ProcessingContext` (with trace_id) to all downstream calls.
>   3. `PlanRequest` to Planner: `plan_request.trace_id = ctx.trace_id`.
>   4. `DAGExecutor.execute(committed_plan, ctx)` propagates `ctx.trace_id` to wave/step context.
>   5. `StepRunner.execute(step, ctx)` passes `ctx.trace_id` in every `CapabilityRequest.trace_id` to Fabric.
>   6. `CapabilityResult` from Fabric carries `trace_id` back -> correlated in step result.
>   7. All guard `evaluate(ctx)` calls receive `ctx.trace_id` for guard-level logging.
>   8. `ErrorRouter.classify(error, ctx)` logs with `ctx.trace_id`.
>   9. `SagaCompensator.compensate(records, ctx)` logs compensation with `ctx.trace_id`.
>   10. `IDeltaEmitPort.emit(delta)` includes `delta.trace_id = ctx.trace_id` on every progress/error/diagnostic delta.
>   11. `IBridgeWritePort.write_wal(entry)` includes `entry.trace_id = ctx.trace_id`.
>   12. `IEventSubscriptionPort.publish(event)` includes `event.trace_id = ctx.trace_id` on all 20 emitted events.
> - **Workflow trace_id generation**: Cron-triggered workflows have NO parent trace_id -> `WorkflowExecutor.execute()` generates fresh `uuid4()`. Cross-workflow triggers propagate `parent_trace_id` field: `child_request.parent_trace_id = parent_ctx.trace_id`. Event-triggered workflows extract trace_id from triggering event.
> - **Structured log fields** (JSON per log entry): `timestamp` (ISO 8601), `level` (DEBUG/INFO/WARN/ERROR), `component` (`orchestrator.{phase}`), `trace_id`, `request_id` (envelope.id), `tier` (MEDIUM/HIGH), `step_id` (if applicable), `wave` (if applicable), `duration_ms` (if timed), `success` (bool). Phase identifiers: dequeue, route, dispatch_medium, dispatch_high, dag_start, wave_start, step_start, step_end, wave_end, dag_end, aggregate, error, interrupt, workflow_compile, workflow_execute, constraint_resolve, guard_evaluate.
> - **Depends on**: All core services (M2-M5) accept `ProcessingContext` parameter containing trace_id. TestEventAdapter (6.1.14) captures trace_id on all published events for test verification.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: trace_id is ALWAYS a UUID string (not int). It flows through `ProcessingContext`, NOT as a separate parameter. Every emit/publish/write call MUST include trace_id -- if you see a call without it, it's a bug. Workflow cron trace_id generation happens in `WorkflowExecutor`, NOT in `WorkflowScheduler` (scheduler fires the trigger, executor starts the execution context).

### Epic 8.4: Alerting

**Goal**: Define alert rules for SLO violations and operational anomalies.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 8.4.1 | Define alert thresholds | TODO | k1/orchestrator/docs/alerts.md | Define alert rules consuming metrics from 8.2.1. **Latency alerts**: (1) `OrchestratorHighOverhead` -- overhead_ms P99 > 18ms sustained 5min (SLI 8.1.1 item 13 violation), (2) `DAGWaveConstructionSlow` -- wave.construction_ms P99 > 10ms sustained 5min. **Error rate alerts**: (3) `DAGFailureRateHigh` -- dag.completed_total(failed) / dag.completed_total(*) > 5% over 15min, (4) `StepRetryRateHigh` -- step.retry_total / step.completed_total > 10% over 15min, (5) `SagaCompensationSpike` -- saga.compensation_total > 3 in 5min window. **Operational alerts**: (6) `WorkflowGapDetected` -- ProactiveGap event emitted (capability removed affecting active workflow), (7) `TokenBudgetExhausted` -- token budget HARD_STOP triggered (counter increment), (8) `HILTimeoutRate` -- hil.request_total(timed_out) / hil.request_total(*) > 20% over 30min, (9) `CircuitBreakerOpen` -- CB_PLANNER (Orchestrator-owned) open > 2min (CB_FABRIC, CB_MCP are owned by Concierge/Fabric -- their CB alerts belong in those modules' alert definitions), (10) `MailboxBackpressure` -- mailbox.depth gauge > 80% of max (mailbox_depth config) sustained 1min, (11) `PendingPlanLeak` -- pending_plans gauge > 0 for > 60s without change (possible orphan PendingPlanContext), (12) `MCPCapabilityCountDropped` -- orchestrator.mcp.registered_capabilities gauge drops below expected count (triggered when ConnectorLifecycleManager receives Fabric health event indicating MCP provider OFFLINE, causing unregister of that server's capabilities). NOTE: actual MCP disconnect detection is Fabric HealthChecker's responsibility -- this alert fires on the Orchestrator's registration state change, not on transport-level disconnect. Per alert: severity (warning/critical), threshold, window, description, runbook_link. Format: YAML alert definitions. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/orchestrator/docs/alerts.md` (8.4.1 -- YAML alert definitions).
> - **Alert -> Metric -> SLI chain** (each alert references exactly one metric and one SLI):
>   - `OrchestratorHighOverhead` -> metric `orchestrator.dag.overhead_ms` P99 (8.2.1 #2) -> SLI #13 (8.1.1) -> threshold 18ms, window 5min, severity CRITICAL.
>   - `DAGWaveConstructionSlow` -> metric `orchestrator.wave.construction_ms` P99 (8.2.1 #4) -> SLI #2 (8.1.1) -> threshold 10ms, window 5min, severity WARNING.
>   - `DAGFailureRateHigh` -> metric ratio `orchestrator.dag.completed_total(failed) / total` (8.2.1 #6) -> threshold 5%, window 15min, severity CRITICAL.
>   - `StepRetryRateHigh` -> metric ratio `orchestrator.step.retry_total / step.completed_total` (8.2.1 #7) -> threshold 10%, window 15min, severity WARNING.
>   - `SagaCompensationSpike` -> metric `orchestrator.saga.compensation_total` (8.2.1 #8) -> threshold > 3 in 5min, severity WARNING.
>   - `WorkflowGapDetected` -> event `k1.orchestrator.proactive_gap.detected.v1` (consumed, not metric-based) -> severity WARNING.
>   - `TokenBudgetExhausted` -> counter increment for HARD_STOP from TokenBudgetGuard (3.2.6) -> severity CRITICAL.
>   - `HILTimeoutRate` -> metric ratio `orchestrator.hil.request_total(timed_out) / total` (8.2.1 #11) -> threshold 20%, window 30min, severity WARNING.
>   - `CircuitBreakerOpen` -> CB_PLANNER state (Orchestrator-owned, from PlannerAdapter 6.1.3) via `orchestrator.cb.planner.state` gauge -> severity CRITICAL if OPEN > 2min sustained. NOTE: CB_FABRIC/CB_MCP alerts belong in Concierge/Fabric alert definitions respectively.
>   - `MailboxBackpressure` -> gauge `orchestrator.mailbox.depth` (8.2.1 #14) -> threshold > 80% of `mailbox_depth` config, window 1min, severity WARNING.
>   - `PendingPlanLeak` -> gauge `orchestrator.pending_plans` (8.2.1 #17) -> threshold > 0 for > 60s without change, severity WARNING.
>   - `MCPCapabilityCountDropped` -> gauge `orchestrator.mcp.registered_capabilities` (8.2.1 #16) drops below expected count after Fabric health event receipt, severity WARNING.
> - **Alert -> Runbook mapping**: Each alert YAML entry includes `runbook_link` field pointing to specific procedure in `orchestrator_runbook.md` (9.3.3). Map: OrchestratorHighOverhead -> procedure #2 (debug stuck DAG), DAGFailureRateHigh -> procedure #3 (force-cancel), CircuitBreakerOpen -> procedure #7 (CB_PLANNER management), MailboxBackpressure -> procedure #1 (drain mailbox), PendingPlanLeak -> procedure #2 (debug stuck), MCPCapabilityCountDropped -> procedure #4 (trigger ConnectorLifecycleManager refresh).
> - **Depends on**: Metrics (8.2.x) for metric values, SLIs (8.1.x) for threshold derivation, Runbook (9.3.3) for procedure links.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Alert YAML format: `name`, `metric`, `condition`, `threshold`, `window`, `severity`, `description`, `runbook_link`. `WorkflowGapDetected` is EVENT-BASED not metric-based -- it fires on event subscription, not gauge threshold. `CircuitBreakerOpen` likewise is event-based. Do NOT create an alert without a matching metric or event source.

---

## Milestone 9: Production Readiness

> **Goal**: Validate production-level quality through load testing, chaos testing, documentation, and final checklist.
>
> **Pattern**: Same approach as Fabric M8.

### Epic 9.1: Load Testing

**Goal**: Stress-test Orchestrator under concurrency pressure and at scale to validate SLI targets hold.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 9.1.1 | Test concurrent DAG executions | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_load_testing.py | Stress-test mailbox + concurrency guard under rapid-fire messages. Scenarios: (1) 10 concurrent TaskEnvelopes(MEDIUM) -> only 1 DAG active at a time (ORCH-02), remaining 9 deferred -> all 10 eventually processed, (2) 50 concurrent TaskEnvelopes -> measure total throughput (messages/sec), queue depth never exceeds mailbox_depth config, (3) 100 concurrent TaskEnvelopes -> measure P50/P95/P99 latency from enqueue to AggregatedResult, verify error rate < 1%, (4) mixed priorities: 50 REALTIME + 50 INTERACTIVE -> verify REALTIME processed first, INTERACTIVE not starved (all complete within 2x expected time). **Targets**: throughput > 10 messages/sec sustained, P95 latency < 500ms overhead per message (excl. Fabric), zero message loss. Methodology: 3 runs each, report median. Use OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing() with zero-latency MockFabricAdapter. ~12 tests. |
| 9.1.2 | Test large DAG plans | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_load_testing.py | Stress-test DAGExecutor with increasing plan complexity. Scenarios: (1) 10-step plan (3 waves) -> wave construction < 5ms, total overhead < 18ms P99, (2) 25-step plan (8 waves) -> wave construction < 10ms P99, total overhead < 30ms P99, (3) 50-step plan (15 waves) -> wave construction < 20ms P99, total overhead < 50ms P99, (4) 50-step plan with all guards active (5 guards per step, V1) -> measure guard pipeline overhead per step, (5) memory profiling: verify 50-step DAG context < 1MB heap. **Methodology**: generate synthetic CommittedPlans with N steps and random acyclic dependencies. 50 iterations per size, report P50/P95/P99. MockFabricAdapter returns instant results (isolate Orchestrator overhead). ~10 tests. |
| 9.1.3 | Test workflow scheduler at scale | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_load_testing.py | Stress-test WorkflowScheduler with large workflow registries. Scenarios: (1) 100 active workflows with cron triggers (various intervals: 1min, 5min, hourly) -> verify all triggers fire within 1s of scheduled time, (2) 500 active workflows -> same precision target, measure scheduler CPU overhead, (3) 1000 active workflows -> verify no trigger drift > 2s, scheduler loop completes within 100ms, (4) rapid workflow save/delete while scheduler running (50 concurrent mutations) -> no crashes, triggers updated correctly. **Targets**: trigger precision < 1s at 1000 workflows, scheduler loop overhead < 100ms. Use TestWorkflowStorageAdapter (6.1.16) with in-memory SQLite. ~10 tests. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: All load tests in `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_load_testing.py` (9.1.1 + 9.1.2 + 9.1.3 share one file).
> - Load tests verify wiring **holds under concurrency pressure**. All tests use `OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()`.
> - **Per-scenario adapter configuration**:
>   - **Concurrent DAGs** (9.1.1): `MockFabricAdapter` configured with `script_result(latency_ms=0)` for ALL steps -> isolates Orchestrator overhead. Test spawns N `asyncio.Task` each calling `orch.process(TaskEnvelope(...))`. Verify: (1) `mock_fabric.call_count == total_steps_across_all_DAGs` (no lost calls). (2) `ConcurrencyGuard` ensures exactly 1 active DAG (`orchestrator.dag.active` gauge never > 1). (3) mailbox depth gauge tracked per-enqueue via `test_mailbox.depth_history` -> max depth < `mailbox_depth` config. (4) No message loss: `len(results) == N`. Mixed priority (REALTIME + INTERACTIVE): verify `test_mailbox.dequeue_order` shows all REALTIME before INTERACTIVE.
>   - **Large DAGs** (9.1.2): Generate synthetic `CommittedPlan` via `_generate_random_dag(n_steps=N, max_deps=3)` helper function. Helper: creates N PlanSteps with random acyclic `depends_on` lists (no cycles guaranteed by only depending on lower-numbered steps). `MockFabricAdapter` returns instantly. Measure DAGExecutor wave construction separately: `start = time.perf_counter_ns()` -> `waves = dag_executor._build_waves(plan)` -> `elapsed`. Memory: `tracemalloc.start()` before DAGContext build -> `tracemalloc.get_traced_memory()[1]` (peak) -> assert < 1MB for 50-step.
>   - **Scheduler at scale** (9.1.3): `TestWorkflowStorageAdapter` (6.1.16) pre-loaded with N workflow specs (100, 500, 1000). Each workflow has cron trigger with varying intervals. Measure: `start = time.perf_counter_ns()` -> `await scheduler._tick()` -> `elapsed`. Verify `elapsed < 100_000_000` (100ms in ns) for 1000 workflows. Trigger precision: record `scheduled_fire_time` vs `actual_fire_time` -> assert `abs(actual - scheduled) < 1.0` (1 second). Concurrent mutations (50 save/delete during tick): verify no `RuntimeError` from dict mutation during iteration.
> - **Benchmark methodology**: 3 runs per scenario, report MEDIAN. For latency: collect 100 iterations into `sorted_times`, P50=`times[49]`, P95=`times[94]`, P99=`times[98]`.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Load tests use `OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()` -- NOT `create_production()` (production calls init() which starts background tasks that interfere with precise measurement). `time.perf_counter_ns()` for ALL timing (nanosecond resolution). Synthetic DAG helper MUST guarantee acyclic graph (depend only on lower-numbered steps). Do NOT use `asyncio.sleep()` to simulate load -- real concurrent `process()` calls via `asyncio.gather()`. Memory tests use `tracemalloc` -- do NOT estimate.

### Epic 9.2: Chaos Testing

**Goal**: Verify graceful degradation when wired dependencies fail unexpectedly.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 9.2.1 | Test Fabric unavailability | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_chaos.py | Simulate Fabric circuit breaker tripping mid-DAG. Scenarios: (1) DAG wave_0 succeeds -> CB_FABRIC opens before wave_1 -> StepRunner gets DEGRADED CapabilityResult for all wave_1 steps, (2) ErrorRouter classifies CB_FABRIC -> tier degradation path: remaining steps attempted with degraded expectations, (3) saga compensation: completed side-effect steps from wave_0 compensated -> CompensationRecords created, (4) partial AggregatedResult returned with wave_0 results + wave_1 failures, (5) CB_FABRIC recovers (HALF_OPEN -> CLOSED) -> next DAG executes normally. Verify events: dag_started, wave_completed(0), step_failed(wave_1 steps), saga_compensated, dag_completed(PARTIAL). ~8 tests. |
| 9.2.2 | Test Planner unavailability | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_chaos.py | Simulate Planner circuit breaker open during HIGH tier dispatch. Scenarios: (1) TaskEnvelope(HIGH) -> dispatch_high -> PlanRequest to MockPlannerAdapter -> adapter raises CB_PLANNER_OPEN, (2) ErrorRouter detects planner unavailable -> degrade HIGH to MEDIUM path (skip planning, split TaskEnvelope directly to Fabric calls), (3) degraded MEDIUM execution succeeds -> AggregatedResult with degradation_notice, (4) CB_PLANNER recovers -> next HIGH tier uses normal Planner path, (5) Planner slow (timeout > 30s) -> same degradation path as CB open. Verify: degradation delta emitted, trace_id propagated through degraded path. ~7 tests. |
| 9.2.3 | Test mid-DAG interrupts under load | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_chaos.py | Stress-test interrupt handling with concurrent activity. Scenarios: (1) 3-wave DAG executing -> InterruptRequest during wave_1 -> wave_1 steps complete, wave_2/3 skipped, (2) interrupt arrives between waves (wave_0 complete, wave_1 not started) -> clean exit with wave_0 results, (3) interrupt arrives during saga compensation (step failed, compensation in progress) -> compensation completes before interrupt acknowledged, (4) rapid interrupts (3 InterruptRequests in 100ms) -> idempotent handling, only first processed, (5) interrupt + new TaskEnvelope arriving simultaneously -> interrupt processed first (InterruptRequest bypasses ConcurrencyGuard), new TaskEnvelope queued. ~8 tests. |
| 9.2.4 | Test crash recovery under chaos | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_chaos.py | Simulate crash at various DAG execution points. Scenarios: (1) crash after PLAN_START WAL but before wave_0 -> recovery resumes from wave 0, (2) crash after WAVE_COMPLETE(0) but before wave_1 -> recovery resumes from wave 1 with wave_0 MergedResults reconstructed from WAL, (3) crash during wave execution (wave_1 partially complete) -> recovery resumes from wave_1 start (incomplete steps re-executed), (4) crash during saga compensation -> recovery detects partial compensation, completes remaining compensations, (5) crash with multiple in-flight contexts (PendingPlanContext + active DAG) -> recovery handles DAG via WAL, orphan PendingPlanContext cleaned up (no Planner response expected after crash), (6) K0 offline during recovery (bridge_port.read_wal returns DEGRADED) -> skip recovery, clean start. ~10 tests. |
| 9.2.5 | Test concurrent workflow trigger | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_chaos.py | Simulate race condition with cron-triggered workflows. Scenarios: (1) workflow cron fires -> DAG starts -> same workflow cron fires again before DAG completes -> previous run aborted (per ADR-1.1.11 Q5), (2) aborted run: saga compensation for in-flight side-effect steps -> CompensationRecords, (3) new run starts fresh with clean DAGContext (no stale state from aborted run), (4) 3 rapid cron fires for same workflow in 5s window -> only most recent executes, previous 2 aborted, (5) different workflows fire simultaneously -> both execute (concurrency guard queues second, processes after first completes). ~8 tests. |
| 9.2.6 | Test MCP server disconnect during step | TODO | tests/k1/orchestrator/test_chaos.py | Simulate MCP server failure mid-step execution. Scenarios: (1) step dispatches to MCP tool -> MCP server disconnects -> StepRunner gets connection error -> ErrorRouter classifies as TRANSIENT -> retry (max 2), (2) retry also fails -> CB_MCP trips -> step marked FAILED, (3) DAG continues with remaining steps (non-MCP steps unaffected), (4) MCP-dependent step in later wave -> skipped due to CB_MCP open, (5) ConnectorLifecycleManager lifecycle monitoring detects MCP server back via Fabric health event -> tools re-registered via refresh(), CB_MCP half-open -> next execution succeeds, (6) fallback: if local MCP alternative exists -> ConstraintResolver substitutes local tool. ~8 tests. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: All chaos tests in `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_chaos.py` (9.2.1-9.2.6 share one file with test classes per failure scenario).
> - Chaos tests verify **graceful degradation** when wired dependencies fail. All use `OrchestratorFactory.create_for_testing()`.
> - **Per-scenario adapter failure injection** (each test configures adapter to fail at specific point):
>   - **Fabric unavailability** (9.2.1): `mock_fabric = MockFabricAdapter()`. `mock_fabric.script_results([SUCCESS, SUCCESS, RAISE(AdapterError("CB_FABRIC_OPEN"))])` -- wave_0 succeeds, wave_1 raises. Verify chain: StepRunner catches error -> `ErrorRouter.classify()` returns `CIRCUIT_OPEN` -> tier degradation path -> `SagaCompensator.compensate(wave_0_side_effects)` -> `CompensationRecord` created -> `test_bridge.assert_wal_written(dag_id, "PARTIAL")`. Recovery: `mock_fabric.script_results([SUCCESS])` (CB half-open) -> next DAG succeeds.
>   - **Planner unavailability** (9.2.2): `mock_planner = MockPlannerAdapter()`. `mock_planner.raise_on_call(PlannerError("CB_PLANNER_OPEN"))`. `orch.process(TaskEnvelope(tier=HIGH))` -> dispatch_high() -> PlanRequest fails -> ErrorRouter detects planner down -> degrade to MEDIUM (`dispatch_medium()` directly). Verify: `test_delta.assert_emitted("degradation_notice")`, `mock_fabric.assert_called()` (MEDIUM path used Fabric directly), `trace_id` preserved through degraded path.
>   - **Mid-DAG interrupts** (9.2.3): Start 3-wave DAG. After wave_0 complete, inject `InterruptRequest` via `orch.process(InterruptRequest(cancel=True))`. Verify: wave_1 in-progress steps complete (StepRunner respects in-progress step), wave_2/3 skipped, saga compensates wave_0 side-effects. Idempotency: send 3 InterruptRequests in 100ms -> `mock_bridge.wal_entries` shows exactly ONE interrupt event. ConcurrencyGuard bypass: InterruptRequest processed even while DAG active.
>   - **Crash recovery** (9.2.4): `mock_bridge = MockBridgeAdapter()`. Pre-populate WAL: `mock_bridge.inject_wal(dag_id, [{"entry_type": "PLAN_START", "committed_plan": {...}}, {"entry_type": "WAVE_COMPLETE", "wave": 0, "merged_results": [...]}])`. Call `await orch.init()` -> crash_recovery() reads WAL -> reconstructs DAGContext with wave_0 results -> resumes from wave_1. Verify: `mock_fabric.call_log[0].wave == 1` (not wave_0). Partial compensation: inject WAL with COMPENSATION_START but no COMPENSATION_COMPLETE -> recovery completes remaining compensations.
>   - **Concurrent workflow** (9.2.5): Register workflow with 5-second cron. Start DAG from trigger #1. Fire trigger #2 while DAG #1 running. Verify: DAG #1 aborted (per ADR-1.1.11 Q5) -> saga compensation for in-flight steps -> DAG #2 starts fresh. `test_storage.assert_run_count(workflow_id) == 2` (both recorded). Rapid fire (3 triggers in 5s): only trigger #3 executes, triggers #1 and #2 aborted.
>   - **MCP disconnect** (9.2.6): `mock_fabric.script_result_for_step("mcp_tool_step", RAISE(ConnectionError("MCP server disconnected")))`. StepRunner -> ErrorRouter classifies TRANSIENT -> retry (2 attempts) -> both fail -> CB_MCP trips -> step FAILED. Non-MCP steps in same wave: unaffected (StepRunner per-step isolation). `mock_connector.assert_rediscovery_scheduled()` after CB trip.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Chaos tests configure adapter failures BEFORE `orch.process()` call (not during). Use `script_results()` / `raise_on_call()` methods on test adapters. CB state transitions are simulated by adapter error types -- do NOT manually set CB state. WAL injection for crash recovery MUST happen before `orch.init()` (crash_recovery runs inside init). InterruptRequest bypasses ConcurrencyGuard by design -- do NOT add ConcurrencyGuard check for interrupts.

### Epic 9.3: Documentation

**Goal**: Complete all Orchestrator documentation for operators, integrators, and workflow authors.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |

| 9.3.1 | Orchestrator integration guide | TODO | k1/orchestrator/docs/orchestrator_integration_guide.md | How Concierge, Planner, and Fabric connect to Orchestrator. **Sections**: (1) Architecture overview: hexagonal 8-port design, actor model, single-DAG concurrency, (2) Message flow diagrams: MEDIUM tier (TaskEnvelope -> Fabric -> AggregatedResult), HIGH tier (TaskEnvelope -> Planner -> CommittedPlan -> DAG -> AggregatedResult), Workflow (WorkflowRunRequest -> compile -> DAG -> results), (3) Port usage: which callers connect to which ports (Concierge -> IMailboxPort, Planner -> IEventSubscriptionPort, Fabric -> IFabricPort), (4) Envelope schemas: TaskEnvelope, CommittedPlan, AggregatedResult, WorkflowRunRequest, InterruptRequest -- with field descriptions and examples, (5) Event wiring: 20 emitted events (who consumes them), 12 consumed events (who produces them), (6) Error handling: ErrorRouter classification -> caller responsibilities, (7) Tier degradation: CB state -> tier mapping -> caller fallback behavior. |
| 9.3.2 | Workflow authoring guide | TODO | k1/orchestrator/docs/workflow_guide.md | How to create, trigger, and manage workflows. **Sections**: (1) WorkflowSpec format: YAML structure with fields (name, description, trigger, steps, capabilities, cross_workflow_refs), (2) Trigger types: cron syntax, event triggers (topic + filter), manual trigger, (3) Step definition: capability_name, params, dependencies, optional flag, side_effect flag, output_schema, (4) Cross-workflow patterns: parent triggers child via WorkflowRunRequest, depth limit (max 3), result propagation, (5) Proactive gap handling: what happens when a capability is removed/updated -- ProactiveGap event, re-compile behavior, notification flow, (6) Concurrent run policy: same workflow re-triggered -> abort previous (per ADR-1.1.11 Q5), (7) Examples: daily health check workflow, weekly report workflow, event-driven notification workflow. |
| 9.3.3 | Orchestrator operational runbook | TODO | k1/orchestrator/docs/orchestrator_runbook.md | Production operations guide linked from alert rules (8.4.1). **10 procedures**: (1) Drain mailbox for maintenance: send shutdown() -> wait for mailbox empty -> all pending messages processed or logged, (2) Debug stuck DAG: inspect orchestrator.dag.active gauge + wave progress logs -> identify blocked step -> check Fabric/Planner health, (3) Force-cancel in-flight DAG: send InterruptRequest(cancel) via mailbox -> verify saga compensation completes -> DAG terminated, (4) Restart MCP discovery: trigger ConnectorLifecycleManager.refresh(server_id) -> verify tools re-registered in Fabric -> check Fabric HealthChecker/CB state, (5) Inspect scheduler trigger state: query WorkflowStorage.get_trigger_state(workflow_id) -> verify next_fire/last_fire -> manually adjust if drift detected, (6) Handle saga dead-letter: inspect dead-letter log (bridge audit trail) -> manually compensate if needed -> clear dead-letter entry, (7) CB state management: force CB open (isolate failing provider), force CB closed (override after fix), force half-open (test recovery) -> via admin API or config change, (8) Trigger manual workflow run: submit WorkflowRunRequest via mailbox with manual trigger type -> verify execution, (9) Read audit trail: query bridge_port.read_audit(trace_id) -> reconstruct full execution path from WAL + events, (10) Handle token budget exhaustion: inspect which DAG exceeded budget -> check step cost breakdown -> adjust budget in TaskEnvelope or workflow spec. **Schema Whiteboard**: WB §5.2 provides the full admin API endpoint table (18 endpoints) -- include endpoint reference in each runbook procedure where admin API can be used for the operation (e.g., /admin/dag/cancel for procedure 3, /admin/cb/{name}/state for procedure 7, /admin/scheduler/triggers for procedure 5). |
| 9.3.4 | Connector administration guide | TODO | k1/orchestrator/docs/connector_guide.md | How to manage MCP connectors. **Scope clarification**: Orchestrator owns discovery and registration into Fabric. Fabric owns provider execution, health monitoring, circuit breakers, timeout enforcement, and output validation. This guide covers Orchestrator's scope only. **Sections**: (1) Adding MCP server: add entry to mcp_servers.yaml (id, type, endpoint, critical) -> restart or trigger ConnectorLifecycleManager.refresh() to re-discover and register into Fabric, (2) Removing MCP server: remove entry -> tools unregistered on next refresh cycle -> affected workflows get ProactiveGap, (3) K0 proxy settings: k0_proxy_endpoint config, fallback behavior when K0 offline, IFL routing rules, (4) Troubleshooting: MCP server unreachable -> Fabric HealthChecker marks OFFLINE -> Fabric CircuitBreaker opens -> Orchestrator's lifecycle monitoring detects recovery via k1.fabric.provider.health.changed.v1 -> triggers refresh(), (5) Health monitoring: view mcp.connected_servers gauge (Orchestrator), check per-server health/CB state via Fabric admin API (Fabric owns health data), (6) Security: MCP tool capability scoping, audit trail for MCP operations. **Removed from Orchestrator scope**: sandbox configuration (Fabric CircuitBreaker + OutputValidation handles timeouts and validation), CPU/memory limits (Fabric providers handle isolation), active health probing (Fabric HealthChecker does periodic checks). |

> **Wiring**:

> - **File locations**: `k1/orchestrator/docs/orchestrator_integration_guide.md` (9.3.1), `k1/orchestrator/docs/workflow_guide.md` (9.3.2), `k1/orchestrator/docs/orchestrator_runbook.md` (9.3.3), `k1/orchestrator/docs/connector_guide.md` (9.3.4).
> - **Integration guide** (9.3.1) documents the full wiring architecture. Must reference:
>   - 8 ports: table from Epic 6.1 wiring (port -> prod adapter -> test adapter -> factory step).
>   - OrchestratorFactory construction order: 15-step breakdown from Epic 6.2 wiring.
>   - Message flow per tier: MEDIUM (TaskEnvelope -> `dispatch_medium` -> CapabilityRequest -> AggregatedResult), HIGH (TaskEnvelope -> `dispatch_high` -> PlanRequest -> event wait -> CommittedPlan -> DAG -> AggregatedResult), Workflow (WorkflowRunRequest -> compile -> schedule -> execute -> DAG -> results).
>   - Port-caller mapping: Concierge -> IMailboxPort, Planner -> IEventSubscriptionPort (plan.ready events), Fabric -> IFabricGatewayPort, SessionState -> IStateReadPort, K0 Bridge -> IBridgeWritePort + IEventSubscriptionPort, WorkflowStorage -> IWorkflowStoragePort.
>   - Event wiring: 20 emitted events (`k1/orchestrator/events.py` constants), 12 consumed events (topic + producer).
> - **Workflow guide** (9.3.2) documents WorkflowEngine pipeline (4.1-4.2):

>   - Step -> capability mapping: step `capability_name` resolves to registered Fabric capability.
>   - Cross-workflow: `WorkflowRunRequest` schema, depth limit enforcement in `WorkflowExecutor`.
> - **Runbook** (9.3.3) is the operational reference linked from alert rules (8.4.1). Must map:
>   - Each of 12 alerts (8.4.1) -> specific runbook procedure (1-10).
>   - Each procedure references: metric to inspect (8.2.1), log query (8.3.1 structured fields), admin action.
> - **Connector guide** (9.3.4) documents ConnectorLifecycleManager (5.1.1-5.1.4):

All code references in docs must match real file paths and class names. Event names in docs must exactly match `events.py` constants. Port names must exactly match Protocol class names in `ports.py`. Wiring diagrams in docs are Mermaid format rendering the ACTUAL factory construction order.

### Epic 9.4: Final Validation

**Goal**: Run comprehensive validation and sign off on production readiness.

<!-- markdownlint-enable MD060 -->

**9.4.1 checklist scope** (what the checklist must verify):

- **Architecture validation**: all 12 ADRs accepted (including ADR-1.1.12 event-driven model), all 8 ports wired (7 original + IWorkflowStoragePort + IAdminPort), all 19 adapters tested (8 prod + 8 test + 2 workflow storage + 1 admin HTTP), 42+ supporting types defined (including ProcessingContext per WB §1, OrchestratorConfig per WB §2, CircuitBreakerConfig/State per WB §7, guard types per WB §8, admin types per WB §5/§6), PlanStep 14-field definition complete, cross-component contract published.
- **Schema Whiteboard validation**: all 10 WB sections have corresponding plan issues -- ProcessingContext (1.2.21), OrchestratorConfig (1.2.22), 32 event payload JSON Schemas (1.5.1-1.5.2), contract YAML templates (1.3.1-1.3.2 with WB §4.1/§4.2 refs), 18 admin API endpoints (6.3.1-6.3.3), health check probes (6.2.2 with WB §6 ref), 4 circuit breakers (1.2.23 with WB §7 ref), guard pipeline G0-G10 (1.2.24 with WB §8 ref, 3.2.1 guard protocol), mailbox durability (6.1.1 with WB §9 ref), Planner-Orchestrator protocol (1.5.3 + 1.2.26-1.2.27 with WB §10 refs).
- **Invariant validation**: all 16 ORCH invariants verified (ORCH-01 through ORCH-16) per 7.4.3 test results.
- **Event validation**: all 20 emitted events verified with JSON Schema validation (1.5.1), all 12 consumed events verified with JSON Schema validation (1.5.2), trace_id propagation on all events (ORCH-09), request_id echo contract verified (7.4.5).
- **Feature validation**: concurrency guard (single-DAG lock), error router (7 classifications per WB §7), saga compensation (CompensationRecord + lookup chain), workflow persistence (SQLite), MCP re-discovery, cost budget guard, sub-step rate limiter, meta-agent DAG pattern, tier degradation cascade, event-driven dispatch_high (PendingPlanContext), HIL async pattern (PendingHILContext with timeouts), safety band re-read at wave boundaries, MEDIUM tier capability validation, WAL interaction protocol (per-wave markers), no-session workflow results, admin API (18 endpoints per WB §5), health/ready probes (per WB §6), RACE-2 orphan plan handling (7.3A.8).
- **SLI/SLO validation**: all 13 latency baselines measured (8.1.1), total overhead < 18ms P99, MEDIUM e2e < 10s, HIGH e2e < 45s.
- **Test validation**: ~530+ total tests passing (M7 unit + integration + contract + cross-subsystem + lifecycle + performance), all load tests passing (9.1.x), all chaos tests passing (9.2.x).
- **Documentation validation**: 4 docs complete (integration guide, workflow guide, runbook, connector guide).
- **Security validation**: ORCH-01 (no writes), safety band enforcement, tool scoping, capability access control, audit trails via K0 Bridge.
- **Sign-off criteria**: zero critical issues, zero failing tests, all SLIs within target, all docs reviewed.

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/orchestrator/docs/production_checklist.md` (9.4.1).
> - **Checklist -> Source mapping** (each checklist item references its validation source):
>   - Architecture validation: ADR count -> `docs/architecture/decisions-K0/` directory listing. Port count -> `k1/orchestrator/ports.py` Protocol count. Adapter count -> `k1/orchestrator/adapters/` file count. Type count -> `k1/orchestrator/types.py` class count.
>   - Invariant validation: ORCH-01..ORCH-16 -> `tests/k1/orchestrator/test_invariants.py` (7.4.3) test results. Each invariant has named test: `test_orch_01_no_writes`, `test_orch_02_single_dag`, etc.
>   - Event validation: 20 emitted -> `k1/orchestrator/events.py` constant count. 12 consumed -> event subscription in `OrchestratorService.init()`. trace_id propagation -> 8.3.1 tracing tests.
>   - Feature validation: Each feature links to its M2-M5 implementation issue AND its M7 test issue.
>   - SLI/SLO validation: 13 baselines -> 8.1.1 SLI doc. Measured values -> 7.7.x benchmark results + 8.1.2 metrics output.
